# Lamplight: Architecture

Version 1, Sep 27 2026. Written in Phase 0 (Opus 5.5, max). Brought up to date with the code on Nov 20 2026 (0.9.0-wip; §22): every section names the files and calls that do the thing today. Where this document and the code disagree, the code is how the game stands, and the difference is a bug in this document.

This is the "how" document. `BRIEF.md` says what the game is and why. `HANDOFF.md` says where the work currently stands. Every session reads all three completely before touching anything.

When documents disagree: the brief wins on what the game is, this document wins on how it is built, and a real conflict between them goes to Kevin as a question. Never resolve it silently.

---

## 1. The constraints that shape every decision

| Constraint | What it forces |
|---|---|
| The product is a Windows PC game played with a controller | Electron desktop build, Gamepad API first, a UI that works without a mouse, no touch or phone considerations anywhere |
| Kevin has no terminal and deploys through GitHub and Cloudflare web dashboards | All building, testing, and packaging happens in Claude's workspace. The repo must build on Cloudflare with `npm run build && npx wrangler deploy` and nothing else |
| No GitHub Actions, ever | The .exe is packaged in Claude's workspace and sent as a download |
| Tests gate every deploy | Tests are fast (the whole gate under about 60 s), deterministic, and never measure performance. Every zip carries every test file that `package.json` names |
| GitHub web upload limits | At most 100 files per zip and 25 MB per file. The repo is designed to stay compact: content lives in a few large data files, not one file per weapon |
| Claude cannot play the game | Every feel value lives in data and can be changed live from an in-game tuning panel. Every run can be replayed exactly from a small file. Claude verifies visuals through screenshots, and verifies behavior through a headless simulation and bots |
| Kevin is away for a stretch | The phase plan front-loads everything that doesn't need a playtest. Feel sign-off is a gate that waits for him |

---

## 2. Stack

Versions checked against the npm registry on Sep 27 2026. Pin the major version and let minor versions float.

| Piece | Choice | Version | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| Language | Plain JavaScript, ES modules, JSDoc types where useful | Node 22 | No compile step, so the simulation runs directly in Node for tests and bots. TypeScript would add a toolchain and gain little for a single builder |
| Bundler | Vite | 8.x | Fast, simple, and already used on Kevin's other projects |
| Renderer | PixiJS (WebGL) | 8.x | v8's ParticleContainer draws about 1,000,000 particles at 60 fps on the PixiJS team's benchmark. Rendering will not be the bottleneck; simulation is |
| Desktop shell | Electron | 44.x | Chromium-based like the Edge or Chrome that runs the playtest build, so the two behave nearly the same. Where they differ, the .exe is the reference |
| Packager | @electron/packager | 20.x | Tested Sep 27 2026: produces a working Windows folder with `Lamplight.exe` from this Linux workspace, with no Wine. About 368 MB unzipped and 151 MB zipped |
| Sound effects | ZzFX | 1.3.x | A tiny procedural synth. Sounds are parameter arrays in data |
| Save export compression | fflate | 0.8.x | Small and fast deflate |
| Deploy | wrangler | 4.x | Kevin's existing Cloudflare pattern |
| Tests | Node's built-in `node:test` | built in | Zero dependencies, so there is nothing extra for Cloudflare to install |
| Workspace-only tools | pngjs, maxrects-packer, playwright-core (with the preinstalled Chromium) | current | For the atlas builder, screenshots, and bots. They live in `tools/package.json`, which Cloudflare never installs |

Rejected: Phaser (it would fight the sim/render split), a heavyweight engine (the brief forbids it), and Godot (Claude can't run or verify it in the workspace).

---

## 3. Repo layout

The repo is named `lamplight`, and the playtest site will be `lamplight.strickland-kevinj.workers.dev`.

The balance tools the characters-and-weapons rebuild used most are NOT in the repo: C1's `runc1.mjs`, `pair.mjs`, `top.mjs`, `held.mjs`, `bench.mjs`, `formfixture.mjs` (in `/mnt/user-data/outputs/work/cw/C1/`) and C2's `sigsets.mjs` and `sigfixture.mjs` (in `/mnt/user-data/outputs/work/cw/C2/tools/`). HANDOFF.md says how to run them. Moving them into `tools/` is open.

Rules:
- `.exe` files, raw art packs, and `node_modules` are never committed.
- The generated atlas is committed (src/assets/), so Cloudflare never runs the art pipeline.
- Content stays in one file per category until a file passes about 2,000 lines. Then it splits (the Harbor Orders are three files by kind: `orders-characters.js`, `orders-content.js`, `orders-weapons.js`, beside `feats.js`), still in few files.

## 4. How the pieces fit

```
 controller/keyboard ──► input ──► InputFrame (one per tick, recorded)
                                        │
 meta: profile ──► RunConfig ──►  ┌─────▼────────────────────────┐
                                  │  sim  (pure, fixed 60 Hz)    │
                                  │  typed-array world state     │──► event buffer ──► render / audio / HUD
                                  └─────┬────────────────────────┘
                                        │ on run end
                                        ▼
                                    RunResult ──► meta.applyRunResult(profile) ──► new profile ──► save
                                                                                        │
 the harbor: hub world (pure: walk, doors, set pieces) ◄── derived state (fog, people, props, stages)
             a purchase ──► meta actions (restore, upgrade, research) ──► new profile ──► save ──► ceremony
```

### Import boundaries

These are enforced by `tests/boundary.test.js`. It reads each file with a small scanner (`tests/helpers/jsscan.js`) that separates code from comments, strings, template text, and regex literals, so a comment that mentions `window` or an import in a doc example can't fail the gate, and real code can't hide in a template's `${...}`. The layer table in the test is the source of truth; this table mirrors it.

| Layer | May import | Must never touch |
|---|---|---|
| `data/` | other `data/` files | anything else |
| `lib/` | other `lib/` files | DOM, Pixi, clocks |
| `sim/` | `data/`, `lib/` | Pixi, DOM, `render/`, `ui/`, `audio/`, `input/`, `save/`, `Math.random`, `Date`, `performance.now`, timers, and the engine math functions whose results can vary between JavaScript engines (`Math.sin`, `cos`, `tan`, `atan2`, `exp`, `pow`, `log`, `hypot`, and relatives, and the `**` operator, which is `pow` by another name); use `lib/fmath.js` instead |
| `meta/` | `data/`, `lib/` | the same list as `sim/` |
| `hub/` | `sim/` (its tile collision), `data/`, `lib/` | the same list as `sim/` |
| `input/` | `lib/` | everything else |
| `save/` | `lib/`, `data/`, fflate | everything else |
| `render/` | reads `sim/` and `hub/` state and `meta/`'s derived state; `data/`, `lib/`, Pixi | `ui/`, `app/`, `save/`, `input/`, `audio/`; writing sim, hub, or profile state |
| `audio/` | `data/`, `lib/` | everything else |
| `ui/` | `input/` (for glyphs), `meta/` actions, `data/`, `lib/` | `sim/`, `render/`, `save/`, `audio/` |
| `app/`, `main.js` | everything | nothing |

Why this matters: because `sim/`, `meta/`, and `hub/` are pure, the whole game can run in Node with no screen. That powers the deploy tests, the balance bots, the progression-pacing simulator, exact replays, and bug reports Claude can reproduce without playing.

---

## 5. The simulation (`src/sim/`)

### 5.1 Loop and time
- The sim runs at a fixed 60 ticks per second (`dt = 1/60`). Rendering runs at the monitor's refresh rate and interpolates positions between the previous and current tick, so 144 Hz monitors look smooth.
- The loop driver (in `app/`) uses an accumulator and runs at most 5 sim ticks per frame, so a slow frame can't snowball.
- **Hit-stop** lives in the loop driver, not in the sim. It holds the sim for a few frames while rendering continues. The sequence of ticks is unchanged, so replays stay exact. Hit-stop fires only on big events (boss hits, elite kills, the player getting hurt, level-ups), never on ordinary hits. In a horde game, per-hit hit-stop would freeze the screen constantly. Since 0.9.0 the sim asks for it (`EV.HIT_STOP`: a hit on you, a boss's or champion's attack that hurt you, an elite or boss kill), only on real hits: a slam that misses and a leech's drain ask for nothing; the loop driver reads only those requests.
- The level-up draft, chest reels, and pause all stop the sim entirely.

### 5.2 Determinism
- All randomness goes through seeded PRNG streams (`lib/rng.js`, sfc32), forked by purpose: `spawn`, `combat`, `draft`, `loot`, `boss`, `storm` (the Marsh's storm), and `trade` (the signature's track and the characters' trades) in `sim/world.js`, and `acts` (the act's site draws) in `sim/acts.js`. Separate streams mean a change to the draft code doesn't reshuffle enemy spawns, which keeps balance comparisons stable.
- Same build + same seed + same input frames = the same run, bit for bit. `tests/determinism.test.js` checks this with a state hash at 60 seconds.
- **Cross-engine determinism.** Replays are recorded in Electron (or the player's browser) and replayed by Claude in Node. Those run different V8 versions, and functions like `Math.sin` and `Math.pow` are not guaranteed to return identical bits across versions. So `sim/` and `meta/` use `lib/fmath.js`: polynomial sin, cos, atan, atan2, exp, log, and pow after exact range reduction (no lookup tables), built only from `+ - * /`, `Math.sqrt` (which IEEE 754 requires to be exactly rounded), and exact bit operations. Verified in Phase 1: the outputs hash identically under Node 22 (V8 12.4), Electron 44 (V8 15.2), and Bun (JavaScriptCore). The boundary test forbids the engine versions. `tests/determinism.test.js` also checks a stored hash from a fixed replay, so any drift fails the gate.
- Replays record the build ID. A replay from a different build is refused with a plain message instead of silently desyncing.

### 5.3 Entity storage
There are hundreds or thousands of enemies, projectiles, gems, and particles, so they live in **structure-of-arrays pools**: one typed array per field, one index per entity. There are no per-entity objects, and numbers stored into typed arrays are never boxed, so the hot loops create no garbage of their own. The soak test holds the whole sim to the budget in §17.

| Pool | Capacity | Fields (examples) |
|---|---|---|
| Enemies | 4,096 | x, y, prevX, prevY, vx, vy, hp, maxHp, type, flags (elite, boss, telegraphing), knockback x/y, flash timer, status timers (burn, slow, freeze, rot), anim phase |
| Projectiles | 4,096 | x, y, vx, vy, life, weapon slot, damage, pierce left, radius, motion type, motion params |
| Gems | 4,096 | x, y, value, state (idle or magnet). Above 1,500 on the field, new gems merge into one large gem |
| Zones | 256 | auras, burning ground, tide pools (few, so small structs are fine) |
| Pickups | 256 | chests, materials, healing, the magnet item |

Visual particles (sparks, embers) are not sim state. They live in a render-side pool (§6.2).

- The pickup pool keeps its last 32 slots for heals, magnets, and chests. Materials past that line go straight into the run's haul, a chest that finds the pool full opens where it fell, and a heal or magnet that finds it full replaces the one lying farthest from the player. Nothing the player earned is lost to a full pool.

- Removal is swap-with-last, so arrays stay dense.
- Anything that holds a reference to another entity (homing targets, chain hops) uses a **handle** (index plus a generation counter), which becomes invalid automatically when the entity dies.
- Multi-hit control: each enemy stores, per weapon slot, the tick it was last hit by that slot. A weapon can't hit the same enemy again until its hit cooldown passes. This is the standard survivors-genre approach, and it stops piercing projectiles and auras from shredding everything in one frame.

### 5.4 Spatial hash
- A uniform grid with 16 px cells over a 128×128-cell window (2,048 px square) that follows the player, rebuilt every tick into flat typed arrays: a linked list per cell (a head per cell, a next per item), and only the cells the last build used are reset, so a build costs O(items), not O(cells). There's no allocation. Items outside the window clamp into its edge cells, which keeps queries exact (every candidate is distance-checked) and only costs speed for rare stragglers. Elites and bosses (radius over 8 px) skip the grid, and every query checks them directly.
- (Phase 0 planned a counting sort. Phase 2 measured the full-grid pass it needs as the larger cost at these counts and switched to the linked list.)
- It's used for projectile-vs-enemy hits, enemy separation, contact damage, zone damage, and the gem magnet.
- **Measured in the Phase 0 prototype** on this 2-CPU container (enemy seek plus separation plus projectile hits, all densely packed): 1,000 enemies with 300 projectiles took 0.46 ms per tick, 2,000/500 took 1.1 ms, and 3,000/800 took 2.0 ms. The budget is 16.7 ms per frame. The brief's 1,000-enemy target has plenty of headroom, and 2,000+ is realistic.

### 5.5 Tick order
This order is fixed, and it's part of determinism:

1. Read this tick's InputFrame
2. Player movement and dash
3. Weapons fire (cooldowns tick, emitters create projectiles and zones)
4. Projectiles move
5. Projectile hits (spatial hash queries, on-hit effects)
6. Zones tick
7. Enemies act (AI, flow field, separation, knockback decay, status effects)
8. Contact damage to the player (i-frames after a hit)
9. Deaths resolve (drops, relic and character hooks, kill counts)
10. Gems and pickups (magnet, collection, XP); then the run's tasks (lamps and their wards, bells and their crews, Lantern Night), the Ferryman, and the Salt Well (§5.18), and the deaths those caused
11. Level-up check (queues drafts; the loop driver opens the level-up screen)
12. Director spawns (timeline, density target, scripted events)
13. Events flush to the event buffer

In code (`stepWorld` in `sim/world.js`), a few bookkeeping steps sit between those: weapon and player stats are rebuilt first if the build changed; after step 2 the flow field updates, the enemy grid is built, and `onTick` hooks run; the grid is rebuilt after step 7 (enemies moved) for contact; dead enemies are removed after step 12. When a step queues a decision (a draft, a chest, a Form choice, a relic pick, a Fog Bell's pick, the Salt Well, the overtime choice), the sim halts after the tick and the next halt gets its cards.

### 5.6 Enemies and movement
- **AI types** (data picks one per enemy): `chase` (walks at the player), `swarm` (spawns as a wall or ring and crosses the screen in a straight line), `charger` (telegraphs, then dashes), `hazard` (drops a telegraphed zone), `splitter` (splits on death), `ranged_slow` (rare, slow, readable shots only; this is not a bullet hell).
- **Separation:** a soft push from neighbors, taken from the spatial hash, so crowds read as a mass and not as a single stacked blob. Half the crowd separates each tick (alternating), at twice the strength, which gives the same spread for fewer grid queries.
- **Obstacles:** maps with obstacles use a **flow field**: a breadth-first search over the tile grid from the player's tile, recomputed when the player changes tile, and limited to 48 tiles (768 px) around the player, well past the 400 px spawn ring. Enemies read their direction from their tile and follow it where it disagrees with the straight line to the player by more than 45°, and wherever the straight step would run into a wall (a probe just past the body, so big enemies see walls too); elsewhere they walk the straight line, so movement in the open stays smooth. Chasers, chargers walking in or recovering, and bosses between attacks all path this way. Bodies wider than a tile (elites, bosses) read a second field whose walls are grown by one tile, so it leads them down corridors they fit through instead of along a wall's edge into its corner. Open areas use a direct chase.
- **Teleport-ahead:** an enemy that ends up far behind the player (more than 1.5 screen widths away) moves to the spawn ring ahead of the player. The horde stays around you instead of trailing behind.
- **Knockback:** a velocity impulse divided by the enemy's mass, decaying over a few ticks. It's tunable per weapon and globally.
- **The pace dials (0.8.1, `tuning.pace`; Kevin, Nov 7: "fewer slower but stronger enemies than the madness we have going on now").** Seven numbers on the tuning panel, under Pace, change the whole fight; every one at 1 (and `maxAlive` at 3,000) is the 0.8.0 fight (measured: the same state hash after 11 minutes on both maps, for both MVP characters, before the kill-counted content below was set again; `tools/bots/pace.mjs --neutral`). `speed` (0.8) multiplies every enemy's walk each tick (`enemiesAct`, so a turn of the dial reaches the enemies already out), a boss's walk, a charger's dash (whose dash runs `dashTime / speed`, so it covers exactly the line it warned of), and an eel's drift and lunge (whose reach is worked out from its data, so it lunges as far, more slowly); no warning gets shorter or longer, a toad's hop keeps its clock, and a boss's warned attacks (a charge line, a leap) keep their own speeds. `hp` (2.5) and `xp` (3.5) multiply an ordinary enemy's health and gem when it spawns (`spawnEnemy`; a boss's crew and a bloater's rats are ordinary); elites keep `eliteHp` and `eliteXp`, and bosses their level and `minFight` (a 15 s safety floor since 0.9.0, beside the phase gates: §5.13). `contact` (1.3) multiplies an enemy's touch when it lands (`contactStep`) and a toad's landing, never a boss's, a shot's, or a pool's. `drops` (3.5) multiplies an ordinary enemy's chance of each material, food, and the magnet (`rollDrops`); an elite's and a boss's tables are their own. `density` and `maxAlive` are the director's (§5.7). What counts kills or crowds was set again in the data for these dials (a run meets about 0.28 the enemies it did; §22, 0.8.1), not read from them: turning `density` back up makes those counts that much easier, and the Wreck Lantern's oil never takes the last quarter of the zone pool, so a crowded pace can't fill it.
- **Combat and enemies (0.9.0, W3; the veteran round's V1 and V2; the contract the view and screens build on is `work/veteran/W3-CONTRACT.md`):**
  - *Early pressure.* The first five minutes had no threat (the kite bots' first hit came at 6 to 10 minutes). Each band carries its own `pace` (the early bands 2.2, 1.85, 1.5, 1.2 on both maps, times `pace.density`), the floors from 0:00 to 7:00 are 15, 26, 36, 40, and one fast kind on each map keeps its full speed under `pace.speed` (data `quick`: the Bilge Rat, which now comes from 0:00 on the Docks, and the Midge Cloud). The commonest kind on each map has a light threat of its own: a **pounce** (`ai.pounce`: within `range` it crouches, `EF.WARNING`, a warned line toward where you'll be if you keep going; then it leaps along it at its own speed, `EF.LUNGING`, biting what it comes within `bite` px of; once; neither state can be held: `midAttack`), on rats and midges, and the midge's **sting** (each bite that hurts you slows you 10% more, up to 30%, for 3 s after the last: `tuning.player.slow`, `world.player.slowPct`).
  - *Warning tiers.* Every warning goes out through `tell` (`sim/attacks.js`), which follows its `TELEGRAPH` with a `TELE_TIER`: MAJOR (bosses, champions, map strikes: the full shape), FULL (an ordinary enemy's or elite's warning that crosses or holds your spot when it starts, while fewer than `tuning.enemies.tellFullMax`, 4, are showing), DIM (the rest: the view draws a short tell, a 20 px arrow and a flash on the body). The tier is presentation; the attack is the same.
  - *What hurt you.* `hurtPlayer(world, amount, x, y, kind, type, attack)`: `PLAYER_HURT` and `SHIELD_HIT` carry c = `HURT` kind (contact, shot, boss, strike, drain, hazard, attack) and d = the enemy type. A shot's or pool's maker is kept by its pool slot (`setSource` before the spawn), and a hit from the weapons code is matched to the shot or zone standing there (`hurtSource`), so the weapons code knows nothing of enemies. `world.lastHurt` and the 8-hit ring `world.hurtLog` (`hurtLogList`) name the attack (`'tide line'`) for the death recap.
  - *Elite affixes* (`sim/affixes.js`, `data/enemies.js ELITE_AFFIXES`): one an elite, two from Tide 2 (`tuning.affixes`), drawn from the spawn stream; none on a muster's captain. Warded (a ward takes 3 hits, one each 0.15 s at most, before damage lands), Herald (6 of its kind with it), Molten (a warned patch of fire every 1.2 s where it walks: a hazard pool, `HAZARD_LOOK.FIRE`), Tethered (2 of its kind linked; while they live a hit on it is shared in thirds), Brine (its touch slows you 30% for 2 s). Pool fields `affix`, `ward`, `wardT`, `link0`/`link1` (handle + 1), `affixT`. A ranged elite stands its ground (one that backed away for ever was a chase, not a fight).
  - *Measured* (W3's probe: the mortal kite bot, 4 runs a cell, a fresh harbor at Tide 0 and three quarters of one at Tides 3, 7, and 10, against 0.8.2 on the same seeds): the first hit at Tide 0 came at 30 to 256 s on the Docks and 7 to 391 s on the Marsh (it was 358 to 611 and 332 to 471), damage in the first five minutes 19 and 4 (it was 0); full-strength ordinary warnings on screen fell from 189 to 110 a minute on the Docks at Tide 0, never more than 4 at once, with about 250 short tells a minute beside them; the bosses fought 25 to 100 s with nothing held back by the floor in most fights (the Old Grey and the Salt Widow had been held to 32 and 50 s, with 60 to 97% of hits capped).
  - *Threat that follows power* (`sim/threat.js`): once a second, your damage over the last 30 s against `tuning.threat.ref` (the kite bots' damage a second at each minute on 0.8.2, Tide 0), raised to 0.4 and held from 1 to 1.4, multiplies enemy touches (not a boss's) and a pounce's bite, and each new elite's health, from 4:00 (the opening is the bands' and the Tide's, so a harbor's upgrades aren't taken back at 0:00). A weak build is never eased.

### 5.7 Spawn director
- Each map has a **timeline** in data: bands of time, each with a roster of enemy weights and a density curve (target number of enemies alive), plus scripted events at fixed times (swarm walls, rings, elites, chests, the 10:00 boss, the 20:00 boss).
- The director keeps the enemy count near the density target (the bands' numbers times the pace dials, §5.6 and below). It spawns on a ring just outside the view and never spawns on screen. The logical view is 640×360, whose half-diagonal is about 367 px, so the ring sits at about 400 px (the half-diagonal plus a 32 px margin).
- The density target climbs until the late game passes 1,000 enemies alive at the bands' own numbers. Since 0.8.1 the pace dial `tuning.pace.density` (0.35) multiplies every band, so the default peak sits near 400 at 18:00 to 20:00, and `tuning.pace.maxAlive` (450) is the alive cap: the director spawns no band filler while that many enemies are alive (a map's scripted crowds still come in full; they are its content). Each band has a `min` (15 in the first minute since 0.9.0, rising to 120 by 18:00), held whatever the dials say, so the screen never empties; only a boss's thinning (`bossDensityMul`) and a run's `densityCap` go under it. A band's own `pace` multiplies `pace.density` for that band (0.9.0: per-band density), and a Tide's `densityMul` (and the Swarm curse) raises the floor by half its step (`director.floorTide`) as well as the target, so a Tide adds horde danger from 0:00, not only where a band's target is above its floor. The `density` stat and Tide levels multiply the target. This is Kevin's direction overriding the brief's 1,000+ on screen (brief §2, §5.5) for the default pace: the engine still runs 1,000+ (the soak keeps checking it), and the dials give it back.
- **The three phases of a standard run** (brief §5.1) are timeline bands: 0:00 to 10:00 (build-up, ending in the first boss), 10:00 to 20:00 (pressure, ending in the harder boss), and 20:00 onward (overtime, if chosen).
- **The honest limit on "1,000+ on screen":** at 640×360, a 16 px enemy covers 256 px², so 1,000 of them cover about 1.1 times the screen's area. A thousand enemies *on screen at once* therefore means an overlapping mass, which is how this genre looks at its peak. Readability comes from separation (crowds read as a mass, not a single stack), smaller swarm enemies (8 to 12 px), and a contrast ramp that keeps the player and the hazards on top. The feel gate judges it. If late-game density turns to mush, the levers are, in order: fewer but tougher enemies (the density curve; pulled in 0.8.1 as the pace dials, at Kevin's word that the combat was too fast), smaller swarm sprites, and a "wide view" setting at 960×540 (integer ×2 at 1080p and ×4 at 4K, but letterboxed at 1440p).
- Scripted event types (every count, a muster's crew, and overtime's repeats times `tuning.pace.events`, 1 since 0.9.0, ruling 7: set pieces stay full size; at least one: `crowdCount`; a set piece's walkers carry `EF.EVENT`, may pass `maxAlive` while they run, and don't count against the band's target or its cap): `wall` and `ring` (swarmers fly straight through; anything else walks at you from where the line or ring stood), `elite`, `muster` (an elite captain past the spawn ring by the crew's radius and a ring of its crew around him, so none appear on screen, walking in together; the captain pays a boss chest: the elite event, brief §7, at 15:00 on the Drowned Docks as the Bosun's Muster; since 0.9.0 a muster with `champion: { name, chest, attack }` makes its captain a **champion**, below), `boss`, and `chest` (a one-item chest on open ground a short walk away). Elites and bosses always find a spot on the ring, even with the player in a map corner. A map's timeline and overtime data are checked when the run starts.
- **Overtime:** after the 20:00 boss, the player picks Leave or Stay. Staying escalates density, enemy HP, and material yield every minute, per the map's overtime data, until the player dies or leaves from the pause menu.
- **The ground** (`sim/terrain.js`): a map's `layout` builds its tiles once, from its own seed. `open`, `rects`, and `scatter` (crate clusters) are for tests and simple maps; `docks` builds the Drowned Docks: a wharf with flooded slips in it (basins 4 tiles wide and 10 to 22 long, open wharf all round each, a footbridge across the long ones), sheds, crates kept off every edge, and a wreck beached in the far northeast. Any open pocket a walker couldn't reach from the start is filled in, so every open tile is reachable. The sim keeps only solid or open; `terrain.kinds` says what each tile is (boards, crate, water, roof, wall, hull, bridge, deck) for the run view, which picks each frame by its kind and neighbors (`render/viewstate.js groundFrame`).
- **Secrets:** a map's `secrets` are caches at set tiles. Each opens once a run when you reach it: its relic while you can take it (not owned or banished), otherwise one chest item, shown as a chest reel titled Secret cache. It's recorded in the run result (`secretsFound`), and meta adds `secret:<id>` to the codex, which unlocks what is keyed `unlock: 'secret:<id>'` (the Wreck Lantern) from the next run on. Nothing points the way: the cache glows faintly violet through the dark once you're near.
- **Short run mode:** a data flag compresses the timeline to 10:00 (bosses at 5:00 and 10:00) and scales rewards down.

### 5.8 Weapons: the pipeline
This is the most important design in the sim, because the brief requires runes and relics to change *behavior*, not numbers. (Runes are gone since ruling 14: their effects are Rare and Epic lines and Form options, which patch this same composition.)

A weapon is a composition of three stages, all named in data:

| Stage | Types |
|---|---|
| **Emitter**: where and how it fires | `nearest` (auto-targets the nearest enemy), `facing` (movement direction), `radial` (N directions), `orbit` (bodies circling the player), `aura` (a zone on the player), `strike` (hits a random enemy on screen), `drop` (left behind at the player's position), `wave` (an expanding ring), `beam` (a line), `summon` (a minion with simple AI), `spout` (the Marsh: a spout under the thickest crowd, sampled across the whole screen, leaving one puddle each: `emit.puddle`, and `emit.shatter` for frozen kills) |
| **Motion**: how the projectile moves | `linear`, `homing`, `boomerang`, `orbit`, `stationary`, `arc` (lobbed), `bounce` (off the screen edges), `chain` (hops between targets) |
| **On-hit list**: what happens on contact | `damage`, `knockback`, `status` (burn, slow, freeze, rot), `split` (N children), `chain` (N hops), `explode` (radius), `pull` (toward the player), `zone` (leaves ground effect), `light` (emits light for a moment), and the Marsh's `daze` (can't walk, act, or hurt you), `spill` (a kill's leftover damage carries on), `tar` (set off by another weapon's hit, or burns off at its end for half), `fever` (+10% damage taken; passes burn, rot, and slow to one it bumps into), `spike` (a chain's hops leave a glass spike that beams the nearest enemy) |

- **The Marsh's additions (0.8.0, §5.17):** zones `PUDDLE` (a spout's puddle: slows; two overlapping freeze, then a 2 s thaw no freeze can break), `SPIKE` (a glass spike, counted per slot against its `max`), and `STRIKE` (a storm ring or the Widow's lightning: warned, then one blow to enemies and, separately, to you). A `drop` with `emit.moving` drops only while you move; `emit.armorDamage` adds damage per point of your Armor (the Armor stat in the slot's damage, and the armor that comes and goes in a run, the Harpooner's crowd armor `p.bonusArmor`, added at each hit). A spout keeps its picks in its own buffer (`slot.picks`), sized for the most Amount the weapon can reach, so every spout of a volley lands. A Salt Pan shard's kill never shatters again (`E.shardKill`), so only the spouts' own frozen kills shatter. Slot fields the hooks and relics read: `cracks` (Frost, Sea, and Steel crack the Salted Dead's crust), `quenches` (Sea and Frost hits on a burning enemy count for the Quencher feat), `statusMul`, `shatter`, `spikes`. The `hops` stat adds to every chain (Copper Wire). Since 0.9.0 the Marsh's weapons have Form lines too (the lead's ruling 1; §5.16).
- Nothing is ever aimed by the player. `nearest` and `strike` are automatic targeting, which the brief allows. A `facing` emitter may bend its volley onto the nearest enemy within `assist` degrees of the facing direction (the Harpoon Chain uses 40°), because a stick pointed roughly at the crowd should hit it. The whole volley keeps the direction of its first shot, so it fans out evenly even if the player turns mid-volley.
- Shots, the player's and the enemies' alike, fly over obstacles (crates and rubble are low, and weapons in this genre ignore terrain). Only bodies collide with walls.
- **Lines, specials, and Form options are patches** on this composition (`boost`: `{ stats, onHit, emit, motion, fx }`; §5.16, §5.21). An on-hit effect joins the weapon's own of that type (`sim/weapons.js addEffect`: amounts add, reaches take the larger, a chain's per-hop multiplier is never added). Only a Form's delivery option replaces an emitter's type and only a flight option a motion's type, so nothing a weapon holds cancels another (the rule that once kept runes and Forms from cancelling).
- **No evolutions (0.9.0, the lead's ruling 1):** Forms are the only way a weapon transforms (§5.16). No weapon has `evolution`; `sim/chests.js` keeps `canEvolve`, `showsReady`, `evolveOne`, and `evolvableSlot` only as stubs that say nothing evolves, and `data/ascensions.js RETIRED_WEAPONS` maps each old evolved weapon to its base weapon with its capstone.
- **Hit memory:** a shot remembers the last 8 enemies it hit, so a piercing or chaining shot never hits one twice, and pierce is capped at 8. A chain hop inherits everything the chain has hit. Each wave hits each enemy once as it passes, so every wave of a volley counts.
- **Test:** `tests/content.test.js` runs every weapon bare, and with each of its lines, specials, and Form options, for 600 ticks, checking for crashes, NaN, and a piece that never hurts anything, and that each piece changes what the weapon does.

### 5.9 Stats and tags
- Stat IDs: `might` (damage), `area`, `speed` (projectile), `duration`, `amount` (projectile count), `cooldown`, `pierce`, `knockback`, `crit`, `critMult`, `moveSpeed`, `maxHp`, `regen`, `armor`, `magnet`, `luck`, `growth` (XP), `greed` (materials), `density` (enemy count; called "Curse" in Vampire Survivors, renamed here so it can't be confused with the Chapel's Curses), `revival`, `reroll`, `banish`, `skip`, `dashCooldown`.
- A modifier looks like `{ stat, op: 'add' | 'pct' | 'more', value, tags?: [...] }`.
- **The formula, used everywhere:** `final = (base + Σadd) × (1 + Σpct) × Π(1 + more)`, then caps from `data/tuning.js` apply (for example, cooldown can't drop below 10% of base).
- **Tag scoping:** a modifier with tags applies to a weapon that shares *at least one* of those tags. Player-level stats (move speed, max HP) have no tags. Passives and relics mostly scale tags, per the brief, so stacking a tag is a build.
- Weapon stats are recalculated only when the build changes (an upgrade, a relic, a Form, a stance or a character's meter changing a step: `markStatsDirty`, `rebuildStats` in `sim/build.js`), never every tick.

### 5.10 XP and the draft
- **The XP curve** (`tuning.xp`, `sim/player.js`; ruling 14 A1): `first` 5 XP, then `perLevel` 30 a level, `latePerLevel` 40 from level 20, times 1.1 a level compounding from level 8 (`growthFrom`, `growth`); no bumps. A cleared 20:00 run ends near level 35 (C1 measured about 11 at 5:00, 21 at 10:00, 30 at 15:00). **Never two drafts inside `draftGap` (20 s):** a level earned sooner waits with its XP kept (`world.draftAt`, hashed); a dev level skips the gap. The base pickup reach is `xp.magnetRadius` 44 px (ruling 20 R20a, so the Lodestone stops deciding runs).
- **The draft** (`sim/draft.js`, on the `draft` stream; `draftCandidates`, `makeOffer`, `applyCard`):
  1. Candidates: a rolled upgrade for each drafted weapon carried (§5.21: no max level; the signature is never a candidate, §5.22), passive levels for passives not at their max (the last level's card shows the rule it adds), new weapons and passives while slots are free (`world.build.weaponSlots`, `passiveSlots`; they grow with the harbor, §5.19), and the Dash slot's cards (§5.24). New passives weigh `newPassiveMul` (0.6) and come at most one a draft. With fewer candidates than cards, a weapon shows twice with two different upgrades. Relics are never cards.
  2. Banished items, and banished upgrade types of one weapon, are removed.
  3. A weighted draw without replacement makes 3 cards (`draft.cards`; more with the Level-ups offer 4 cards gear affix or the Spyglass run tool, at most 6: `RunConfig.draftCards`). A Legendary card never sits beside a number card for the same weapon.
  4. Until the player owns `earlyWeaponUntil` (2) weapons, at least one card is a new weapon when possible.
  5. **Late drafts** (ruling 17 W6): from `lateFrom` (15:00), a draft with no card that changes the build (a new piece, a line, a Legendary, an axis, a keystone, a dash item: `changesBuild`) has its last card turned into a line or a Legendary on a carried weapon, else a new piece or a dash card (`lateSwap`). The drafts of levels 6 and 16 offer another dash item for certain (`dashAt`).
  6. When nothing can be drawn (`draftIsDry`), the level pays without stopping the run (`draft.autoFallback`, `levelGift`): food below half health, else Pearls while the Ferryman is in the run, else Coin, and every 8th such level an Ember kept for later. A skip pays the same (`skipPay`). `autoPick` is the card with the best `upgradeScore`, for the app's "Pick for me" and the bots.
- **The card** (`ui/describe.js upgradeText`, `cardInfo`; the sim's card carries `now`, `after`, `worth`, `milestone`, `opens`, `next`, `deed`): a weapon number shows its real number before and after (`Damage 24 -> 29`, `Flares 2 -> 3`), a line or a special its name and sentence, the rarity as a word and a plain frame, and one line of what's next for its Forms (`New Form at Lv 6 (4 to go)`, its Final Form goal once the player has seen a Form screen). Its tag chips show how many of each tag the build holds, and `Tag bonus: +16% Damage` when that applies. G on the level-up screen opens the card's hardest glossary term (`hardestTerm`).
- Reroll, banish, and skip charges come from the profile (the run tools and the Lamp Post tree); every run starts with 2 of each. Reroll rolls the rarities again.

### 5.11 Chests and relics
- **Elites** drop a chest with 1 item; **bosses** a chest that rolls 1, 3, or 5 items (weighted by luck) with a reel (`sim/chests.js openChest`).
- A chest's item for a weapon is one upgrade rolled on the loot stream, now and then at Rare or better (where a chest once held a rune); with nothing to level, Coin or a heal. No chest evolves anything (§5.16). A boss chest that earns an Ember shows its reel, then the Ember and its Forms, on one screen (tests/firsthour.test.js).
- Relics never come from chests or level-ups: they come from Reliquaries, Lantern Night's vault, the Ferryman, a map's secret, and the Heirloom (§5.18, §10.9).
- **A rung Fog Bell's chest is a pick** (0.8.2, `CHEST_BELL`; `bellOptions`, `bellPickDecide`): every drafted weapon that can roll and every passive carried that isn't maxed; the one chosen gains `tuning.tasks.bell.levels` (2). A weapon's levels are rolled when picked (the loot stream; the halt keeps them as `rolled`). With nothing to level, Coin.
- The last boss's chest opens on the spot, before the stay-or-leave choice, so leaving at once can never skip it. The first boss's chest drops on the ground like any other.
- `nextChestSource` says where the next chest comes from (a chest on the ground, a living elite or boss, the next timeline event, overtime) for the notices.

### 5.12 Characters, relics, and gear as hooks
- Character mechanics, relic rules, and gear's hook affixes are small functions in a **hook registry** (`sim/hooks.js` `HOOKS`; the six characters' in `sim/hooks-characters.js`, joined to it). Data names a hook id plus parameters.
- The hook points (`HOOK_POINTS`): `onTick`, `onLevelUp`, `onKill`, `onBossKill`, `onHit`, `onGemCollect`, `onHeal` (can turn healing into nothing or into shield), `onHurt` (a hit taken, a shielded one too), `onChestOpen`, and `modifyStats`. **There is no `onDash`** (ruling 16, §5.24). A hook may have an `init` and an `undo` (a relic given to the Salt Well or swapped out: `sim/build.js removeRelic`).
- Every hook is deterministic, uses only sim APIs (through `api`, late-bound by `world.js`), keeps its running state as numbers in `h.state` (the world hash walks it), and lists the params it `needs`; the content test checks every hook in data exists and gets them.
- What the characters' hooks do is §5.22. Two rules from Phase 4 still hold for every stun and freeze: bosses are immune to a stun, and an enemy in the middle of a warned attack (a crab winding up or dashing, a gunner aiming, an eel lunging, a toad in its hop: `midAttack` in `sim/enemies.js`) is neither stunned nor frozen, so its attack goes off when its warning said and never after; a boss warning or charging isn't frozen either. (The Harpooner's stun began as the daze of Phase 4, §20 call 44.)

### 5.13 Bosses and elites
- **Elite:** any enemy with the elite modifier gets more HP (times the threat that follows your power, §5.6), a larger size, an outline, a chest, and since 0.9.0 its affixes (§5.6).
- **Champions (0.9.0, `sim/champions.js`):** the 5:00 and 15:00 musters' captains are named champions (the First Mate and the Bosun on the Docks, the Reed Cutter and the Well Warden on the Marsh) with twice an elite's health, no affixes, and one attack each, thrown every `every` s while you're within `reach`: `anchor` (a ring where you stand, then an anchor lands in it), `rope_drag` (a line toward you; on it, you're hurt and dragged along it through `world.pull`, which a dash is free of), `scythe` (a ring round him), `salt_wall` (a row of brine pools across your way, square to the line from him, burning for 6 s). Each warns at least `telegraphMin` as a MAJOR warning, and while it warns the champion stands where it warned and can't be held (`EF.WARNING`). Its hits are `HURT.ATTACK` and ask for a boss's hit-stop. A 5:00 champion (`chest: 'elite'`) pays an elite's chest; a 15:00 one a boss chest and an Ember.
- **Boss:** an enemy definition with phases and attacks drawn from a pattern library: ground slam ring, charge line, summon wave, slow projectile fan, and hazard pools, plus each MVP boss's own: the Harbor Maw's **lure** (its bite's ring shows, and for the whole warning you're drawn toward it, slower than you walk, through `world.pull`, which a dash ignores; then it bites inside the ring; it starts only while the whole ring is on screen, and otherwise waits its turn, and ends with its own Maw) and the Tidecaller's **tide lanes** (rows of pools across where you stand, each row warning a moment after the last; stand between two rows). The Marsh's bosses (0.8.0) add three: the Old Grey's **stab_line** (a warned line from the boss toward you, `length` by `width`), **leap** (it flies, untouchable, while a shadow ring at least 0.8 s long warns where it lands; landing on mud sticks it for `stuck` seconds and it takes double damage, its **weak window**), and the Salt Widow's **lightning_march** (`count` warned rings marching `step` px apart toward you, `stagger` s each, hurting enemies by `enemyDamage` and you by `damage`, never her; on salt each raises a glass pillar; she kneels, weak, for `kneel` s after). `summon_wave` takes `water: N` to put its crew (eels) in water within N tiles of the spot instead of on the ring. **Phase gates (0.9.0, ruling 5; they replace minFight's time cap):** each phase names its `signature`. A boss can't lose health past the notch below its phase (the next phase's `from`, held a hair above; for the last phase, its death, held at 1) until it has thrown that phase's signature once; a boss held at a notch throws its signature next (`bossAllow`, `openGate`; `EV.BOSS_GATE`, `HIT_GATED`), and a gate never holds longer than `tuning.bosses.gateMaxHold` (20 s, a safety). The boss record carries `notches` (for the bar), `sigDone`, `held`, and `eaten`. A weak window multiplies damage by `weakMul` and skips the floor (`EV.BOSS_WEAK` at its start and end); `minFight` is a 15 s safety floor for every boss. Every attack has a `name` said mid-sentence (the death recap's "the Tidecaller's tide line"). Every boss's attack data is checked by type when a run starts (`checkBossDef`): a lure slower than you walk with its ring able to fit on screen, tide rows far enough apart to stand between. **Every attack telegraphs for at least 0.8 s** with a clear shape (a pulsing ring or line), so the game stays playable while half-watching TV. Bosses get an HP bar at the top of the screen.
- Big bosses are **code rigs**: several sprites (body, jaw, tentacle segments) animated by code, which is how a small sprite pack produces big bosses. `render/rigs.js` holds each rig's layout (parts, pivots, what follows what); `render/rigpose.js` holds the pure pose function, driven by the boss's state (walking, which attack it is warning of and how far into the warning, charging) read straight from the sim (`rigInput`, which uses the sim's own warning time), and each rig's own light; `render/rigview.js` draws it. The atlas builder reads only the layout, so changing how a rig moves or glows never makes the atlas stale. The Harbor Maw is an anglerfish head: its jaw drops as a slam's warning fills, its fins spread for a summon, and its lure glows like a lamp in the light map. The Tidecaller is a hooded bell-ringer: its bell swings hard while it rings up a fan or a summon, the robe rises before a slam, and tentacles reach from under the hem before its pools, further before its tide lanes. The Maw's lure swells and flickers fast while it lures. The atlas builder draws each boss's resting pose as one frame too (for the death pop).

### 5.14 Events out of the sim
- The sim writes to a preallocated **event buffer** of typed arrays: a type plus six numbers (x, y, a, b, c, d) whose meaning depends on the type (`sim/events.js` lists them), with room for 8,192 events: hit, kill, player hurt, gem collected, level-up, chest, boss spawn, telegraph start, and so on. The headless runner clears it every tick; the game clears it once per rendered frame, which can hold several ticks.
- Render, audio, and the HUD read the buffer. Nothing they do flows back into the sim.
- The sim sends one event per hit. Render groups damage numbers per enemy over a few ticks (Phase 3), so a thousand hits don't become a thousand numbers.
- 0.9.0 (W3) adds codes 64 to 73: `TELE_TIER`, `HIT_STOP`, `BOSS_GATE`, `BOSS_PHASE`, `BOSS_WEAK`, `AFFIX`, `WARD`, `TETHER`, `PLAYER_SLOW`, `CHAMPION` (`work/veteran/W3-CONTRACT.md`).
- The last 1,024 slots are kept for events that matter (kills, hurts, level-ups, telegraphs). When the buffer is that full, low-priority events (hit numbers, status sparks, shot flashes, light pops) are dropped first and counted for the debug overlay.

### 5.15 RunConfig and RunResult
- **RunConfig** (from meta, `meta/runconfig.js runConfigFor`; checked in plain words by `sim/world.js`): seed, map, mode (standard, short, or challenge), character, its start weapon or a kit's (`startWeapon`), the resolved pools (weapons, passives, relics, dash items: `pools`, `dashes`; all or none, each id listed once, known, and draftable, never a character's signature), the slots (`weaponSlots`, `passiveSlots`, `relicSlots`), the Tide and curses (as multipliers and modifiers), worn gear (`mods`, `gearHooks`, rules), the Heirloom (`relics: [id]`, `heirloom`), run tools' starts (`startGlass`, `startEmbers`, charges, `harbor.foodHeal`), the skill tree (`skills: { id: numbers }`), the capstones the profile has (`forms`), `tempered`, `legendaryEarly`, the map's `layers`, `mapEvents` open, `wellRelics`, `noHeal`, the harbor's part (`harbor`), and the reroll, banish, and skip charges. A config from before ruling 14 that lists `runes` is passed over.
- **There is no active item button**: the dash stays the only active input, per the brief, and it fires nothing (§5.24).
- **RunResult** (`sim/run-result.js buildRunResult`): time survived, how the run ended (died, left, cleared, `dawn`), kills per enemy type, bosses killed and with what, each weapon (`{ id, slot, level, lines, specials, forms, deed, damage, real }`; `runes` is an always-empty list kept for old readers), `damageBySlot` and `realBySlot` (§5.21), the dash (`{ id, ups, swaps }`), the trade's counts (`trade`), thralls and their damage, the ledger of the map's tasks, relics, relic swaps, the Heirloom, secrets and finds, map events, first sightings, materials, damage taken, and whether the tuning panel or its dev commands were used (`devTools`, §15). It must be rich enough to judge every Harbor Order without rerunning anything.

---


### 5.16 Forms and Embers (0.9.0, ruling 14 C; the format: `work/cw/C1-CONTRACT.md` §4 to §6)
Checked against the code Nov 20. Evolutions are gone (the lead's ruling 1, Nov 8): a weapon changes only through its Forms. The history (Forms as three stacking pieces a line in 0.6.2, toned down in 0.7.2, given a partner passive and Form progress in W1 and W1b) is in §22 and DECISIONS.md; none of it is in the code now.
- **Three axes, two options each, one held per axis** (`data/ascensions.js`, 20 lines: the 14 drafted weapons' and, joined by `data/index.js`, the six signatures' from `data/signature-forms.js`). The axis ids stay `delivery`, `flight`, and `payoff` (saves, replays, and codex keys use them); the screens say First Form, Second Form, and Final Form (WRITING.md §4). An option is `{ id, axis, name, desc, price, boost, capstone?, control?, look?, excludes? }`. `price` is the line the card prints after "Drawback:"; where it is a number, `boost.stats` carries it. `boost` is the one shape lines, specials, and options share: `{ stats, onHit, emit, motion, fx }`. Only a delivery option replaces an emitter's type and only a flight option a motion's type, so nothing a weapon holds can cancel another thing it holds. `excludes` keeps two options apart either way round (Breakers and Great Toll, ruling 21 WA; `sim/ascend.js excluded`). `control: true` marks an option whose worth is what it stops (Dazzle, Brinicle, Drag Under, Dredge, Hamstring, Rime, Ice Wall, Fog Bank): one damage rider of at most +40% (ruling 18 WC).
- **When an axis opens** (`sim/ascend.js`): `delivery` at the weapon's 5th upgrade and `flight` at its 10th (`tuning.forms.at` [5, 10]; `levelUpAxis`, `axisAt`); `payoff` when the weapon's deed is done in the run and the profile has its capstone (`formsStep` reads `world.deedsDone`). An axis opening stops the run for the choice: halt `{ kind: 'ascend', slot, axis, why }`, answered `{ kind: 'ascend', slot, id }` or `{ kind: 'keep' }` (the pause menu's Forms holds it for later). `openAxis` sends `EV.AXIS_OPEN`; `takeOption` puts an option on and sends `EV.ASCEND` (b 1 for a capstone, c 1 when it switched one). `axisState(world, s)` is what every card and screen reads: each axis open or shut, what it holds, its two options, what keeps it locked, the upgrade it opens at, and its deed's count.
- **Deeds** (`sim/deeds.js`; a weapon's data `deed: { kind, count, text, ...params }`): `kills` (with `near`, `far`, `moving`, `still`, `status`, `origin`), `multi` (n kills inside a time), and `hits`. Counted from the moment the weapon is carried (`deedKill`, `deedHit`; `slot.deed`, hashed); `deedState` gives `{ have, need, done, text }`; `EV.DEED` (81) when the count moves. Ruling 18 WE set the counts so a deed lands at about 5 to 12 minutes in a run that shares its kills (C1 measured 2.5 to 15.9 minutes, most inside 5 to 12).
- **A line's capstone may wait on the Library**: `capstoneUnlock` (four tier-1 projects: the Lantern Orb's, the Harpoon Chain's, the Tide Bell's, and the Oil Flask's); `RunConfig.forms` lists the first capstone option's id when it is open.
- **Partner passives** gate nothing now. With a capstone held, the weapon does `tuning.forms.partnerPer` (6%) more damage for each level of its partner passive carried (`capstoneOf(data, weaponId)`); the capstone card and the passive card both say so.
- **Embers** (an elite's drop at `tuning.embers.eliteChance`, every boss, a 15:00 champion, the Ferryman, a run tool): `collectEmber` stops the run for the Form screen while some weapon can use one (`emberUseful`), else keeps it. An Ember either opens a drafted weapon's next shut axis early (delivery, then flight; the capstone only once both others are held and the profile has it) or switches the option held on an axis for the other one (`optionChoices` lists each choice with `cost: 'free' | 'ember'` and what it would switch). Embers never go on a signature. Leveling no longer earns Embers, and nothing is bound to a weapon. The Form screen shows one weapon at a time (RULING-L1).
- **The mechanics** (`sim/forms.js`, `FORM_KEYS`): a `boost.fx` names keys this file knows (the list in `work/cw/C1-CONTRACT.md` appendix A); a key it doesn't know passes through into `slot.fx` for a character's hook to read (§5.22). `tests/forms.test.js` fails a key in the data that neither knows. `slot.fs` holds each weapon's running state (a planted pot's place, a stance, the last cue's tick). What fires from a kill is bounded: a kill burst at most every `tuning.forms.killburstEvery` (0.2 s) a weapon, a spread burn 60% of the one it came from.
- **Balance rules with tests** (ruling 17 W2, ruling 18 WA, ruling 21 WE): on every axis the two options are within 2x of each other on their own ruler and none is under x1.15; every set of three (one option an axis, 8 a weapon) is within 2.5x of its weapon's median set. `tests/forms-balance.test.js` reads the bench numbers checked into `tests/fixtures/form-bench.json` (written by C1's `work/cw/C1/formfixture.mjs` from `bench.mjs` rows; neither tool is in the repo). The signatures have their own: `tests/sig-balance.test.js` over `tests/fixtures/sig-sets.json` and `sig-bshare.json` (§5.22).
- **Saves and replays:** the first payoff option of each line keeps the id of the old capstone, so `RunConfig.forms`, research keys, and `form:<id>` codex keys still work; `data/ascensions.js RETIRED_WEAPONS` maps each evolved weapon to its base weapon with that capstone (a save's codex key and a dev `give`), and `RETIRED_FORMS` maps the cut options to what took their place. `sim/chests.js` still exports `canEvolve`, `showsReady`, `evolveOne`, and `evolvableSlot` as stubs that say nothing evolves.

### 5.17 The Salt Marsh: changing ground, the storm, the Well (0.8.0)
The design is `docs/design/SALT_MARSH.md` (Kevin took all eight decisions as recommended); the contract other parts build on (ids, fields, events, halts, the RunConfig, the result) is `docs/design/SALT_MARSH_BUILD.md`.
- **The layout** (`sim/marsh.js buildMarsh`, cached per seed and size; `checkMarsh` checks its data): 5 creeks edge to edge, 3 to 4 tiles wide and kept apart (each middle at least 0.2 of the map from the others, at most 12% of one alongside another), 2 or 3 boardwalks over each, 12 peat islands, 140 stumps, 90 reed beds, a plank landing at the map's `start` with a 3x3 deep pool east of it for the skiff, and in the far southeast a peat island in a deep moat with a stone causeway (the secret's). Ground a fill from the landing can't reach becomes stumps. It returns the tiles (`kinds`, `solid`), each tile's **flood height** (`floodAt`: creeks 0 to 100 by their distance from the mouths, the causeway 50, the salt flats 110 to 250 by their distance from water, 255 never), the floodable tiles sorted by height (`order`, with `firstAt[h]` the count below `h`, so the tiles between two levels are one slice), and the counts the overtime flood needs. Ground kinds 9 to 17 (`GROUND`): SALT, MUD, REEDS, PEAT, STUMP (solid), DEEP (solid), CAUSEWAY, BOARDWALK, LANDING.
- **The terrain's grids** (`sim/terrain.js`): `base` (the layout's walls), `wet` (water now), `pillar` (glass pillars now); `solid` is any of the three. `swimWall` is what stops a swimmer (walls and pillars, not water) and `dryWall` what an eel can't leave (everything that isn't water). `refresh()` rebuilds `solid` and the summed-area tables from them and bumps `version`; a flow field rebuilds when the version it was built on has changed. Amphibious enemies (leeches) read a second field with `swim: true` (`world.swimFlow`), whose walls are `swimWall`, and collide on that grid.
- **The water** (`map.water`; `createMarsh`, `marshStep`, right after the player moves): high water at 3:00 to 8:00 and 13:00 to 18:00, creeping in over 10 s from the creek mouths (the level climbs through the creeks' heights to `HIGH`, 101) and out the same way, warned 15 s ahead (WATER events: warning, rising, high, ebbing, low). In a short run the windows scale with the timeline but the creep and warning don't. High water is a wall to all but swimmers. Mud slows enemies to 0.6 (never you). Overtime: in at its start after its own warning, then every minute a further 8% of the flats floods (`flatsLevel`: the highest level that floods no more than that share and leaves 35% of the open ground dry), never out.
- **Rule 8: the flood never closes around you.** Once per water level, a 0-1 breadth-first search from the landing gives every tile a step toward it over the fewest would-be-wet tiles (stumps and deep water block; pillars count as open, since they fall). Each pass walks that chain from your tile and holds its would-be-wet tiles dry; it releases the tiles the last chain held and the new one doesn't, and records only tiles whose state changed (`touched`). A pass runs only when the level or your tile changed. The ground catches up at most 4 times a second (`rebuildEvery` 0.25 s): each touched tile takes the water it should have, then one `refresh()` if any changed. Walkers on a tile that now blocks them move to the nearest bank, an eel left on dry ground sinks into the mud (no kill, no loot), and a chest, Reliquary, or vault moves to dry ground. A glass pillar never rises on the held chain (`onPath`), and one the chain comes to cross falls at once. The overtime flood's level for each minute is worked out once (`otLevel`). An open Salt Well the water reaches moves to dry ground too. The gate checks the deepest creek, the island and its causeway, a 4-minute overtime walk among pillars, a pillar the chain comes to cross, the overtime cap, and the flow fields re-routing at high water (a walker round by a boardwalk, a leech straight across); `tools/marsh/rule8.mjs` sweeps every tile at 6 levels (up to the overtime cap) and six 6-minute overtime walks.
- **Herbs:** 80 beds on creek mud, 1 or 2 Marsh Herbs each, placed from the loot stream; under water at high water; walking over one at low water picks it (HERB).
- **The storm** (`sim/storm.js`, its own RNG stream `world.rng.storm`; layer `storm`): in its windows (5:30 to 8:00 every 40 s, 3 to 6 rings; 15:30 to 18:00 and overtime every 30 s, 4 to 7) warned rings fall on the thickest crowd standing on salt (`crowdPoint`, sampled across the whole screen among the enemies on salt only, so a few on salt among many off it are found), every ring of a burst through that pick, kept in the storm's own picks buffer (`world.storm.picks`, sized for its largest burst: the map's, overtime's, and the Eye's), 1.2 s warning, radius 28, 80 damage to enemies and 8 to you; every 8th ring of the run leaves Salt Glass. With fewer crowds on salt than rings, the burst is that much smaller. A strike is a STRIKE zone (`strikeAt`); stormproof enemies (the Salt Widow) are never hurt by one. **The Eye** (19:00 to 19:45, a director event `eye`; its length follows the run's clock, so a short run's lasts 22.5 s and ends before the Salt Widow): spawns stop, rings every 2.5 s (4 at a time, radius 30, 120 damage), 30 gems and 4 Glass. **Glass pillars** (`raisePillar`) rise only where the Salt Widow's lightning lands on salt (on any map, storm or not): never within 3 tiles of you, never on the held chain, at most 6, falling after 8 s; a pillar the held chain comes to run through (the water rose, or you moved: the search counts pillars as open) falls at once (`pillarsOffPath`), so none ever stands on your way home.
- **The Salt Well** (`sim/well.js`; layer `well`): the 15:00 muster brings the Well Warden and 24 Salted Dead (without the layer, an elite Salted Dead instead: the director's `otherwise`; and no captain opens a well, the overtime Salt Watch's included). Where the captain falls a well opens, with no timer; standing still within 18 px for 0.6 s (not while sunk) stops the run for the `well` halt: give a carried relic (`{ kind: 'give', id }`) or keep (`{ kind: 'keep' }`). A given relic is gone for the run: `removeRelic` (`sim/build.js`) runs each of its hooks' `undo` (the Sea Urchin, Brine Vat, Try-Pot, and Bog Torc put the world back), removes its modifiers, and bans it for the rest of the run (the Brine Vat's undo also ends every salt and stacked slow on the field); the result's `wellGiven` names it (the Ferryman's Well slot is meta's, B5).
- **Thralls** (`sim/allies.js`, the Bog Warden): an enemy that dies while rotting rises as a thrall for 6 s (at most 6 by the hook, 8 in the pool), walks to the nearest enemy and bites it; a thrall caught by the water moves to the bank.
- **New enemies** (`sim/enemies.js`): `swims` (`land`, `amphibious`, `water`), `clump` (spawns in clumps), `crust` (halves damage until a slot that `cracks` it hits: Frost, Sea, Steel), `latch` (a leech latches on, at most 3, drains 1 HP every 2 s; a dash, a freeze, or a daze throws them off; it never bites, so a fourth waits beside you until a latch frees, and none latches while you're sunk), and two AI types: `hopper` (the Mire Toad: a shadow ring warns, it's untouchable in the air (`EF.AIR`), it lands in the ring on its own clock, held or not) and `eel` (the Creek Eel: stays in water, lunges along a warned line exactly as long as the lunge, which carries at most `bank` (24) px past the water's edge and ends where it reaches you, then slides back; you never push it; it sinks into the mud when its creek drains or you're more than 560 px away). An eel warning or lunging and a toad in its hop count as mid-attack (`midAttack`): no freeze, daze, or salt holds them. `EF_GONE` (dead or in the air) is what every search and hit skips. A muster captain with `well: true` opens the well.
- **Hold statuses:** SALT (the Brine Vat: slows that add up past 80% turn to salt, held still for 4 s, then crumbling, a kill whatever the crust; never a boss, an enemy in the air, or one mid-attack), SOAK and the puddles (two overlapping freeze), THAW (2 s after a freeze ends, no freeze), TAR, FEVER, DAZE.
- **The director** (`sim/director.js`): each map lists the `layers` it has (`LAYERS`: storm, well, secret); an event with `layer` runs only when the run's layer is on, and its `otherwise` runs instead when it's off. New event types: `flock` (gulls, at most 8 in a tight clump from one side: the Marsh has no `wall` events) and `eye`. Enemies with `swims: 'water'` spawn in water on the ring (`waterPosition`), or not at all.
- **First sightings** (`sim/sight.js`): the first time a run meets each enemy type, and each thing (the water, the storm, the Eye, the herbs, the well, a pillar, the causeway, a thrall), one FIRST_SIGHT event and a name in the result's `sighted`, for the field guide.
- **Determinism and cost:** everything new is in the world hash (`hashMarsh`, `hashStorm`, `hashWell`, `hashAllies`, the new enemy fields). The search is O(tiles) once per level change (a high water has about 100; overtime adds one a minute); a pass is O(chain); the per-tick cost without a level change is a tile compare. No allocation on the tick path. Measured in `tools/soak --map salt_marsh` (§17).


### 5.18 The interactables (0.8.2)
The design is `work/interact/proposal.md` (sections 2 and 3) under the lead's calls (`DECISIONS-I1.md`); the contract the view, HUD, sound, and UI build on (events, rings, arrows, halts, the ledger) is `work/interact/I1-CONTRACT.md`. It reworks §10.8's tasks, relics, and Ferryman.
- **One verb per shape, no use button.** Walk over a pickup or box. Stay inside a dashed ring until it fills, moving allowed (a lamp; the **ring-fill opening** of the Ferryman, the Salt Well, and a bell's start, which fills in `openSec` 0.6 s, opens once, and re-arms only when you leave its ring; the Well's doesn't fill while you carry no relic or are sunk). Kill inside a dashed ring with a number (a tolled bell). A solid ring in the warning color is always something landing (§5.13); `sim/signs.js collectRings` gives every task ring with its kind, color, fill, and count.
- **Wayside Lamps are wards** (idea 1; Kindled is gone). Lighting is as before (`tasks.lamp`). A lit lamp keeps a pool of light (`tasks.ward.radius` 80 px) that every `every` 0.5 s burns each enemy in it for `every / killSec` of its own health (an ordinary one dies in about 5 s), elites and bosses at `eliteShare` (0.1) of that rate (the boss floor holds, §5.13). Wards farther than 1,000 px from you skip their pulse (nothing stands there: stragglers jump ahead). A chest, an Ember, a Reliquary, or the vault that lands in a lit lamp's light says so (`EV.DROP` b), so a lamp watches the ground.
- **Lantern Night waits for the crowd** (idea 4). Every lamp lit at once banks it (`night.banked`, `EV.NIGHT` a 2). It falls when `tasks.night.crowd` (15) enemies are within `crowdRadius` (160 px) of you, checked every 0.1 s, or when a muster or boss arrives (`crowdArrives`, called by the director). Its burn is `night.burn` times the pace dial's health (`pace.hp`) times the minute's growth. When it ends, the vault drops `vaultDelay` (1 s) later, `vaultAhead` (60 px) ahead of you.
- **(Until 0.9.0) The fog took the lamps back** (idea 5; ruling 6 ended it: lamps stay lit, and each act brings new lamps instead, §5.20). Each boss's arrival put every lit lamp out, nearest you first, `outGap` (0.4 s) apart; each one's ward shrinks over `outSec` (1 s), then it's an unlit lamp again that asks and pays at the new minute. All of them lit again banks another night. On a short run the bosses come at 5:00 and 10:00, so that's when.
- **Fog Bells call their crowd** (idea 2). The bell's small ring (`startRadius` 20 px) tolls it. It plans a crew as big as the kills it needs (10 plus 2 a minute, at most `BELL_CREW_MAX` 64) from the current band's roster (no water-only swimmers), placed round it at `crewRadius` (220 px, falling back to closer or farther rings on blocked ground); they gather for `gatherSec` (1.2 s: the view shows their places, `crewX/crewY`), then spawn as ordinary enemies tagged with the bell (`E.crew`, the task index + 1) and hunt you. A kill inside its ring counts down (`EV.BELL_COUNT`), and so does each of its crew wherever it falls. At none left it rings: everything inside the ring is dazed `dazeSec` (0.4 s; the Harpooner's daze), and its chest (`CHEST_BELL`, §5.11) drops at the bell. Crew spawns draw from the spawn stream.
- **The relic pool** (core 2.4). A fresh profile's pool holds 4. `relicPool(world, out)` counts it without allocating. A Reliquary waits at least `reliquaryGap`, and after one drops at least the seconds left to the map's last boss over the relics still to find after it plus one (`reliquary.wait`), so a small pool lasts the run. With every relic in the pool found none drops, and the run says so once (`EV.RELIC_POOL`); nothing replaces it. One from a kill more than `pullFrom` (200 px) from you lands `pullTo` (120 px) from you, toward the kill. A vault (or a Reliquary emptied by a purchase since it dropped) with nothing to offer gives an Ember (`EV.VAULT_EMPTY`).
- **Pearls** (Sea Glass until 0.8.2; the code and saves still say glass) exist only with the Ferryman in the run (`world.pearlsOn`): before the Docks no kill drops them and `addGlass` does nothing, the Pearl Pouch included.
- **The Ferryman calls on the water** (idea 6). `map.ferry.moorings` (4 to 6), each snapped to a dry tile beside water or a creek's bed (`snapShore`: the Marsh's mud counts as water for a skiff, and the boards are never mud or the causeway). He isn't there at 0:00: he calls at `firstCall` (3:00) and every `callEvery` (5:00) after, on the map's clock (on the Marsh, the turn of the water), casts off from where he was, and ties up at the mooring nearest you that's at least `minAway` (250 px) from you, rowing in from 160 px beyond the skiff for `rowSec` (4 s) before the trade opens. Each call is a new stock, a little better each time: one relic, two from the Docks' level 2, three from level 4 (the Docks' level 4 was a faster restock). A relic found elsewhere since he stocked it is marked `gone` when the trade opens and can't be bought.
- **Placement.** The Drowned Docks' lamps sit one in each quarter and one west, 544 to 672 px of path from the start; the Salt Marsh's bells within about 1,200 px of the landing.
- **Food and the magnet** keep their own rates a kill: the pace dial's drops reach materials only.
- **First sightings** add the lamp and the bell (first within 320 x 180 px of you), the Ferryman (his first call), and a Reliquary (the first that drops), with field-guide lines in `data/meta.js`.
- **The ledger** (idea 10): `mapHolds(map)` (what a map has, for the gate's card), `runLedger(world)` (the run against it, for the pause menu), and the RunResult's `ledger`, with `lanternNights` and `ferryTrades` beside it.
- **The bots.** The kite bot answers the `bellpick` halt (its card score) and skips `gone` offers. `createKiteBot({ follow: true })` plays a player who follows the edge arrows (between bosses and above 40% health: the nearest unlit lamp, bell not rung, or the Ferryman with something it can afford); it's off by default, so the soak, the balance sweeps, and the pacing yields play as before. `work/interact/probe/probe2.mjs` measures the interactables with it.
- **What the player sees, hears, and feels** (I1b; the view reads the sim and writes nothing back). `render/signview.js`: the dashed task rings in each kind's own color (`SIGN_COLOR`: lamp gold, a ward's pool warmer orange, the bell blue, the Ferryman teal, the Well cold grey) with a solid arc for how far each has filled; a lit lamp's warm pool and its bright glass with a white flame (an unlit lamp's glass is dark), a small flame on each enemy burning in a ward; a tolled bell's crew taking shape over the fog at its gathering places, its count over it, and the wave when it rings; what an unlit lamp or idle bell pays over it (`rewardOf`: a flame for a ward, `+2 Lv` for the bell's pick, a Pearl and the number); light beams over a Reliquary (violet) and the vault (gold-white); `Carry a relic first` over the Salt Well; a post at each mooring, and the Ferryman (the harbor's fisher) standing in his skiff as it rows in, eased, from his call to tying up (`skiffAt`). The Lamplighter's own aura keeps a faint solid edge, never dashed. `render/arrows.js`: the edge arrows (`collectArrows`), one per kind to the nearest off screen, each its color wearing its minimap mark in a dark disc with a pointer, placed round the view's edge under the top line, slid off the HUD's corners and spread 16 px apart; Settings' `Edge arrows` turns them off (`hud.arrows`). The minimap marks every lamp and bell from 0:00 (unlit, part-lit, and lit lamps; idle and tolled bells; each its own shape), the Ferryman only while he's out, the vault apart from a Reliquary, and pins to its edge a Reliquary (with the Cartographer's first level) and anything a lit lamp watched land. Under the minimap, the map's row: lamps lit and bells rung (`placeMapRow`), the lamps framed and glowing while Lantern Night is banked. Notices are one short line each (`app/runlogic.js noticeFor`). The pause menu (`This map`) and the run's end show the ledger (`ledgerLines`); the gate's map card says what a map holds (`ui/hubtext.js mapHoldsText`). Every player-facing "Sea Glass" reads Pearls, and the Pearl is drawn as one (§16.5).
- **Determinism and cost.** Everything new is in the world hash (`hashTasks`, the Ferryman's call state, the Reliquary's wait, `E.crew` in the pool). The ward pulse is two queries a second per lit lamp near you; the crowd check one query every 0.1 s while a night is banked; the relic pool count is a loop over the pool with its banish keys made once (no strings a kill). No allocation on the tick path; a bell's toll and its crew walking in, a call, and a boss's lamps-out are cold paths.

### 5.19 Slots, relic slots, and the draft's tools (0.9.0, the lead's rulings 3 and 4)
- **Slots grow with the harbor** (`data/tuning.js SLOTS`, `meta/state.js slotsFor`, sent as RunConfig `weaponSlots`, `passiveSlots`, `relicSlots`; 6, 6, 6 when a config leaves them out, for tools and tests): weapons 4 for drafted weapons (a character's signature takes a slot of its own on top, ruling 17 W5), +1 at the Lamp Post's levels 3 and 5; passives 4, +1 at the Smithy's 2 and 4; relics 3, +1 for each Docks level past 2. A fresh profile's pools hold 6 weapons and 8 passives (the Oil Flask, Sea Legs, the Lodestone, the Tallow Candle, and Bittern's Call became starters), so the pool is about twice the slots from the first run.
- **Relic slots.** A Reliquary's, the vault's, or a secret cache's pick with the slots full (`halt.full`) is `{ kind: 'pick', index, swap: id }` or `{ kind: 'pass' }`; buying a relic from the Ferryman with them full needs `swap` too. The relic given up is taken off as the Well's is (its hook's `undo`, its modifiers) but not banned: it can be found again, and the Ferryman stocks it (`returned`), in his stock now if he's tied up, else his next (`build.returned`). `EV.RELIC_SWAP`; the result's `relicSwaps`. Relics a run starts with stretch the slots to hold them.
- **Relics with a price:** the Seventh Bell (-10% XP), Volatile Gems (-25% Pickup Range), the Drowned Bell (-10% Move Speed), and the Brine Vat (-15 Max HP): the price is in `mods` and named in `downside` for the card.
- **Measured** (`tools/bots/builds.mjs`: the kite bot following the arrows, from a harbor stage's own profile and pools, six archetypes on two seeds, Docks; 0.8.2, then 0.9.0's W1 alone): a fresh profile's runs ending with the same build 11 of 12, then 2 of 12 (8 different builds, was 2); drafts short of 3 cards 21%, then 3% (2.70 cards a draft, then 2.97); levels paid without a pick 19.0 a run (10.5 after 15:00), then 0; Forms taken 2.3 a run, then 7.4. At half the harbor: the same build 2 of 12 both; levels without a pick after 15:00 12.4, then 4.0; Forms 3.3, then 12.8. Each base weapon alone for 9 minutes without Forms (damage a minute; 0.8.2 had no Form progress): the Lantern Orb 5,271 (2,254), the Harpoon Chain 5,072 (2,310), the Glass Caltrops 8,198 (2,626), against the Flare Gun's 8,996 and the Oil Flask's 13,693.


### 5.20 The run in three acts, the maps' places, and overtime's ending (0.9.0, W4; the lead's ruling 6; V2's and V3's lists)
The contract other batches read (result fields, map data, events 74 to 78, the orders) is `work/veteran/W4-NOTES.md`.
- **Three acts** (`sim/acts.js`, created and stepped from `sim/tasks.js`; `world.acts`). Act 1 from 0:00, act 2 at the first boss's arrival (`crowdArrives`), act 3 when you Stay for overtime (`overtimeDecide`). **Lamps stay lit**: the fog no longer takes them back (§5.18's idea 5 is gone). Each act appends its own tasks to `world.tasks` (records carry `act`, `place`, `twist`): its lamps at its start, its Fog Bells one at a time through it (`acts[n].bellAt`, seconds into the act, each placed when it wakes). Sites come from the map's `sites` (about 14 to 16 lamp and 10 to 11 bell sites spread over the whole map), drawn on the run's own stream `world.rng.acts` (a fork of the root, so no other stream moves): unused this run, at least `tuning.tasks.acts.lampAway` (34) tiles from you for a lamp and `bellAway` (30) for a bell, `apart` (30) from the act's other lamps, one place at a time while places are left (relaxed in that order when sites run short). Act 3 on the Marsh keeps to ground overtime's flood never takes. No task has a countdown. Lantern Night banks again once every lamp out is lit.
- **Twists** (`map.twists`, `acts[n].twists`: act 1 none, act 2 one, act 3 two): a **held lamp** (`tuning.tasks.held`: 2.5 times as long; while you're inside its ring, a crowd of 8 times `pace.events` gathers 200 px round it every 5 s, gathering like a bell's crew with a DIM tell, then walks in as a set piece; it pays a chest and twice the Pearls) and an **elite crew** (a bell's crew led by an elite with its affixes).
- **The map's deck** (`map.deck`): each act draws `acts[n].deck` entries open to it (RunConfig `mapEvents`, W5's `mapEventsOpen`; all when left out), spread over the act's `span` in equal slices, and inserts their events into the director's timeline (`insertEvent`, keeping `lastBossEvent` on the last boss; the last boss's event is marked `final`). Kinds: any timeline set piece (sized by `pace.events`), and the deck's own: `cache` (a chest at a cache site away from you, its guard rising `guardWake` 460 px out, just past the view), `trail` (a cache plus a line of gems toward it), `rising` (a crowd out of a named place, its spoils left there), `fire` (a line of warned strikes through the thickest crowd). `EV.MAP_EVENT`; the result's `mapEvents`.
- **Places** (`map.places`): named districts and landmarks; notices say where a bell wakes by them. The Drowned Docks' layout builds districts from the ground kinds there are (`layout.districts`, `sim/terrain.js DISTRICT_KINDS`): the crane yard (container stacks in rows, its north strip open for the cranes), the fish market (two halls and rows of stalls), the breakwater (the harbor mouth's water and a stone arm out to its point), the ropewalk (one 72-tile shed), and the dry dock (a basin open to the sea, a ship's frame in it). Slips, sheds, and crates keep off them (34, 44, 240 became 24, 32, 200).
- **Each map's own task** (`TASK.CRANE`, `TASK.CAIRN`; `map.cranes`, `map.cairns`; `tuning.tasks.crane`, `.cairn`): its ring fills while you're in it (1 s); a crane then drops 3 loads 1.6 s apart, each a warned ring on the thickest crowd within 340 px of it; a cairn calls 2 strikes every 2 s for 20 s on the crowds within 300 px. Both are strikes that never hurt you (`STRIKE_SRC.CRANE`, `.CAIRN`), so their warnings are DIM tells (`strikeAt` with no damage to you); their damage grows as enemy health does. Ready again each act. Drawn in code (`render/landmarks.js`), no atlas.
- **Small finds** (`map.secrets` with `gift`, `from`): 5 on each map at its edges, plus the character secret (Breakwater Point, from act 2). Walking onto one gives Pearls (Coin without the Ferryman), an Ember, a chest item, materials, or food, once a run (`openFind`; `EV.FIND`; the result's `finds` and `secretsFound`, so the codex keys them as secrets). A faint glint within 150 px; the Cartographer's marks show them once out.
- **The Salt Well asks once a run** on its own; after that it asks again only when you stand still in its ring for `tuning.well.openSec` (ruling 16: the dash links to nothing).
- **Overtime's shape** (`map.overtime.repeat` and `.events`): an elite pairing every minute, a new pair each time in turn (`pairs`); the map's first boss back at 5:00 (`spawnBoss`, not final); at 10:00 **the Long Night**: the map's bosses back together, the second 15 s behind, at 80% health (`longNight`). Kill them all and **dawn** breaks: nothing more spawns (`director.dawn`), what's left of the horde crumbles (no kills, no loot), every chest, Ember, Reliquary, and vault on the ground comes to you, the dawn chest opens (a boss chest, its halt marked `dawn`), and the run ends cleared `dawnSec` (8 s) later with `result.dawn`. Losing is dying in it; overtime's escalation is the pressure. `EV.FINALE`.
- **The follow bot** walks a path field to its errand (`sim/botpath.js`), and goes to chests on the ground.
- **Determinism and cost.** The acts' state, its stream, the director's dawn and one-shots, and every task's place are in the world hash (`hashActs`). An act's draw, a bell's waking, a cache's guard, and the dawn are cold paths; a held lamp's crowd and a crane's loads allocate nothing per tick.

### 5.21 How a weapon grows (0.9.0: Kevin's ruling 10, then ruling 14 A and B, rulings 17 to 21; the format: `work/cw/C1-CONTRACT.md` §2, §3, §7, §16 to §19)
Checked against the code Nov 20. Weapons have no level table and no max level (Kevin, Nov 8). Ruling 14 halved the level-ups and made each card bigger: a cleared 20:00 run reaches about level 35.
