- **The weapon format, v4** (`data/weapons.js`, the 14 drafted weapons; `data/signatures.js`, the six signatures): `{ id, name, desc, tags, sprite, icon, unlock, weight, sfx, unit, units, base, pipeline, numbers, lines, specials, deed, partner, steps?, caps?, signature? }`. `numbers` lists the number types its Common and Uncommon cards can roll (ids in `data/weaponups.js`). `lines` are its Rare and Epic behavior, each once a run (`{ id, name, rarity, desc, boost, shared? }`; a `shared` line keeps one name on every weapon that has it: Splinter, Arc, Burning Ground, Backfire, Encore, Weak Point). `specials` are its two or three Legendaries, once each (`moves: true` on at least one: it changes how you move or where you stand). `steps: [{ at, pierce, damageMul }]` is a late step at its `at`-th upgrade (the Harpoon Chain: +1 Pierce and x1.2 at its 10th and 20th). Gone from a weapon: `core`, `upgrades`, `upCaps`, `upMul`, `special`, `runeSlots`, `evolution`.
- **Upgrade types** (`data/weaponups.js`; their numbers in `tuning.weaponUps.value`): Damage, Cooldown, Area, Duration (Common and up); Projectile, Pierce, Crit Chance, Hit Delay (Uncommon and up; +2 at Epic and above for a whole type); a `line` (Rare or Epic) and a `special` (Legendary). Damage multiplies what the weapon does now (+20% is +20%); Cooldown multiplies it down; Area and the counts add. A number's value at a rarity is `value x mul[r]` (`mul` 1, 1.5, 2.2, 3, 4). Each Damage card on one weapon is `damageDecay` (0.84) of the one before (`upValue`), and `baseDamage` (1.3) lifts every weapon's base. A type at its cap leaves the pool (`upCapped`; caps in `tuning.weaponUps.caps`); past every cap, growth is damage.
- **The roll** (`sim/weaponups.js rollUpgrade(world, s, rng, skip?, opts?)`): odds 55, 27, 12, 5, 1, shifted up by Luck. **The floor:** no card is offered that changes the weapon's output by less than `floor` (8%), by `cardWorth`'s estimate, so tiny cards never show. **Milestones** (`isMilestone`): every `milestone`-th (3rd) upgrade of a weapon is Rare or better (the Whetstone relic makes it every 2nd). **Legendaries:** the 10th, 20th, ... upgrade rolls a Legendary while a special is left, unless one was offered to that weapon since its last ten (`noteLegendaryOffered`); one rolled by luck before the `legendaryFrom` (6th) upgrade is rolled as Epic. A weapon whose mastery order is done gets its first Legendary at its 6th (`RunConfig.legendaryEarly`). A Legendary card never sits beside a number card for the same weapon in one draft. `takeUpgrade` applies one; `giveUpgrade` (`sim/ascend.js`) is the one call for an upgrade that didn't come from a card (it also checks the level's axis and marks the stats dirty). `slot.ups` holds what was taken; `slot.level` is 1 + its count; `slot.lines` and `slot.specials` the ids held.
- **Shared tags, the Tag bonus** (ruling 17's Kindred; RULING-L1 renamed it on every screen): each other weapon or passive held that shares a tag with a weapon makes it `tuning.weaponUps.kindred.per` (8%) stronger, counting at most `max` (3) of them (`sim/weapons.js kinCount`, applied in `buildSlot`). The card says `Tag bonus: +16% Damage` only when it applies; the chips show the counts.
- **Late caps** (ruling 17's runaway pass): `tuning.caps.weapon.zoneHits` (wave 40, beam 40, tether 30, ground 24) is a zone's pierce, counted in `Z.hitN`; `caps.amountExtra` 4.
- **Passives** keep fixed levels and a max. Each one's last level adds a rule (`keystone`, `world.rules.<id>`, rebuilt with the stats; the table in `work/cw/C1-CONTRACT.md` appendix C). New passives are drawn at `draft.newPassiveMul` (0.6) of their weight, at most one a draft.
- **Damage counted two ways:** `result.damageBySlot` (every hit's number, overkill included) and `result.realBySlot` (the health a weapon actually took off enemies). Stats, the run's damage table, the results' signature share, and every balance tool read `realBySlot` (ruling 21 PA).
- **Tempering** (the Smithy's Lv 4, where rune stones were; `meta/runconfig.js`, `sim/world.js temper`): the weapon a run sets out with starts with one of its Rare lines (`RunConfig.tempered: true`). A weapon's order (`data/orders-weapons.js`, `DATA.weaponPerks` kind `temper`) tempers that weapon even without the Smithy. (C1-CONTRACT §12 planned one tempered weapon chosen at the Smithy, `profile.tempered`; the code does neither.)
- Upgrade, line, and Legendary events: `EV.UPGRADE` (84: a slot, b rarity, c kind) and `EV.LEGENDARY` (83: its own sound, rumble, and a held beat; never text mid-fight).

### 5.22 Characters: signature weapons, trades, drawbacks, skill trees (0.9.0: the lead's ruling 2, ruling 14 D, rulings 15 and 16, rulings 17 to 22; the design: `work/cw/C2-DESIGN.md`, its later sections for the numbers' history)
Checked against the code Nov 20. The numbers below are read from `data/characters.js` on that date; the builders were still tuning some of them.
- **A character** (`data/characters.js`) is `{ id, name, sprite, who, desc, gives, costs, card: { does, costs }, start, signature, mechanic: { hook, params }, trade: { id, params }, deedText, base, noHeal?, noDraft?, unlock, hubLine, find }`. `who`, `gives`, and `costs` are the Details page's lines; `card` is the gate's two short lines. A number in any of these lines is a placeholder filled from the data when it loads (`data/textnum.js fillFields`: `{m.x}` mechanic params, `{t.x}` trade params, `{b.x}` base stats, `{s.x}` the standard player; in signatures and Forms `{p.x}`, `{x.x}`, `{o.x}`, `{w.x}`, `{c.x}`); a wrong name throws at load, and `tests/characters.test.js` fails a digit typed by hand in `characters.js`, `signatures.js`, or `signature-forms.js`. `noDraft` keeps weapons out of that character's draft (the Tide Bell for the Harpooner and the Tide Priest).
- **The six** (start weapon, signature, starting dash item, drawback as the card says it):

  | Character | Start | Signature | Dash item | Drawback | Arrives |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Lamplighter | Flare Gun | Lamplight | Wick Step | Her light dims when her Fuel runs out | from the start |
  | Harpooner | Anchor Chain | Grapnel | Heavy Step | She takes 20% more damage | the Lamp Post's Lv 2 |
  | Punter | Lantern Orb | Punt Pole | Long Push | Turns, hits, and stops lose Momentum (no Momentum: -25% damage), 80 Max HP | order First Crossing |
  | Bog Warden | Eel Pot | Bog Hands | Mire Step | Her other weapons deal 10% less damage | order Eel Season |
  | Drowned Man | Oil Flask | Grave Pennies | Sink | 2 fewer weapon slots, 85 Max HP, -15% Move Speed | the Docks' Breakwater Point secret |
  | Tide Priest | Corpse Candles | King Tide | Swell | Can't heal; takes 15% more damage with no shield | order Untouched Tide (Brother Hale's; reach 15:00 taking 100 damage or less) |

  Kits (`data/mastery.js KITS`) offer other start weapons at mastery ranks 2 and 4.
- **The signature weapon** (`data/signatures.js`, v4 weapons with `signature: '<character>'`; their axes in `data/signature-forms.js`): put in the first weapon slot at setup (`sim/trades.js giveSignature`), outside the slot count (`sim/world.js`: a character with a signature gets one more weapon slot, so every slot is for drafted weapons). Never in the draft, a chest, a Fog Bell's pick, an Ember's choice, or the Ferryman's stock. It does its own verb at level 1, in the character's hook: Lamplight burns what stands in her light (the game's one aura); the Grapnel hauls the last enemy its hook bit (or, when that bite killed, the nearest living enemy within `catchRadius` of it, C2b Nov 20) to her feet, stunned; the Punt Pole jabs, and at full Momentum sweeps all round him; Bog Hands hold and rot, and what dies in a hand's grip rises; Grave Pennies finish an ordinary enemy under 15% HP; King Tide is two beats on a clock, an ebb that pulls in and a flood that knocks back (no Cooldown or Projectile numbers, so nothing touches its clock).
- **The track** (`sim/trades.js sigUpgrade`, `tradeDeed`, `payDeeds`, `trackStep`, `trackState`; each trade's params): one rolled upgrade (`rollUpgrade` on `world.rng.trade`, then `giveUpgrade`) from the run's clock every `clock` (180 s), and one for every `per` trade deeds, but no closer than `gap` (40 s) to the last trade upgrade and at most 2 in any `span` (180 s; ruling 19 KH). Deeds the wait holds back are not lost: each upgrade's worth is `pastCap` (+4%) signature damage, up to `pastMax` (+40%), shown on Stats. `lvEvery` is 0 (no upgrades from the run's level-ups since ruling 18), and the per-minute growth (`grow`) is 0. The axes open on the track: its 5th and 10th upgrades raise the choice (`sim/ascend.js offerAxis`, halt `why: 'track'`); the capstone axis opens when the character's own deed is done (`trade.deed`, `capDeed`; `deedText` says it). `EV.SIG_UP` (89: b 0 clock, 1 trade, 2 level-up). `trackState` feeds the HUD meter's "next level" count. The interim clock C1 kept before C2 landed (`signatureClock`, `tuning.weaponUps.sigEvery`) is off (`sigEvery: 0`).
- **Trades** (`sim/trades.js`, with each character's hook in `sim/hooks-characters.js`, joined to the registry by `sim/hooks.js`): each fires on its own, from where they walk and face, what they are near, a meter, or a clock. Never the dash (ruling 16).
  - Lamplighter (`lamps`): lamps light at a touch and her lamps' light reaches twice as far; lighting a lamp fills her spare flames and Fuel and leaves a lantern by it; while her light is dimmed she sets a lantern down every `darkEvery` (30 s); a lantern lasts 60 s, burns at 60% of Lamplight's damage, and gives Lamplight +10% Damage while it stands (`lanternUp`, C2b). Every lamp lit banks 2 Lantern Nights, hers falling at 10 enemies. Spare flames come from 5 or more enemies in her light, one every 5 s, up to 3, each +12% Area and +15% Damage to Lamplight. Standing still to plant a lantern is the Great Lamp skill's build only (ruling 19 KJ).
  - Harpooner (`hook`): every 6 s her hook flies the way she faces, at the nearest elite, chest, Reliquary, the vault, an Ember, or a mooring post (which calls the Ferryman); an elite is pulled in, stunned 2 s, and Hooked (25% more damage taken for 4 s: `tuning.status.gaff`). On the Marsh it reaches twice as far for an elite.
  - Punter (`pole`): water is ground to him (`poleMove`, his own grid) for 5 s at a time, then a rest (`puntStep`, §5.23); Momentum (`world.trade.s.mo`) fills over straight runs, a sharp turn costs a step, a hit or a stop empties it; each step adds Damage and Punt Pole reach (`reachPerStep`, C2b); a floating chest comes out on the water for him.
  - Bog Warden (`raise`): walking over an enemy within 5 s of its death raises it as a thrall; the map's bog bodies and wreck crews rise at a touch; each thrall gives 1 Armor, up to 6. Her dead have their own row in the results (`result.thralls`, `thrallDamage`) and count in Bog Hands' share.
  - Drowned Man (`cargo`): a penny that finishes close to him pays into his purse; a full purse marks a sunken crate nearby (one mark every 60 s at most); walking to it brings it up. Every 3rd crate is a sea chest: the first gives back 1 of his 2 missing slots with a weapon in it, later ones a Grave Pennies level.
  - Tide Priest (`bells`): 12 enemies close in the 5 s before a beat call it early with +30% Damage; her flood rots what it hits; Fog Bells ring twice for her; she can't heal (`noHeal`: no food drops, no heal reaches her), and each flood gives her a shield.
- **The skill trees** (ruling 15; `data/skills.js`, 13 a character, 78 in all; `meta/skills.js`; the screen `ui/skillscreen.js` at the Tavern, which opens it at Lv 2): a skill is `{ id, character, row, name, desc, p, rival?, needs?, unlock? }`. Three fork rows: taking a skill closes its `rival` (the builds row's fork closes the rival's follow-up too); `needs` names an earlier skill; two skills a tree open by orders. Points come at mastery ranks 1, 3, 5, 7, 9, 10, and 11 (`SKILL_RANKS`; the ranks at `META.masteryRanks`), so a full tree holds 7 of 13. A reset at the Tavern costs 150 Coin and 8 Rum (8 Marsh Herbs for the Punter and the Bog Warden) a point spent (`SKILL_RESET`, `resetCost`). `runSkills` sends the tree as it stands into `RunConfig.skills`; a hook reads `world.sk.<id>` (the skill's numbers). The gate's Character row lists the skills in play. Skills change the signature, the trade, or the drawback, never the dash and never a plain percent. The mastery perks are gone: a save's trained perks are paid back once and their points are there to spend (`meta/skills.js retirePerks`, called from `meta/retired.js`, marked `profile.perksRetired`).
- **The ledger** (ruling 14 item 12, ruling 19 PF): `profile.characters[id].ledger` keeps clears by map and Tide, the best clear's time, and the trade's best counts (`meta/apply.js`, change kind `ledger`); the results say one line per character ("Lamplighter: 4 lamps lit (best 9)"), and the gate's strip shows the ledger.
- **What the player sees** (C3): the HUD's signature icon first and framed, a 2 px deed bar under each weapon counting toward its capstone, and the character's own meter under the passives (King Tide's clock, Momentum, Fuel and spare flames, the purse, thralls, the hook: `render/hudmodel.js updateTrade`, `HUD_LAYOUT.trade`); events 87 to 95 (`TIDE`, `MOMENTUM`, `SIG_UP`, `LANTERN`, `DIVE`, `OIL`, `CALL_DEAD`, `HAUL`, `SKILL`) carry their sounds and rumble; a world tip the first time each trade comes up (`app/runtips.js`); the gate's character card is three short lines (does, Drawback, Dash) with a Details page (`ui/hubscreens.js characterDetailsScreen`).
- **Balance tests:** `tests/sig-balance.test.js` reads `tests/fixtures/sig-sets.json` (no whole option set over 2.5x its signature's median set) and `sig-bshare.json` (option B on every axis still holds 12% or more of the character's damage), each stamped with the options it was benched on, so a changed option fails the test until the bench is rerun (`work/cw/C2/tools/sigsets.mjs`, then `sigfixture.mjs`; neither is in the repo).

### 5.23 The sweep's sim seams (0.9.0, X4; work/sweep/S1.md, work/sweep/X4.md)
- **The Punter's punt has an end** (`sim/trades.js puntStep`, `poleMove`, `poleSpeed`; numbers in his `trade.params`: `punt` 5 s, `rest` 3 s, `tired` 1.5 s, `winded` 0.7). Afloat, the pole tires. Back on land before it's spent, it rests as he walks and he pushes off again with what's left. Spent on the water, the water carries him to the nearest ground a walker can stand on (no steering; a dash still goes), and until the pole is fresh he is beached: 70% speed, and water is a wall to him. `EV.TRADE` a 9 (b 1 tiring, 0 spent, 2 fresh). A punt cache floats no farther from a bank than a third of a punt. On the Marsh rule 8 holds his chain while he stands on land; on the water it holds nothing for him. State is numbers in his hook's state (hashed).
- **Overtime's flood leaves a way to everything armed** (`sim/marsh.js collectSites`, `pass`). In overtime only (the water never goes out), at most 4 times a second, the tiles of every unlit lamp, bell not rung, ready cairn, unused trade site, find not found, the Ferryman's mooring while he's tied up (every mooring for the Harpooner), the open Well, and every chest, Reliquary, and vault on the ground are collected; when they change (`siteSig`, hashed) the pass walks each one's chain to the landing beside yours and holds them all. The chains share the search's one tree, so yours meets each. The Drowned Causeway keeps its own rule. An ordinary high water still cuts the map.
- **Act 3 keeps dry, always** (`sim/acts.js drawSites`, `sim/marsh.js floodsEver`): a site floods if its tile is under overtime's highest level (`flatsLevel` at every flat: the flood stops at its `keepDry` share), not only if it isn't peat. Act 3 never takes a flooding site, in any pass; acts 1 and 2 leave act 3 as many dry sites as it asks for while they have others. The Marsh has 4 bell sites on flats the flood never reaches.
- **The dawn** (`sim/acts.js crumbleAll`): every enemy crumbles, bosses and anything in the air included (boss records closed with no kill), latched leeches let go, a lure ends, hostile shots, pools, and warned strikes go. From the dawn to the run's end nothing hurts you (`isInvulnerable` reads `director.dawn`). A run that ended on the tick the last boss fell is not a dawn, and a result never says dawn beside a death.
- **The threat counts your weapons** (`world.threat.dealt`, added in `damageEnemyR`): slots 0 to 8 only, and only the health a hit took. `tools/bots/threat.mjs` prints the median curve over N seeds as a `ref` line to paste.
- **The Brine Vat** never salts a muster's captain (a champion), as it never salts a boss.
- **Minor:** the causeway is sighted only while its secret is in the run; the ledger counts a bell once (`t.uses`), the result's `bellsRung` every ring; a dev jump past a boss begins its act and the last boss passed arrives (`director.js skipEventsTo`); a crane or cairn at work when an act turns finishes first, then is ready (`acts.js workStep`).

### 5.24 The dash and the Dash slot (0.9.0, ruling 16; Kevin, Nov 16: "the dash button... is meant to be an escape move not linked to an ability")
Checked against the code Nov 20.
- **The dash does one thing: the dash.** No trade, signature, skill, Form option, Legendary, line, relic, gear affix, or run tool fires anything from it, on its start, path, or landing. `HOOK_POINTS` (`sim/hooks.js`) has no `onDash`. The only other things a dash does are the enemy's rule that it throws off latched leeches and its item's mark. Every piece that once fired from a dash now reads walking (`p.moveT`, seconds moving without a stop), standing still (`p.stillT`), or its own clock (`slot.fs.cueAt`, `p.ruleAt`), never a kill rate (the list is in `work/cw/C1-CONTRACT.md` §15). `tests/content.test.js` fails data that fires from the dash or a hook that reads it.
- **The Dash slot** (`data/dashes.js`, 8 items; `sim/dash.js`; the draft's part in `sim/draft.js`): a run holds one dash item. An item changes only how the dash works: `{ id, name, desc, icon, character?, dist?, time?, cooldown?, charges?, guard?, mark, numbers, weight, unlock }`, where `dist`, `time`, and `cooldown` multiply `tuning.player.dash` (distance 52, time 0.16 s, cooldown 2.5 s), `guard` is the seconds you can't be hurt after it ends, and `mark` is null, a slow, or a knock on its path or where it ends (never damage). Each character starts with their own (§5.22); Spare Oar and Light Step are drafted only.
- **Recharge is time only** (`dashCooldown`, `rechargeDash`, `spendDash`): a charge comes back every item cooldown x the `dashCooldown` stat, never under `tuning.dashes.minCooldown` (1.2 s). Nothing a kill, a hit, or a pickup does gives one back. Only the item and its cards change the recharge now (the dash-cooldown stats came off passives, relics, and gear).
- **In the draft** (from level `tuning.dashes.fromLevel`, 4): another item is offered like a new weapon (weight `newWeight` 12); taking it replaces the held one, fresh with full charges and no cards (`setDash`; `result.dash.swaps`). A card for the held item (weight `upWeight` 18) is one of its `numbers`: Dash Distance +15% (to 1.6x the item's own), Dash Recharge -12% (to 0.6x), Dash Charges +1 (to 3), at most 4 cards an item (`rollDashUp`, `takeDashUp`; `ui/describe.js dashUpText`). Another item is sure to be offered on the drafts of levels 6 and 16 (`draft.dashAt`). Banish works on both (`dash:<id>`, `dash:<id>:<up>`).
- `world.dash = { def, index, ups, stats, list }`; the player's `dashCharges` and `dashCd`; all of it, with `moveT`, `stillT`, and `ruleAt`, in the world hash. `dashGround` lays the item's mark each tick after the player moves. The run result's `dash` is `{ id, ups, swaps }`.

---

## 6. Rendering (`src/render/`)

### 6.1 Resolution and scaling
- The world renders into an offscreen **640×360** canvas with nearest-neighbor filtering, then scales up by a whole number: ×3 at 1080p, ×4 at 1440p, and ×6 at 4K. All three are exact. Odd resolutions letterbox with fog-colored bars. Settings offer an optional "fill screen" mode that isn't pixel-perfect.
- Everything in the world, including rotated projectiles, is pixel-correct at that resolution. There's no mixing of pixel sizes.
- **Smooth camera:** the offscreen canvas has a 1 px margin (642×362), and the final upscale is shifted by the camera's sub-pixel remainder. Slow camera moves don't stair-step on high-refresh monitors.

### 6.2 Layers (bottom to top)
1. Ground: the tiles in view (about 1,100) in one ParticleContainer, rebuilt only when the camera crosses a tile. (Phase 0 planned pre-baked chunk textures; the tile layer measured cheaper and needs no texture memory per chunk. Phase 3 change, §20 call 30.)
2. Decals: scorch marks and splashes (pooled, fading)
3. Blob shadows: one ParticleContainer
4. Gems
5. Enemies: one ParticleContainer, no y-sorting among themselves (overlapping swarms read fine in this genre). A v8 ParticleContainer holds lightweight Particles that must share **one texture source**, so every enemy frame, flash silhouette, outline, and ramp variant lives on one atlas page (up to 4096×4096, which holds about 65,000 sprites at 16 px). The same rule applies to the gem and projectile container and the effects container. Phase 2 prototypes one container with tint and a per-frame texture swap before building on it.
6. Projectiles
7. Elites and bosses (boss rigs), y-sorted against each other
8. Effects: sparks, embers, death puffs (one ParticleContainer, additive blend, 4,096 pooled particles)
9. **Lighting:** a light map at half resolution (321×181, covering the 1 px margin) with smooth filtering, cleared to the map's ambient light. Lights (the player's lamp, fire and light shots, burning ground, hazard pools, chests, explosions, gem glow; at most `feel.light.maxLights`) draw additively as soft radial sprites from one glow texture. The light map multiplies over the world. The result is soft light over crisp sprites, and the cost is almost nothing at this resolution.
10. **Fog:** drifting tileable noise (a generated texture, anchored to the world) drawn into a second half-resolution texture, with the same lights then *erased* out of it, so its density drops wherever there is light. **Light literally pushes the fog back** in runs, which is the theme. v1 needs no custom shader. In the hub, a fog mask texture driven by restoration state does the same thing.
11. **Warnings** (boss attack shapes, charger and gunner lines, hazard pools filling in) are drawn after the light and fog, unlit, so a hazard is never lost in the dark (§20 call 35).
11b. **The player**, drawn over all of that, unlit (her lamp lights her spot anyway), so she is never lost under a stack of warning shapes or in the fog (§20 call 46; found in the Phase 4 screenshot review, where ten crab charge lines at 10:00 hid her completely).
12. World text: damage numbers built from digit sprites in the atlas, grouped per enemy over `feel.numbers.groupMs`, capped at 150 on screen, and switchable off.
13. HUD: drawn at 640×360 in its own layer, so it never jitters with the camera. It shows HP (and shield), the dash's charges and recharge, the XP bar, the timer, level, kills, weapon and passive icons with levels (the signature first and framed; a pip for each Form; a 2 px bar under a weapon counting toward its Final Form), the character's own meter and its label under the passives (Fuel and spare flames, the hook, Momentum, thralls, the purse, the tide's clock, with its signature's next level: §5.22), the minimap, the boss's health (two bars side by side when two bosses are up, the Long Night), the map's row (lamps lit, bells rung), overtime, and short notices. Materials found are on the pause menu and Stats, not the HUD (they drew the eye from the fight for a number that only matters after the run). Its text is a code-drawn 5×7 pixel font in the atlas, at the world's own pixel size. Where each piece sits is data in `render/hudmodel.js` (`HUD_LAYOUT`, `placeHud`); the view test places every piece at its longest text (every weapon and passive slot, the longest boss name and notice), at every minimap setting, and fails if any two overlap or one leaves the screen.
    - **The minimap** (`render/minimap.js`, its model `render/minimapmodel.js`), part of the HUD: top right under the kills. The ground is a texture a pixel a tile, filled in as you see it: a tile shows once it's been on screen (`revealView`, from the run view's camera, which is kept inside the map), so the map is yours to uncover and the wreck stays a secret until you've been near. It keeps uncovering with the minimap off, and newly seen ground goes to the GPU at most every 150 ms. It fades to a quarter while you stand under it (a corner of the map pins the camera, and you can end up beneath it), and its marks are clipped to its box. Marks, each its own shape (never told apart by color alone), drawn materials first, then the other drops, elites and bosses, and you on top (your slot is always kept): you a ring with a dot, a boss a filled diamond and an elite a hollow one (red), a chest a hollow box wider than tall (amber), an Ember a big plus with a dark middle (violet), food a small plus (green), a magnet a U (blue), a material a 2 px dot (rare ones brighter) (`MARK_SHAPES`); never the experience gems or ordinary enemies. Settings: Minimap Nearby, Whole map, or Off; size Small (72 px) or Large (120 px). Nearby is whole pixels a tile (Small 1, 72 tiles across, about two screens; Large 2, 60 tiles), its corner on a whole pixel, so it never shimmers as it slides; the whole map is smaller than a pixel a tile and is smoothed.

### 6.3 Feel toolkit
Every value below lives in `data/tuning.js` (the render-only ones under `feel`, which the sim never reads) and appears in the tuning panel.
- **Hit flash:** the atlas builder generates a white-silhouette version of every enemy frame, and the renderer swaps to it for a few frames. It stays in the same atlas, so batching isn't broken.
- **Knockback:** visible displacement, from §5.6.
- **Hit-stop:** from §5.1, per event type.
- **No screen shake** (removed in 0.7.1: Kevin, "it's making me sick to my stomach"). Hits, kills, and slams read through light, sparks, scorch marks, flashes, and hit-stop; the camera only follows the player.
- **XP vacuum:** gems accelerate toward the player on an easing curve, with a sparkle trail and a rising pitch when many are collected in a row.
- **Death:** a pop plus particles plus a short scale-down. Big kills get extra.
- **Squash and stretch** on hit and on spawn.
- **Chest reel:** a slot-machine animation with sound and light.
- **Level-up:** the sim freezes, a light burst plays, and the cards appear. Inputs are ignored for 300 ms, so a dash press can't accidentally pick a card.
- **Warning pulse:** an enemy about to act (a charger winding up, a gunner aiming) pulses warm and swells, on top of its warning line.

### 6.4 Procedural animation
Free packs are often single-frame. Enemies get code-driven life: a bob (vertical sine with a random phase per enemy), a lean in the movement direction, a horizontal flip on direction change, squash on hit, a telegraph pulse (scale plus tint), and spawn and death tweens. Multi-frame animation is supported: an entry with `frames` in the manifest becomes `<id>`, `<id>@1`, ... in the atlas (with flash and elite versions of each), the table lists it under `anims`, and the run view steps each enemy through its frames at the entry's `fps`, starting from its own phase so a crowd never flaps in step (the gull's wingbeat, the crab's scuttle); a frozen or dazed enemy holds its frame. The player hops a pixel every other step while walking (`feel.bob.stepsPerSec`). Bosses move by their rigs (§5.13).

These motions run on **sim time** (the world's tick), not the render clock, so the actors stand still whenever the sim does: under a level-up, a chest, the pause menu, and a hit-stop. That covers the bob, the frames, the hop, the rigs, the daze wobble, the warning pulse, and the status flickers; a frozen or dazed enemy stops on one frame (its own, by its phase). The feel effects that should carry on through a hit-stop stay on the render clock: hit squash, particles, and the fog's drift. The pieces are pure functions in `render/viewstate.js` (`animFrameIndex`, `stepHop`, `dazeWobble`, `simSeconds`), tested in Node, and a source check keeps the run view calling them with each enemy's own phase. Status tints show what's happening to an enemy: the warning pulse, then frozen, burning, slowed, rotting. A dazed enemy isn't tinted (dimming it would break its contrast, §16.4); it reels instead, a quick sway on its own phase.

---

## 7. Input and controls (`src/input/`)

- Actions are abstract (`move`, `dash`, `confirm`, `back`, `reroll`, `banish`, `skip`, `pause`, `tuning`, `debug`), and bindings map devices to actions.
- The Gamepad API is polled once per frame. Movement is sampled once per sim tick into an **InputFrame** (move x and y quantized to 8-bit signed integers, plus a button bitmask). That is what gets recorded for replays.
- The left stick uses a radial deadzone (tunable, 0.2 by default). The d-pad also moves.
- **Controller type:** a gamepad ID containing Sony's vendor ID `054c` or "DualSense" shows PlayStation glyphs; anything else shows Xbox glyphs. The glyphs are drawn in code. Steam Input can disguise a DualSense as an Xbox pad, so Settings has a "Button prompts" override (Auto, PlayStation, Xbox, Keyboard).
- A Sony pad whose `mapping` isn't `"standard"` (a DualShock 4 or DualSense over DirectInput) is read through a remap table, hat switch included. Any other pad is read as if standard. On Windows, Chromium and Electron report every XInput pad as standard, so that case is rare.
- Disconnecting the controller in use pauses the game, even with a spare pad still plugged in; reconnecting it, or using the keyboard, the mouse, or the other pad, resumes. Pulling out a pad nobody was using changes nothing. Losing window focus pauses the game. (`src/app/pauses.js`, tested with the real input module and loop.)

| Action | DualSense | Xbox | Keyboard |
|---|---|---|---|
| Move | Left stick or d-pad | Left stick or d-pad | WASD or arrows |
| Dash | R1, or ✕ during a run | RB, or A during a run | Shift, or Space during a run |
| Confirm | ✕ | A | Enter |
| Back | ○ | B | Esc |
| Reroll (level-up) | □ | X | R |
| Banish (level-up) | △, then pick a card | Y, then pick a card | B, then pick |
| Skip (level-up) | Hold L1 for 0.5 s | Hold LB | Hold Tab |
| Pause | Options | Menu | Esc or P |
| Tuning panel | Hold Create for 1 s | Hold View for 1 s | F1 |
| Debug overlay | none | none | F3 |

In the harbor, ✕, A, and Space are confirm only (walking up to a door and pressing it must not also dash you away from it); the dash there is R1, RB, or Shift (`sampleFrame({ dashOnly: true })`).

Remapping (Settings, Controls; `input/bindings.js rebind`): Confirm and Back always swap with each other (B given to Confirm makes A Back; Dash on Confirm follows Confirm), and a change that would leave them sharing a button is refused with a line saying why. Opening or closing a screen releases every held button except the movement keys, so walking held through a level-up goes on after it (their menu directions still wait for a let-go). The window's close question pauses the loop under its own reason, `close`.

Skip is a hold so it can't happen by accident. The tuning panel is a hold on one button (Create, or View on Xbox), not a two-button chord, so it can never also fire Pause (changed in Phase 1; see §20).

---

## 8. UI (`src/ui/`)

- **The world and the HUD are Pixi. Menus are DOM overlays** above the canvas: level-up cards, pause, results, hub building panels, research tree, codex, bestiary, settings, and the dev panel. DOM makes text-heavy screens far faster to build and keeps text crisp at native resolution. Atlas icons appear in the DOM through CSS with `image-rendering: pixelated`.
- **Controller navigation framework** (built once in Phase 3, because the feel gate is played on a DualSense and needs working level-up, chest, pause, and tuning screens; every later screen uses it): every interactive element registers with a focus system. Directional input moves focus to the nearest element in that direction, with explicit overrides where layout needs them. Focus is always visible (a thick, high-contrast frame). Back always backs out. The mouse also works, and hovering moves focus.
- Menus are sized in game pixels (a CSS variable `--u` is one pixel of the 640×360 view), so they scale with the game and stay aligned to it. The screens (`ui/menus.js` for the run, `ui/hubscreens.js` for the harbor): title (with Credits), level-up (reroll, banish mode, hold-to-skip with a fill, G for the card's hardest glossary term), the chest's reel and the Boss chest screen (reel, then an Ember and its Forms, on one screen), the Form choice (one weapon at a time: its three axes, each option with its Drawback; Keep for later), a Reliquary's, the vault's, and the Ferryman's picks, the Fog Bell's pick, the Salt Well, the overtime choice, pause (the build, Forms when an Ember can be spent, Stats, the Codex for the run's map, This map, Save bug report), Stats (the character's numbers, the signature's growth line, each weapon's numbers with its DPS and Total Damage, its Forms, lines, and Legendaries, the passives and relics), the run's end (the death recap, the damage table by weapon), settings, the glossary (`ui/glossary.js`), confirmations, the error screen, and the tuning panel. In the harbor: building panels, the gate (map, then character: three short lines a card, R or Y for the Details page; then the setup rows), the results, the Harbor Orders board, the codex and each weapon's page (`ui/weaponpage.js`), a character's skill tree (`ui/skillscreen.js`), and the harbor's menu. A first run shows How to play in the corner for its first seconds. Menu sounds come from `data/sfx.js`.
- **The Fog Bell's pick** (0.8.2, the `bellpick` halt): laid out as the other picks (a Reliquary's): a card for each weapon and passive it offers, with `Lv 3 to 5` and what those levels add, four to a row; Confirm takes one, Back stays on the cards. The Reliquary's and vault's screens say `Relics: 3 of 5 found`, and the vault's that its relics are one rarity higher. The Ferryman's screen leaves off a relic found since he stocked it.
- **Visual rules** (Kevin's standing UI preferences apply here): dark panels, 1 px pixel borders, square corners, colors only from the game palette (`data/palette.js`; a test checks `ui.css` against it), one bundled pixel font (Jersey 10, SIL Open Font License, in `src/ui/fonts/`), and plain labels, worded as `docs/WRITING.md` says (the genre's stat lines and labels; `tests/voice.test.js` checks it). The title of a screen is the name of the thing. No cream or paper backgrounds, no serif type, no small-caps labels, no rounded pill buttons, and no gradient buttons. This game must not share a look with Kevin's other apps.
- **Panels give way, never their title or prompts** (0.9.0, X6; work/sweep/X6.md): every `.panel` is a flex column. Its parts keep their height; a banded list (`ui/hints.js bandList`), a scroll box (`scrollBox`: text with no buttons in it, one focus stop), or a part marked `.give` takes what is left and scrolls inside itself, its bands saying "N more". A new screen marks the one part that may give way. Up and Down on a focused element first scroll what it sits in (`scrollStep`: a scroll box a step at a time, a row taller than its list to its end, the rest of a list from its first or last row), then move the focus. `tools/shots/hint-check.mjs` opens 23 screens at 100, 125, and 150% text at 1280x720 and fails when a title or hint row leaves the view.
- A screen's `onBack` may return `false` for "Back does nothing here" (a pick that only keeps the focus on its cards); then the same press can still pause (Esc is Back and Pause on the keyboard). The keyboard's Pause prompt names a key that only pauses (P).
- Accessibility toggles: damage numbers, flash reduction (caps full-screen flashes), and telegraphs that use shape as well as color.

---

## 9. Audio (`src/audio/`)

- Sound effects are ZzFX parameter arrays in `data/sfx.js`, rendered once at boot into audio buffers, so playback is cheap. The generator is ZzFX's own sample function, vendored into `audio/zzfx.js` with its MIT notice (the npm package opens an AudioContext when imported, which the game doesn't want). Weapons name their fire and hit sounds in data.
- **Voice limiting:** each sound has a maximum number of simultaneous voices (hits: 4 with pitch variation; the gem pickup: 1, with pitch rising through combos) and a global cap of 24. Without this, 1,000 enemies make white noise.
- Three volume buses: master, music, and SFX. Music is optional, must be free-licensed, and gets credited.
- **Sounds by kind** (0.8.2, §5.18): each of the map's things has its own (`data/sfx.js`): a lamp catching and going out, the bell's toll, its count (played higher as the kills left fall, up to an octave: `app/runlogic.js pitchFor`) and its ring, a Reliquary's chime, the vault rising, two knocks for a chest that lands out of sight, a chime for a drop a lit lamp watched, the Ferryman's oar, Lantern Night banked and falling, and a Pearl. None borrows the level-up, evolve, or gem sound any more.
- **Rumble** (0.8.2, and the fight's own in 0.9.0; `render/rumble.js`, which `app/rumble.js` re-exports; the shakes in `data/sfx.js RUMBLE`): a short shake of the controller in use (the Gamepad API's `vibrationActuator`, `dual-rumble`) for the map's things (a Reliquary, the vault, the Ferryman, a lamp lit, a bell rung, Lantern Night), for the fight (a hit taken, scaled by it; a low-health pulse, never while the run is stopped; a boss attack landing; an elite or boss kill; a level-up; a Form or a Legendary taken), and for the characters' moments (a deed done, a sea chest, an elite yanked, a called tide). One shake a frame, the strongest asked for. On by default at half strength; Settings has `Rumble` and `Rumble strength` (`controls.rumble`, `controls.rumbleStrength`). The controller only: there is no screen shake (§6.3).
- **Music is made in code** (ruling 8; `audio/music.js`, its loops in `data/music.js`): a small step sequencer with three loops (harbor, run, boss) and an ambience bed (the sea's wash, the wind), on the music and ambience buses. `audio/cues.js` (pure) says which loop plays, each weapon's own fire sound (`data/sfx.js WEAPON_FIRE`), and the growth and character sounds (events 81 to 95, each its own).
- In Electron, autoplay restrictions are turned off. On the web, audio starts on the first input.

---

## 10. Meta-progression and the hub (`src/meta/`, hub scene)

### 10.1 The profile
The profile is the save's core (schema v4 since 0.8.0, §12.2):
- material counts
- building levels
- research (completed, and active with runs remaining; how many can run at once comes from the Library's level)
- the Lamp Post's upgrade ranks
- characters (unlocked, mastery XP, skills taken, the ledger: §5.22)
- gear (the items held, what's worn in each slot, the next item's number, and a count of re-rolls)
- run tools held (`consumables`; rune stones are gone, paid back by `meta/retired.js`)
- kill counts per enemy (the codex shows them; the Hunts read them)
- codex discoveries
- Harbor Orders done and found (`feats`, `featsFound`; challenge runs are orders)
- Tides opened on each map and the one last chosen (v4), and the gate's other last choices (the map among them)
- relics given to the Salt Well, and what each map has shown (first sightings) (v4)
- what a retired system left (`retired`, `perksRetired`, `retiredNote`, `kept`, `keptThrough`, `heirloomHeld`, `cursesHeld`: §10.9, §12.5)
- prestige cycle and prestige relics
- lifetime stats
- dialogue and first-time flags

Everything that can be *derived* is derived, never stored (`src/meta/state.js`, `next.js`):
- `resolveContent(profile)`: what's in the weapon, passive, relic, and dash pools, which characters are available, and what the harbor can make or sell (gear recipes, run tools). One source of truth: each content item's `unlock` says where it comes from ('starter', 'research:<id>', 'building:<id>:<level>', 'secret:<id>', 'feat:<id>', 'challenge:<id>', 'boss:<id>', 'map:<id>', 'mastery:<character>:<rank>', or 'innate', never in a pool), and the content test checks every key resolves
- `researchSlots`, `researchTier`, `treeTier`: what the Library's and the Lamp Post's levels open
- `nextAffordable(profile, 3)`: the three purchases closest to affordable, across building levels, research, upgrade ranks, and Phase 7's goals (gear not made yet, a worn item's next tier, a run tool none of is held; re-rolls, bought again and again, are left out)
- `buildingStage(level)`: which visual stage to draw
- `revealedFog`, `hubPeople`, `shownProps`, `npcLine`: what the harbor shows for a profile (the fog's cleared regions, who is out and where, the props, what a keeper says)
- `completion(profile)`: the completion percentage on the title screen (building levels, upgrade ranks, research, the codex, feats, challenge runs, and Tides)
- `masteryOf`, `slotsFor`, `tideAvailable`, `cursesAvailable`, `heirloomOpen`, `heldOf`, `contentOpen`, and what each building level opens (`levelEffect`)

A save from another build is fitted on load (`meta/reconcile.js`): anything it names that this build doesn't know moves to `orphans` (kept, never deleted, restored if the content comes back), and counts out of range are brought back in.

### 10.2 Run lifecycle
1. Run setup at the gate (`app/setup.js setupRows`; six rows since X2, §10.9): the map (with the short run), the character (and the skills in play), the starting weapon (a kit), the Heirloom, run tools, and the difficulty (the Tide and the curses), each row shown once the harbor opens it; worn gear, the skill tree, and Tempering come along. Challenge runs start from the Harbor Orders board, not the gate
2. `RunConfig` goes to the sim
3. `RunResult` comes back
4. `applyRunResult(profile, result)` returns the new profile plus a **change list**: materials gained, research advanced or finished (with what it unlocked), a note when projects are running but the run didn't count, new codex entries, bestiary milestones, a new longest run, and Phase 7's: Pearls sold, a mastery rank, the next Tide opened, orders done or found, the character's ledger, and what a retired system paid back. It runs the moment the run ends, and the profile is saved then, so closing the game on the end screen loses nothing
5. The run's end screen (the run's numbers), then **Return to harbor** (or **Run again**, straight into the next run)
6. The results screen ("Results"), over the harbor: how the run ended, what came home, the news, and the next three things you can nearly afford; **Start run** goes straight to the gate. After Run again, every run since the harbor reads as one visit: the materials add up, each finished project and milestone is said once, and the heading says how many runs it was (`app/hublogic.js mergeRuns`)
7. The harbor

`applyRunResult` is a pure function with heavy test coverage.

- **Materials on death:** the player keeps everything collected. Dying costs time, not progress, which fits the brief's "does this make Kevin want to do one more run?" test. (Builder's call; see §20.)
- **Research progress:** each finished run advances active research by one run, if the run **reached 5:00 or killed a boss**. That blocks instant-quit farming without punishing a real attempt. (Builder's call.)

### 10.3 Materials and the Graveyard Keeper loop
- Every map drops its own materials (from the brief's table). Elites and bosses drop the rare ones. The greed stat multiplies drops. Since 0.8.1 an ordinary enemy's chances are times the pace dial `tuning.pace.drops` (3.5), set so a minute brings home about what it did with three or four times the enemies (§5.6); elites' and bosses' tables are as they were.
- Recipes deliberately mix materials from different maps, so a goal in one system points at a run somewhere else.
- **Content test:** every material has at least one source, and every cost is obtainable using only content unlocked before it's needed. A dead-end economy fails the build. A later map's materials (the Salt Marsh's, the Sunken Chapel's) are in the data before their map is, with no source yet: the test holds every built map's drops to a use, every cost to a built map's drops, and the MVP harbor to map 1's alone; a later map's materials join the check when their map is built.
- **The pacing simulator** (`tools/pacing/`) uses material yields measured by the bots to simulate hundreds of runs with a greedy buyer, and reports hours to each milestone. Starting targets, all for Kevin to adjust:

| Milestone | Target |
|---|---|
| First building level | End of run 1 |
| Something new in the hub | Every 1 to 2 runs early on, every 3 to 4 later |
| MVP hub fully restored (3 buildings × 5 levels) | 6 to 10 hours |
| Lighthouse lit (v1.0) | 60 to 80 hours |
| Unlocks still arriving | Past 100 hours, counting Tide 20, mastery, and prestige |

**How it measures (0.8.0, `tools/pacing/stages.mjs`, `measure.mjs`, `model.mjs`).** What a run brings home grows with the harbor (more weapons and relics in the pools, the Lamp Post's upgrades, the Ferryman, the Marsh's storm and Well), so the bots are measured per map at four **harbor stages**: a fresh profile, then a quarter, a half, and three quarters of the harbor, each a declared share filled evenly (every building at that share of its levels, the upgrades whose tiers are open at that share of their ranks, that share of the research, lowest tiers first). A stage is never bought, so it reads no cost. Past the fresh profile each map's bosses are killed and each map cleared once, so the Salt Marsh is open and measured with its storm and Well on (a profile has them after its first Marsh run, so its first one or two runs are slightly overstated); the secret is past every stage. That makes 7 sets a player (the Docks at stages 0 to 3, the Marsh at 1 to 3), 12 runs a set, for two players: the **practiced** one (bots that can't die, `yields-skilled.json`) and the **ordinary** one (bots that can, `yields.json`). Each run lasts until the bot dies, clears the map after the 20:00 boss, or reaches 25:00.

The model plays each simulated run with the yields of the stage nearest the player's own progress (building levels, research done, and upgrade ranks over all there are), scaled by the player's greed over the greed of the stage it was measured at. A player runs where a project under way points, else on the map whose own materials the next thing they want is shortest of, else on the newest map open. The buyer's order is building levels, then research, then upgrades, the cheapest first, with 1.5 minutes in the harbor per run. The simulated player doesn't buy gear or run tools.

The report says what its hours stand on before it gives them, and what its numbers are: each column's own median (so a row's two needn't be one player's) and the 10th to 90th percentile of the players' hours. Each milestone with a target says met or missed on the median, and one some player never reached is missed. "Something new" counts building levels and finished research, not upgrade ranks, pooled over the players; the early window is runs 1 to 10 and the later one from run 11 to each player's last new thing. "The MVP harbor" is the MVP buildings (`META.mvpBuildings`). It gives the share of runs on each map, the fresh profile's share of the run content, and a sensitivity line for the ordinary bots' shortest third of each set's runs. The report alone (`node tools/pacing/pacing.mjs`) runs on the committed yields in about 3 s; `--remeasure --jobs 2` measures all 168 runs (13 minutes at 0.8.1's pace), and `--stale` only the stale sets.

**The drift gate.** The yields are kept with a fingerprint per set of the measuring code and what it reads. When a set is stale the gate fails, naming each stale set ("salt_marsh stage 2") and the command to remeasure it. What can't change a measured run doesn't make the yields stale: a `sprite` or `icon` field, the palette and the sounds, a harbor cost (no stage reads one, so a cost refit leaves the yields fresh), or another map's data. The gate also checks that the sim's code never names those fields, which is what makes leaving them out safe.

**0.8.1's refit.** On the 0.8.1 yields, before the refit, the MVP harbor took 2.2 hours and a first cleared run bought 10 or 11 building levels and opened the Marsh. The economy had grown about four times in common materials and about twice in rares, so the costs were scaled to match rather than cutting what runs bring home: building levels 2 to 5, and the Docks' and the Tavern's level 1 (so run 1 can't open the Marsh), cost four times their commons and twice their Iron and Map Scraps; every upgrade rank and research tiers 2 to 4 the same; the Marsh's materials 14 times (Herbs) and 10 times (Salt Glass) their first placing, so a Marsh run brings home about as many Herbs as a Docks run does Timber. The Lamp Post's and the Library's level 1 and tier-1 research are as they were. Gear and consumables moved only in their Marsh costs (Herbs 3.5 times, Glass 2.5 times; a rune stone 3 Glass; gear tiers 4 and 5 add 5 and 10 Glass), so they, and the mastery perks, are about four times cheaper against a run's income than before (§20 calls 162 to 169). A first cleared run now buys the Lamp Post's first two levels and the Library's first, and in 8 of 12 runs the Library's second.

0.8.1's reading (168 runs on 0.8.1's code, every one of them a clear, the ordinary bots' included). **Stale:** the committed yields were measured before 0.9.0's changes; X3's reading on today's runs is in §10.10, and the release remeasure (`node tools/pacing/pacing.mjs --remeasure --jobs 2`, now three players) replaces both:

| Who | MVP harbor | Whole harbor |
|---|---|---|
| The practiced player (bots that can't die) | 7.4 h (6.7 to 8.9), met | 13.0 h (12.3 to 13.8) |
| The ordinary bots | 7.5 h (7.1 to 9.3), met | 13.0 h (12.3 to 13.4) |
| The ordinary bots, the shortest third of each set's runs | 7.8 h (7.1 to 10.4) | |

The first building level at run 1 (met), the Harpooner at run 1 (the Lamp Post's level 2), the first research at run 2, and the Salt Marsh open at run 4 (about 1.5 h). Something new every 1.0 runs over runs 1 to 10 (met), then every 1.2 to 1.3 runs, against the later target of 3 to 4 (missed, sooner): 72 things fit into about 35 runs, so that miss is structural until the content passes add more. Players spend 60% of their runs on the Docks and 40% on the Marsh. A fresh profile's pools hold 13 of 57 (23%, met). An average practiced run brings home, on the Docks, about 1,585 Coin, 807 Oil, 443 Timber, 425 Drowned Pages, and 21 Iron at stage 0, 1,440 Coin at stage 1 (with the Ferryman in the run, dry level-ups pay Sea Glass instead of Coin), and 2,000 to 2,200 Coin at stages 2 and 3; on the Marsh, 1,200 to 1,600 Coin, 590 to 770 Herbs, and 42 to 51 Salt Glass. The ordinary bots are within a few percent. With every bot clearing, nothing here measures a newer player any more: that would need bots that play worse (§20 call 169).

**Earlier readings, in short.** Phase 5: the MVP harbor in 7.1 h on bots that mostly died (17.7 h on the shortest third). Phase 7: 9.4 h, the whole harbor of nine about 13.5 h. 0.7.2 set the pace against a player who clears the map (Kevin cleared his first run and bought half the town): 8.4 h to the MVP harbor and 12.0 h to the whole harbor on the skilled bots' cleared runs, 17.0 h for the ordinary bots, with the early costs raised and `run.materialMul` at 1.2 (§20 call 89). A fresh profile's share was 42% (10 of 24) in Phase 5 and 34% (12 of 35) in Phase 7.

### 10.4 The hub scene (built in Phase 5)
- **The map** (`data/hub.js`, 60 x 36 tiles): the town between the forest and the sea, around the square where the one lamp still burns. Building plots with their doors, the ruined plots of the buildings to come (Phase 7, and the Lighthouse in Phase 10), the fog's regions and the milestone that clears each, the keepers' posts and walks, the waiting spots on the square, the pier and the boat's route, the gate, and the props that appear as the town comes back. The content test checks it all fits: every door and the gate reachable from the start and with every prop out, every walk and arrival path straight and open when it's used, props out of everyone's way, and at every mix of building levels each building and ruin either clear with clear ground all round it or in the fog with fog all round it (the fog's soft edge is a tile wide, so the tile of margin keeps it off the building: never half fogged).
- **Walking** (`hub/world.js`, pure): the run's movement and dash (distance and time), at your character's speed; only the dash's cooldown is the harbor's own (0.9 s). You walk as the Lamplighter (`hub.avatar`); the other characters you have wait on the square. The fog is a wall: you can't walk into a region that hasn't cleared. Props and the small lamps (the gate's, the pier's) are solid; a prop that appears where you stand moves you to the nearest open tile, and one that only reaches your edge pushes you clear of it at once.
- **What's in reach**: the nearest door, the gate, or person within 14 px. Confirm opens the building's panel (restore its next level, and the Lamp Post's upgrades, the Library's research, or who comes back to the Tavern), the gate's screen (choose who sets out), or a line in a speech bubble. In the harbor ✕ (A, Space, Enter) only confirms; the dash is on R1 (RB, Shift). The pause button opens the harbor's menu (set out from anywhere, settings, the title) and closes it again.
- **In a panel**, something you can't do yet (short of materials, the Library busy, a tier not open) still takes focus and a press: it shows dimmed with the reason under it, and pressing it says the reason with the menu's "no" sound. A purchase keeps your place: the same row stays focused and the list doesn't scroll.
- **Five visual stages per building**, after the ruin: 1 a scaffold goes up over the ruin, 2 the roof with the windows boarded, 3 lit windows, 4 the keeper at the door with a sign and a lantern by it, 5 fully alive (window boxes, chimney smoke, the keeper walking the streets). Each stage is the pack's building (Tiny Town tiles in a layout) with the stage done in code (§16): a ruin is the building looked down into with its roof gone.
- **Level-up ceremony**: the camera pans to the building (0.7 s), the new stage rises over the old from the ground up with dust along the line while the fog peels back from its streets (1.4 s and 2 s), the keeper steps out and the props appear, a banner names what the level brought, and the camera comes back. Confirm or back skips it; what it leaves behind lands at once. Set pieces queue: a level that brings a character plays its ceremony and then the arrival.
- **Arrivals by boat**: the camera finds the boat out in the fog, its stern lantern showing through; it sails the harbor's route and ties up with its bow at the pier's edge, the newcomer, standing in the bow, steps off from where she stood (the walk starts on the boat's deck, at the height she was drawn, so nothing jumps), walks up to the square, and waits there from then on, facing the square's middle (her waiting figure takes over on the tick she stops walking, so she never blinks out). While she's in the boat she is drawn over the fog with it, lit as the light map lights the spot she's in where the fog is clear and in the boat's shade where it's thick; stepping off, she's drawn over the boat as well as under the light map while she's in front of it (the hull drawn again over her feet, so its bow never covers her), and that figure fades out over her next few steps (`look.stepOffFade`), so her shade carries straight on. Every later arrival reuses it.
- **Over the harbor** (DOM, `ui/hubhud.js`): your materials (the ones the harbor asks for, top left), the **next three you can nearly afford** (bottom left, clear of the buildings up the hill and of the page's status notice) with how close each is and what's short, the prompt over what's in reach, speech bubbles, the banner, and how to skip a set piece. A corner panel fades while you walk under it.
- **The guide for a new player** (`meta/guide.js`, `ui/hubhud.js`): one goal across the top and a marker: a bobbing triangle where the target's prompt would show (over the gate's sign, the lamp, a door), or at the screen's edge, slid clear of the corner panels and the goal line. Off screen it follows the walk there (`hub/map.js routeTo`: where the route leaves the screen), so it never points through the fog. A target on screen but under a corner panel gets an arrow just past the panel, pointing at it. Once the target is in reach its prompt takes over. With no run yet: go to the gate at the bottom left of town. After a run, with the Lamp Post still a ruin: restore it, or, home with too little for it, go back out through the gate. Then it's gone (Next up says the rest). A first run also shows How to play in the corner for its first `feel.firstRunTipsSec` seconds (40: move, dash, weapons fire on their own, gems level you up, survive); it fades while you stand under it, as the minimap does.
- **Walking off the quay** at the gate (its row or the one below, `timing.walkOff` seconds, 0.15, pushing west against the map's edge) opens the gate's screen, as confirm at the gate does. It fires once a push: only a real frame with west let go arms it again, and while a screen is open the scene steps the harbor with no frame, so backing out of the gate with the stick still held west doesn't reopen it.
- **Night and fog** (`render/hubview.js`): a light map multiplies the town down to a blue dusk (lighter than 0.5.1's, since 0.6.0: Kevin wants a dingy town where what's cleared is easy to see); the town lamp's pool grows with each Lamp Post level; lit windows, door lanterns, the gate's lamp, and the pier's lamp glow; the fog over uncleared regions is thick, soft-edged (a texel a tile, scaled up smooth), ragged where it meets clear ground (a fog tile there thins and a clear one takes a little fog, each by its own hash, never on a building or the ring around it), and drifts. The fog is drawn whole (a fill at the look's thickness, the drifting wisps over it) and then erased by how clear each tile is, so a tile shows its density times the thickness, a region peeling back thins in step with its fade, and the ragged edge shows as tuned. A boat coming in is drawn over the fog and the light map: where the fog is clear it's lit as the light map lights the water at its middle (its own lantern and the pier's lamp with the rest, as its newcomer is, so she never reads brighter than her boat), and out in the fog it keeps its own shade (never brighter than the night's, darker the thicker the fog, read smoothly between tile middles as it's drawn); it rises and falls on the swell with its lantern. How it all looks is data (`HUB_TUNING.look`, on the harbor's tuning panel, §15): the lights and the fog, the flicker's two waves, the boat's halo and bob, the camera's lift, a prop's pop, and the dust and smoke. What each building shows during a ceremony, how its lights come up, the lamp's swell, the light map at a point, and an arrival's boat and newcomer are pure and tested (`render/hubtiles.js`), and the view itself runs in the gate on Pixi's own scene graph, its drawing checked part by part against them (`tests/hubview.test.js`). Everything that moves (you, the people, the camera, the boat, a newcomer walking up) is drawn between its last two ticks.
- NPCs speak short lines keyed to milestones (`data/npcs.js`): the newest line that applies.
- **MVP buildings: the Lamp Post, the Library, and the Tavern.** The Lamp Post opens the capped stat tree, one tier a level, and brings the Harpooner at level 2. The Library runs research, which is what makes materials point at the next thing. The Tavern keeps the Harbor Orders board (level 1), opens the skill trees (level 2) and the Heirloom (level 3). (Builder's call.)

### 10.5 Buildings, materials, and when each arrives

Starting material assignment (a builder's call; each content pass can adjust it):

| Source | Materials |
|---|---|
| Every map | Coin, Oil |
| The Drowned Docks (map 1) | Timber, Rope, Drowned Pages, Rum; Iron from elites (salvage) |
| The Salt Marsh | Marsh Herbs, Salt Glass |
| The Sunken Chapel | Bone, Candles |
| The Cliff Lighthouse and the Reef | Their own rare materials, set in their content passes |
| Elites and bosses everywhere | Map Scraps, plus each map's rare material |

With this assignment, **everything the three MVP buildings need drops on map 1**, so the MVP economy has no dead ends. With a single map, the MVP proves the brief's "materials point at the next thing to build". "Where to run next" arrives with map 2.

| Building | Built in | Restores with | Opens |
|---|---|---|---|
| Lamp Post | Phase 5 | Oil | Capped stat tree; the Harpooner (level 2); Wayside Lamps light faster (levels 3 and 5); a weapon slot at levels 3 and 5; it clears the fog from the six Phase 7 plots |
| Library | Phase 5 (basic), Phase 7 (full tree) | Drowned Pages | Research slots and tiers (tier 4 at level 5) |
| Tavern | Phase 5, Phase 7, 0.9.0 | Coin, Rum | The Harbor Orders board (level 1), skill trees (level 2; they replaced mastery perks), the Heirloom (level 3); some relics' unlocks |
| Docks | Phase 7 | Timber, Rope | The Ferryman in every run (§5.18): more relics in his stock and lower prices with its levels; a relic slot at levels 3, 4, and 5. Each map's boat joins its levels when that map is built |
| Smithy | Phase 7 | Iron, Timber, Rope | Gear crafting and tiers (up to 5), affix re-rolls (level 3), Tempering (level 4, where rune stones were: §5.21); the 5th and 6th gear slots at levels 3 and 5; a passive slot at levels 2 and 4; its first level waits on the Tavern's level 2 |
| Chapel | Phase 7 | Drowned Pages, Oil | Tides 1 to 10 (2 more a level), curses (levels 4 and 5), a wider Fog Bell ring (level 3); its first level waits on the Salt Widow killed |
| Market | Phase 7 | Coin | Trading at a deliberately bad rate (better at levels 3 and 5); buying leftover Pearls as Coin (levels 2 and 4) |
| Apothecary | Phase 7 | Rum, Oil | Run tools (eight: charges, a Revival, things in hand at 0:00), a slot at levels 1, 3, and 5; food heals more at 2 and 4; its first level waits on the Smithy's level 3 |
| Cartographer | Phase 7 | Map Scraps, Drowned Pages | Reliquaries on the minimap (1), small secrets (2), challenge runs posted on the board (3), short runs (4), big secrets and hints (5) |
| Lighthouse | 0.9.0 (10 stages), Phase 10 | Everything | A staged material sink (W5: lamps faster at 1, a run tool slot at 4, Market rares at 7, a wider bell ring at 8); the endgame and Prestige later |

Phase 7 builds every building's system thinly, which already meets the v0.5 column's 7 buildings. Content passes fill them in. With one map built, the six restore with map 1's materials (the content test holds every cost to a built map's drops); Salt Glass, Candles, Bone, and Marsh Herbs move into their costs as their maps arrive. The six stand in the fog until the Lamp Post clears it: the Docks from the start, the Chapel at the Lamp Post's level 3, the Smithy and the Apothecary at 4, the Market and the Cartographer at 5 (a building in the fog can't be restored: `The fog still covers it. Needs the Lamp Post at Lv 4`).

### 10.6 Details of the Phase 7 systems
Each building's purchases are pure functions in `src/meta/workshop.js` with a `whyNot...` twin the panel shows (§10.4: a refusal is a label), returning `{ doc, changes }` as `meta/actions.js`'s do; nothing there is random the way a drop is (what a new item rolls and what a re-roll offers come from a seed of the item and the profile's own counters, so a reload never rerolls an offer).
- **Research** (the Library): tiers 1 to 4, open weapons, passives, relics, gear recipes, capstones, and more of the Library's tree. Unchanged otherwise (§10.2).
- **Equipment** (the Smithy, `data/gear.js`): six slots, the brief's four (Lantern, Coat, Boots, Charm) and a Ring and a Pendant at the Smithy's levels 3 and 5. A recipe is opened by research, a challenge, or a feat, and costs materials. An item is a tier (1 to 5) and its affixes: the recipe's own first, one more at tier 1, a third at tier 3. Affixes are of four kinds, and every recipe's own is one that changes the build (brief §6.3), never a flat stat: a **build** affix is a number a tag's weapons fire with (`+1 Projectile (Fire)`, `+1 Pierce (Steel)`), the same at every tier; a **hook** affix is a rule with its own code in `sim/hooks.js` (`Walking leaves burning oil`, `Level-ups send out a wave`; ruling 16 took every dash trigger off gear), its listed params grown by the tier; a **rule** affix is a number the run is built with (`Level-ups offer 4 cards`, `Final Forms get +6% Damage`), only on the small things you carry; a **stat** affix is a passive's modifier times the tier (×1 to ×3). A tier up costs the recipe times the new tier. A **re-roll** (level 3) picks one affix past the recipe's own, pays materials times the tier, and chooses from 3 offered (never the recipe's own, never one already on the item); the offer holds until one is taken. There is no random drop grinding. Worn gear goes into every run (`gearForRun`): its modifiers, its rules once each, its hooks once each at the best tier worn (`RunConfig.gearHooks`).
- **Tempering** (the Smithy, level 4; it replaced rune stones, ruling 14): the weapon a run sets out with starts with one of its Rare lines (§5.21).
- **The Market:** a fixed table: 5 commons for a common, 30 for a rare, a rare for 3 commons, and a rare for a rare at the common rate (5), better at levels 3 and 5 (4, 24, 4; then 3, 18, 5). It deals in map 1's materials; the Marsh's aren't traded (§20 call 151). Since 0.7.3 it sells at most 3 rares between two runs, and its row takes an Amount (1, 5, 10, or 25 a press) and the material you pay with (call 106). The next-3 list counts only materials in hand (call 84). From level 2 it buys the Pearls left at a run's end as Coin (half a Coin each, then one).
- **The Apothecary** (`data/consumables.js`, ruling 12): eight run tools, bought before a run and used up as it sets out (`startRunProfile`): charges for the level-up cards, a Revival, and things in hand at 0:00 (Pearls, an Ember, a card more, food that heals more). The code keeps the word consumable; the screens say run tool (§10.9).
- **The Chapel** (`data/tide.js`): Tides 1 to 10 and 5 curses. A Tide multiplies enemy health (bosses too), damage, numbers, and speed, and adds to the materials a run brings home; clearing the map at the highest Tide opened opens the next, and the Chapel's level caps which can be chosen. A curse is a modifier list or a multiplier (Hunger: no food drops) plus its own share more materials. Both are chosen at the gate.
- **Arcana** are gone (ruling 11): five became relics and the gate's row became the Heirloom (§10.9).
- **The Cartographer** (§10.9): Reliquaries on the minimap (level 1), small secrets (2), challenge runs posted on the board (3), short runs (4), and big secrets and hints (5). The bestiary's ladders and claims are gone: each ordinary enemy has a Hunt on the Harbor Orders board (`data/trophies.js`), counted over every run, and a Hunt opens that enemy's trophy (5% more damage against it: `RunConfig.bestiaryBonus`). **Challenge runs** (`data/challenges.js`) set the character, a Tide, curses, and pools, with a goal and a reward; they are orders with `rules`, started from the board.
- **Character mastery** (`data/mastery.js`, `meta/state.js masteryOf`): each run gives the character played mastery XP (a point a minute, more a boss; `tuning.mastery`). Ranks 1 to 11 at `META.masteryRanks` (8 to 800 points): a skill point at ranks 1, 3, 5, 7, 9, 10, and 11 (§5.22), a kit at 2 and 4, a piece of gear at 6, and the character's own challenge run at 8. The perks of 0.7.0 to 0.9.0's first builds are gone (ruling 15).
- **Harbor Orders** (`data/feats.js` with `orders-characters.js`, `orders-content.js`, `orders-weapons.js`, and `trophies.js`; judged by `meta/feats.js judgeFeats`): 143 conditions read from a run's result and the lifetime numbers, judged from the first run and held until the Tavern keeps the board (level 1). Each opens exactly one thing ('feat:<id>') or pays materials. A visibility: `visible`, `after_found` (shown once its discovery condition is met), or `hidden`. A condition this build can't judge is never met. Tiers follow a run to the dawn (§10.10). Three a drafted weapon open its codex ticks, Tempering, and an early Legendary (`data/orders-weapons.js`, `DATA.weaponPerks`).
- **The gate** (`app/setup.js`, `ui/hubscreens.js setupScreen`): the map, then the character (§10.6.1), then the rows open now (§10.2), starting from the last choices still open, with a line for what the focused row's value does. Every value is one `runConfigFor` accepts, and Start checks the run as the run would, so a refusal stays on the screen.
- **The 70% rule** (brief §6.4): the pacing tool reports the share of run content in a fresh profile's pools (the weapons that go in the pools, the passives, and the relics; characters don't count). The target is 30% or less. Since 0.9.0 the fresh pools hold 6 weapons and 8 passives, so the pool is about twice the slots (ruling 3); X2 measured the share at 32% (§20 has the earlier readings). The gate pins the share exactly, so any change to it is a deliberate one.

### 10.6.1 The second map: the Salt Marsh in the harbor (0.8.0)
The Marsh's sim is §5.17; this is what the harbor, the gate, the screens, and the save do with it. The design is `docs/design/SALT_MARSH.md`; the contract is `docs/design/SALT_MARSH_BUILD.md`.
- **Which maps are open** (`meta/state.js` `mapOpen`, `mapsOpen`, `mapMissing`): a map's `unlock` is a key or a list of keys, every one needed; a map without one is open from the start (map 1). The Salt Marsh's: `building:docks:1` (the boat repaired), `boss:harbor_maw` (that boss killed on any run, read off the bestiary), and `building:tavern:1` (the Tavern keeps the feat list), so skill and the Tavern open it, not a pile of Coin. Two unlock kinds are new for any content: `boss:<id>` and `map:<id>` (that map open). Each building level a map's way waits on says so on its panel ("Opens the way to the Salt Marsh, with the Harbor Maw killed and the Tavern at Lv 1"); a level or a run that opens it says "New map: Salt Marsh. Choose it at the gate" (the restore's change carries `maps`; a run's change list `map_open`).
- **The gate asks where first** once a second map is open (`ui/hubscreens.js` `mapScreen`; the hub scene's `openGate`), a card per map (`ui/hubtext.js` `mapCard`), starting on the map last chosen (`setup.map`): its description, what drops there alone (a material whose `maps` names only that map; Coin, Oil, and Map Scraps drop everywhere), which of Next up's three a run there gets you closer to ("Gets you closer to: Brine Notes": one short of a material only that map drops, or a project that counts only runs there), the research that counts there, and the Tides open there (once the Chapel picks Tides), held to what the gate's Tide row offers: more opened than the Chapel allows says so ("Tides open here: 1 to 3. The Chapel allows up to Tide 2. Restore it to Lv 2 for more"). Then who sets out, then the setup rows, whose Tide row offers that map's own Tides. `runConfigFor` takes `choice.map` and refuses one that isn't open in plain words ("The Salt Marsh isn't open yet. Needs the Harbor Maw killed and the Tavern at Lv 1."). A challenge plays on its own map (`challenge.map`, map 1 when left out), whatever the gate's, and the gate offers only challenges whose map is open (`app/setup.js challengesOpen`). Unlock keys are worded by `meta/actions.js unlockText` for every kind `parseUnlock` reads; a key this build can't meet reads "something not in this version", never its id.
- **Layers** (`meta/runconfig.js` `MAP_LAYERS`, `layersFor`): what of a map the profile has opened goes into `RunConfig.layers`, any one key enough: on the Marsh the storm once the Old Grey has died (`boss:old_grey`), the Salt Well after its first clear (`tide:salt_marsh:1`, its Tide 1 opened), and the secret at its Tide 2 or the Cartographer's level 5. A map without `layers` sends none (the sim then turns all on, for tools and tests).
- **Tides per map** (save v4, §12.2): `profile.tides` holds each map's Tides opened; a clear at a map's highest opens its next, on that map only (Tide 1 on the Marsh by clearing it at none); `profile.tide` keeps only the last chosen. The Chapel caps every map alike (`tideAvailable(doc, data, map)`). A Tide asked for above that says what to do: "Tide 2 isn't open on the Salt Marsh yet. Clear the Salt Marsh at Tide 1 first.", or "Tide 3 needs the Chapel at Lv 2." `tide:<n>` keys (`reached`) read the highest of any map; the layers' `tide:<map>:<n>` keys read one map's ladder. Completion counts every map's ladder (maps x 10). The Chapel's panel says which maps each Tide is open on.
- **Research on a map** (`runsOn`): only qualifying runs on that map move it; on a run elsewhere it waits, and the results say so ("Research on Brine Notes: 2 more runs on the Salt Marsh", change `research_elsewhere`). The Library's row says "Counts only runs on the Salt Marsh" and, under way, "In progress: 2 more runs on the Salt Marsh"; Next up adds "Then 2 runs on the Salt Marsh". While its map is closed a project can't start (`whyNotResearch`: "Opens with the Salt Marsh, which needs the Harbor Maw killed and the Tavern at Lv 1"), and a cost short of a material only a closed map drops says where it comes from (`meta/actions.js needText`: "Need 25 Marsh Herbs. Marsh Herbs drop only on the Salt Marsh, which needs ..."); Next up's option carries `waits` and reads "Opens with the Salt Marsh".
- **The Salt Well**: the `well` halt opens `ui/menus.js` `wellScreen`: the relics carried as cards, "Give up one relic. It is gone for the rest of this run, and the Ferryman offers it on every map from then on.", and Keep them (where focus starts; Back keeps too, since ignoring the well costs nothing). A result's `wellGiven` joins `profile.well` (once each); `runConfigFor` passes those still in the run's pools as `RunConfig.wellRelics`, and the Ferryman (`sim/relics.js wellOffer`) adds a Well slot to each stock: one of them in turn, not carried, not banished, not already on offer, at its tier's price, with no draw (the loot stream never moves for it). The trade screen names it "Well" beside its tier.
- **First sightings and the field guide**: a FIRST_SIGHT event is a notice on the quiet line, "First sighting: Mire Toad" and then what it is (the enemy's description, or `META.fieldGuide`'s line for a thing: the water, the storm, the Eye, the herbs, the Well, a pillar, the causeway, a thrall), only for what no earlier run has shown on any map (`seenAnywhere`). A result's `sighted` joins that map's `profile.seen` (change `sighted`: "New in the field guide: ..."). The pause menu's **Field guide** lists what this map has shown, earlier runs' first and this run's marked "New this run" (`app/runlogic.js fieldGuideView`). The water speaks too: "High water in 15s: the creeks will flood" on the middle line, high water, low water, and overtime's rising flood on the quiet line; the Eye and the Well's opening each have a line.
- **Who arrives**: a run that brings a character (a feat, a challenge, a map) lists `arrival` in its changes (`applyRunResult` compares the characters before and after); the harbor is built without them, and their boat comes in when the results close (`app/hublogic.js runArrivals`); setting out straight from the results, they're simply on the square. The Punter comes home after the first crossing (5:00 on the Marsh).
- **Costs on the Marsh's materials**, never at a building's level 1 (the content test holds it): the Apothecary's Oil becomes Marsh Herbs from level 2, the Docks take a pinch of Herbs at levels 3 to 5, the Smithy Salt Glass from level 3, and every Smithy and Apothecary level keeps a Docks material beside the Marsh one. Re-rolls (per tier) take Glass, gear tiers 4 and 5 add Glass (`GEAR_TIER_EXTRA`), and the Gull Bone and Tar Salve take Herbs. The content test holds every cost to a built map's drops, the MVP buildings and the upgrades to map 1's, and each research tier to some projects payable and finished on map 1 alone. The numbers are B6's to refit.

### 10.7 Prestige: New Tide (Phase 10)
- **Kept** (brief): one Prestige Relic per cycle, character mastery, and the bestiary. **Also kept** (builder's call): character unlocks, codex discoveries, feats, lifetime stats, and settings.
- **Reset:** buildings, materials, research, gear, Tide unlocks, and map access.
- **The Prestige Relic** is picked from a set when the cycle ends and is active in every run of every later cycle. They accumulate, one per cycle.
- `data/cycles.js` defines each cycle's building variant set, NPC story lines, enemy ramp variants, and new boss, so the town comes back different but still authored.
- **The hidden map:** the Reef has a secret interaction (for example, standing in a sunken bell's light for several seconds). It records the discovery in the profile, and the Docks then shows the route. This is built in Phase 10.

---

### 10.8 Run tasks, relics, Pearls, and the Ferryman (Phase 7, as reworked)
Kevin set these out for Phase 7 (`sim/tasks.js`, `sim/relics.js`; numbers in `tuning.tasks`, `tuning.relics`, `tuning.glass`, `tuning.ferry`). How they work now is §5.18 (0.8.2: lit lamps' light, bells calling a crew, Lantern Night banked, the Ferryman calling at moorings) and §5.20 (0.9.0: three acts, sites drawn per run, lamps that stay lit). What still holds from Phase 7:
- **No timers.** A task moves only while you're doing it and waits when you leave. What it asks and gives is set by the minute you start it, so one left until late costs more and pays more.
- **Pearls** (Sea Glass in the code and saves: `glass`) are the run's own currency, spent with the Ferryman; what's left at the end is lost unless the Market buys it.
- **Relics** (`data/relics.js`, 30) carry a `tier` (1 to 3) that the screens name by rarity (Common, Uncommon, Rare; RULING-L1 cut Trinket, Charm, and Relic). They take relic slots (§5.19). They come from a **Reliquary** (a rare drop, at least `reliquaryGap` apart, certain after a pity count of kills, sized to the pool: §5.18), the vault (one rarity higher), the Ferryman, a map's secret, and the Heirloom. What's offered leans on the minute (`tierWeights`), so the strong ones arrive late. Actives answer walking (Spark Flint), a hit taken (Barnacle Shell), a crowd close (Bloodied Hook), and their own clock (Drowned Bell); none answers the dash or a kill count.
- **The harbor's part** (`RunConfig.harbor`): the Ferryman's level, how fast lamps light, how wide bells ring, and what the Cartographer puts on the minimap.

### 10.9 Fewer systems (0.9.0, the sweep's batch X2; the lead's rulings 11 to 13; work/sweep/X2.md)

The sweep found ten systems handed over in two hours and a gate of 14 rows. Three systems went into ones that were already there. The older sections (§5.15, §10.2, §10.6, §11) were brought in line with this one on Nov 20.

- **Arcana are relics (ruling 11).** `data/arcana.js` is gone.
