The five arcana with code of their own are relics (Moon Pull, Long Beam, Sea Rite, Kindling, Glassblower: hooks `gem_vacuum`, `sea_heal`, `ember_luck`, `relic_tier`); the nine that were a relic's rule with bigger numbers went. **The Heirloom**: at the gate, one relic the codex has seen, carried from 0:00 in a relic slot (`RunConfig.relics: [id]` and `heirloom: id` for the result; `meta/state.js heirloomOpen`, `heirloomChoices`). The slot is its price. The Tavern's level 3 opens it (`heirloom: 1`).
- **Consumables are run tools (ruling 12).** `data/consumables.js` holds eight: charges for the level-up cards, a Revival, and things in hand at 0:00 (`start`: Pearls, an Ember as `RunConfig.startEmbers`, a card more, food that heals more). The code keeps the word consumable (`profile.consumables`); the screens say run tool. The Apothecary's levels: a slot at 1, 3, and 5; food heals 25% and 50% more at 2 and 4 (`RunConfig.harbor.foodHeal`, read by `sim/pickups.js` and the Ferryman's food).
- **One board (ruling 13).** A challenge run is a Harbor Order with `rules` (`data/challenges.js SET_RUNS`, group `run`; `DATA.challenges` is the same list as the run's config reads it), posted by the Cartographer's level 3 and started from the board (`ui/hubscreens.js ordersScreen onSetRun`, `app/setup.js whyNotSetRun`), never the gate. Its condition is `when.run` plus its goal. The bestiary's ladders and claim screen are gone: each enemy that isn't a boss has a **Hunt** (`data/trophies.js`, group `hunt`, `when.enemyTotal`: kills over every run, which `meta/apply.js` passes to `judgeFeats` as `stats.enemyKills`), and a Hunt opens that enemy's trophy (5% more damage against it: `RunConfig.bestiaryBonus`, `meta/workshop.js bestiaryBonus`).
- **Two books.** The glossary says what a term does. The codex (`meta/codex.js`) lists what you've found; an enemy's entry carries `kills` and its `hunt`. `codexEntries(doc, data, map)` is one map's codex (what the map has shown, its enemies, bosses, and secrets): the pause menu's Codex button in a run. The field guide's and the bestiary's screens are gone.
- **The gate** (`app/setup.js setupRows`): Map (with the short run), Character (skills shown), Starting weapon, Heirloom, Run tools, Difficulty (the Tide and the curses). A `multi` row holds several items on one line (`ui/hubscreens.js setupScreen`: left and right move along them, confirm changes the one in focus).
- **The Cartographer** draws maps: Reliquaries (1), small secrets (2, `harbor.findsOnMap`), set runs posted (3), short runs (4), big secrets and hints (5). **The Lighthouse**: stage 1 speeds lamps, stage 8 widens bells, stage 9 sells the Ember Tin.
- **D5, level gates:** curses come with the Chapel's level 4 (they came with its first, beside Tides).
- **Saves** (`meta/retired.js retireOld`, called first by `reconcileProfile`, once per save: `profile.retired`): the gate's arcana is its relic as the Heirloom; an arcana opened keeps its relic in the pool (`profile.kept`), found (the codex), and the Heirloom row open (`profile.heirloomHeld`); tonics held are paid back at their price; a challenge won is its order done (same id); a trophy rung claimed or reached is the Hunt done, and rungs reached and unclaimed pay what they would have; curses a Chapel level had opened stay (`profile.cursesHeld`). What was paid is said once in the harbor (`profile.retiredNote`, `app/hublogic.js retiredNoteText`). Three run tools moved keys (`MOVED_UNLOCKS` in `meta/reconcile.js`). The save's schema keeps `setup.arcana`, `challenges`, and `bestiaryClaimed` (no version bump); nothing writes them now.

### 10.10 The economy pass (0.9.0, the sweep's batch X3; work/sweep/X3.md)
- **Overtime pays less in bulk.** Its materials grow to 1.5x at most (`map.overtime.materialsCap`, was 3), and its elites drop their rare material (Iron, Map Scraps, Salt Glass) at half the chance (`map.overtime.eliteRare`, read in `sim/pickups.js rollDrops`). The Long Night pays in its dawn chest and its order on each map. Measured: a run to the dawn brings home 3.2 to 4.3 times a leaving run's materials for 1.5 times the minutes (it was 4.2 to 7.6 times).
- **The pacing tool has a third player, who stays** (`tools/pacing/measure.mjs STAYS`, `--stays`, `tools/pacing/yields-stays.json`): bots that can't die, staying to the dawn. The deploy gate checks that file only once it exists; the release remeasure writes it (about an hour on 2 cores for all three players).
- **Gear's Iron** went from 5 to 3 a recipe (7 to 4 for the dear ones), and each slot asks one of the materials that ran out first: Oil, Timber, Drowned Pages, and 10 Rum on every recipe.
- **The board against a run to the dawn**: what a run to the dawn finishes is tier 1 or 2; tier 3 and 4 ask for more. Level 100 is Level 50 (a run to the dawn reaches Lv 42 to 49). The Hunts are about 5 to 8 runs to the dawn each.
- **Level gates** (`needs` on a building level, `meta/actions.js levelNeeds`): the Chapel's first level waits on the Salt Widow killed, the Smithy's on the Tavern's level 2, the Apothecary's on the Smithy's level 3, so the harbor hands its systems over a run or more apart.
- **Overtime's crowds drop less**: an ordinary enemy's material chances in overtime are times `map.overtime.dropMul` (0.45). With `run.materialMul` at 1.2, the practiced player who leaves restores the MVP harbor in about 9.8 h and the one who stays to the dawn in about 7.0 h: staying pays about 2.1 times a run, about 1.4 times an hour (the lead's ceiling: 1.5).
- **Rope**: the Drowned Bosun's Rope drop is 0.02 a kill (0.2 made Rope pour in once Bosuns came by the thousand), and a Coat or Boots recipe asks 25 Rope.

## 11. Content data (`src/data/`)

Conventions:
- Every entry has an `id` (snake_case and **permanent**, because saves reference it), a `name`, and a `desc` in plain speech.
- IDs are never reused, and a rename keeps the id, so saves and replays keep working (RULING-L1 renamed dozens of names that way; no save stores a name). There is no `data/renames.js`. A changed `unlock` key gets a `MOVED_UNLOCKS` row (§12.5); a retired system's ids are mapped once by `meta/retired.js` (§10.9).
- **Code words and screen words differ** where the language pass renamed a term but kept the code (RULING-L1, WRITING.md §7.6): `daze` is Stunned, `gaff` is Hooked, `kindred` is the Tag bonus, `ward` is a lamp's light or a Shield (the Tide Priest's), `glass` is Pearls, `consumable` is a run tool, `feat` is a Harbor Order, `ascension` and `axis` are Forms (delivery, flight, payoff: First, Second, Final Form), a weapon's `deed` is its Final Form goal, `cost` and `price` are a Drawback, `oil` (the Lamplighter's) is Fuel, `line` and `special` are a trait and a Legendary trait, `set run` is a challenge run, `affix` (an elite's) is an elite power, `tier` (a relic's) is a rarity, and `find` is a Secret. A character's `trade` (`sim/trades.js`) has no screen word at all: the screens say what it does (`gives`), and the signature's growth line says the action ("Levels up every 3:00, and as she lights lamps"); "trade", "own weapon", "own track", and "the wait" are never player words (WRITING.md §7.6).
- **Numbers in player text read the data** where `data/textnum.js` placeholders are used (characters, signatures, signature Forms, the glossary's numbers); a typed digit there fails a test (§5.22, `tests/sweep-x1.test.js` for the glossary).
- Kevin directs tuning by name ("Harpoon Chain cooldown 20% lower at level 3"). The builder edits data only, and the tests prove the change is valid.
- `tests/content.test.js` validates every file against the shapes below: unique IDs, every reference resolves, every sprite exists in the atlas, every hook exists, the research graph is valid, nothing evolves, nothing fires from the dash, and there are no dead materials. The shapes below are examples; the file headers in `src/data/` are the full description.

```js
// weapons.js (format v4: work/cw/C1-CONTRACT.md §2; §5.21)
{
  id: 'flare_gun', name: 'Flare Gun', desc: 'Fires a flare at the nearest enemy.', tags: ['fire', 'light'],
  sprite: 'proj_flare', icon: 'icon_flare_gun', unlock: 'starter', weight: 100, unit: 'Flare', units: 'Flares',
  base: { damage: 12, cooldown: 1.1, amount: 1, duration: 1.6, pierce: 1, knockback: 0.6, radius: 3, interval: 0.1 },
  pipeline: { emit: { type: 'nearest', range: 360 }, motion: { type: 'linear', speed: 230 },
              onHit: [{ type: 'damage' }, { type: 'knockback' }, { type: 'light', radius: 14, ms: 120 }] },
  numbers: ['damage', 'cooldown', 'amount', 'pierce', 'crit'],          // its Common and Uncommon cards
  lines: [{ id: 'splinter', name: 'Splinter', rarity: 'rare', shared: 'splinter', desc: '...', boost: { /* ... */ } }, /* ... */],
  specials: [{ id: 'parting_shot', name: 'Parting Shot', moves: true, desc: '...', boost: { fx: { cue: { /* ... */ } } } }, /* ... */],
  deed: { kind: 'kills', far: 90, count: 90, text: 'Kill 90 enemies from far off with it' },
  partner: 'wick_oil', sfx: { fire: 'flare_fire', hit: 'hit_soft' },
}

// ascensions.js (Forms: §5.16)
{ line: 'flare_gun', weapons: ['flare_gun'], capstoneUnlock: undefined,
  options: [{ id: 'salvo', axis: 'delivery', name: 'Salvo', desc: '...', price: '+10% Cooldown', boost: { /* ... */ } }, /* two an axis */] }
// relics.js (tier 1 to 3, shown by rarity name: Common, Uncommon, Rare; a hook, or modifiers)
{ id: 'volatile_gems', name: 'Volatile Gems', desc: 'XP gems explode when collected.', tier: 3,
  hook: 'gem_explode', params: { damage: 12, radius: 26, every: 0.1 }, unlock: 'research:volatile_salts' }
{ id: 'brass_button', name: 'Brass Button', desc: '+6% Damage.', tier: 1, unlock: 'starter',
  mods: [{ stat: 'might', op: 'pct', value: 0.06 }] }

// passives.js (fixed levels and a max; the last level's keystone adds a rule: world.rules.<id>)
{ id: 'undertow', name: 'Undertow', maxLevel: 5, tags: ['sea'], levels: [/* modifiers a level */],
  keystone: { id: 'undertow', desc: 'Sea hits slow enemies by 20% for 1.5s.' }, unlock: 'research:signal_codes' }

// enemies.js
{ id: 'drowned_sailor', name: 'Drowned Sailor', sprite: 'en_sailor', ramp: 'drowned',
  hp: 12, speed: 38, contact: 5, mass: 1, xp: 1, ai: { type: 'chase' },
  drops: [{ material: 'rope', chance: 0.01 }, { material: 'drowned_pages', chance: 0.008 }], tags: ['undead', 'water'] }

// maps.js
{ id: 'drowned_docks', name: 'Drowned Docks', size: [256, 256], layout: 'docks_1', ambient: 0.35,
  materials: ['timber', 'rope', 'drowned_pages', 'rum'],   // plus coin and oil, which drop on every map
  timeline: [
    { from: 0,   to: 60,  roster: { fog_wisp: 1 },                      density: [20, 60] },
    { from: 60,  to: 180, roster: { fog_wisp: 3, drowned_sailor: 1 },   density: [60, 150] },
    { at: 300,  event: 'elite', enemy: 'drowned_bosun' },
    { at: 600,  event: 'boss',  enemy: 'harbor_maw' },
    { at: 1200, event: 'boss',  enemy: 'the_tidecaller' },
  ],
  overtime: { densityPerMin: 1.12, hpPerMin: 1.15, materialsPerMin: 1.10 },
  secrets: [{ id: 'wreck_cache', name: 'Wreck Cache', at: [212, 40], radius: 10, relic: 'wreck_lantern' }] }

// buildings.js (the plot and the door are the harbor map's, data/hub.js)
{ id: 'library', name: 'Library', desc: '...', npc: 'librarian', material: 'drowned_pages',
  levels: [
    { cost: { drowned_pages: 10, coin: 20, timber: 5 }, researchSlots: 1, researchTier: 1 },
    { cost: { drowned_pages: 30, coin: 45, timber: 12 }, researchTier: 2 },
    /* 3 more: every building has 5 */ ] }
// A level's effects are `tree` (the Lamp Post's upgrade tier), `researchTier`, and `researchSlots`;
// what else it brings says so itself: the Harpooner's `unlock` is 'building:tavern:1'.

// research.js (what a project adds says so itself: Lantern Orb's `unlock` is 'research:orbwright')
{ id: 'tide_charts', name: 'Tide Charts', desc: 'The old harbormaster\'s charts, and what the tide does to things.',
  tier: 1, cost: { drowned_pages: 12, coin: 30 }, runs: 2, requires: [] }

// upgrades.js (the Lamp Post's capped stat tree)
{ id: 'thick_blood', name: 'Thick Blood', desc: '8 more health.', tier: 1, ranks: 5,
  mods: [{ stat: 'maxHp', op: 'add', value: 8 }], cost: { oil: 8, coin: 6 } }   // rank r costs r times this

// npcs.js
{ id: 'lamp_keeper', name: 'Old Wick', desc: '...', sprite: 'npc_lamp_keeper', home: 'lamp_post',
  lines: [{ when: 'lamp_post:4', text: 'Forty years I lit these streets. Nice to have help.' }, /* ... */] }

// characters.js (§5.22; the full field list is the file's header)
{ id: 'harpooner', name: 'Harpooner', sprite: 'pc_harpooner', start: 'anchor_chain', signature: 'grapnel', noDraft: ['tide_bell'],
  base: { maxHp: 110 }, mechanic: { hook: 'harpooner', params: { /* ... */ } },
  trade: { id: 'hook', params: { every: 6, clock: 180, per: 4, gap: 40, span: 180, deed: 6, /* ... */ } },
  who: '...', desc: '...', gives: 'Every {t.every}s her hook flies the way she faces. ...', costs: '...',
  card: { does: '...', costs: '...' }, unlock: 'building:lamp_post:2' }

// skills.js (ruling 15; §5.22)
{ id: 'sleigh_ride', character: 'harpooner', row: 'build', name: 'Sleigh Ride', desc: 'When her hook pulls an elite, she is pulled to it instead, hitting enemies on the way.',
  p: { damageMul: 1.5, width: 14 }, rival: 'deadweight' }

// dashes.js (ruling 16; §5.24)
{ id: 'heavy_step', name: 'Heavy Step', character: 'harpooner', icon: 'icon_dash_heavy', dist: 0.6, cooldown: 0.75,
  mark: { kind: 'knock', where: 'path', radius: 18, strength: 2 }, numbers: ['dash_recharge', 'dash_distance'], weight: 100, unlock: 'starter' }

// feats.js (when: conditions on the run's result and the lifetime numbers, all of them met)
{ id: 'untouched', name: 'Untouched', desc: 'Reach 5:00 without taking a hit.', visibility: 'after_found',
  found: { untouchedSec: 60 }, when: { untouchedSec: 300 }, reward: { materials: { rum: 10, coin: 100 } } }

// gear.js (an affix: stat, build, hook, or rule; §10.6)
{ id: 'storm_lantern', name: 'Storm Lantern', slot: 'lantern', icon: 'gear_lantern', base: 'fire_volley',
  pool: [/* stat and build affixes */], cost: { iron: 2, oil: 16, coin: 40 }, unlock: 'research:smiths_ledger' }
{ id: 'fire_volley', kind: 'build', name: 'Projectile (Fire)', mod: { stat: 'amount', op: 'add', tags: ['fire'] }, value: 1 }
{ id: 'oil_wake', kind: 'hook', name: 'Walking leaves burning oil', hook: 'walk_trail', grow: ['damage', 'dps'], params: { /* ... */ } }

// challenges.js (a challenge run is a Harbor Order with rules, started from the board: §10.9)
{ id: 'flood', name: 'Flood', desc: 'Lamplighter at Tide 4 with Swarm. Survive 8:00.', character: 'lamplighter',
  tide: 4, curses: ['swarm'], goal: { surviveSec: 480 }, reward: { materials: { map_scraps: 6, iron: 8 } } }

// maps.js, Phase 7's part
tasks: [{ kind: 'lamp', at: [128, 100] }, { kind: 'bell', at: [176, 84] }, /* ... */], ferry: { at: [120, 136] }
```

Other files follow the same pattern:
- `tide.js`: the Tides and the curses, multipliers and modifier lists plus a reward share
- `consumables.js`: the eight run tools (charges, a Revival, things in hand at 0:00)
- `mastery.js`: the kits (other start weapons at ranks 2 and 4)
- `weaponups.js`: the upgrade types (§5.21); `rarity.js`: the five rarities' names and colors (view-only)
- `signatures.js`, `signature-forms.js`: the six signature weapons and their axes (§5.22)
- `skills.js`, `dashes.js`: skill trees and dash items
- `feats.js`, `orders-*.js`, `trophies.js`, `challenges.js`: the Harbor Orders board
- `glossary.js`: one entry a term (`desc` up to 40 words, `short`, `more`), read by every screen that explains a term (WRITING.md §7.5)
- `textnum.js`: the placeholder filler for numbers in text
- `music.js`: the code-made music's loops
- `market.js`: what the Market trades and its rates by level
- `cycles.js`: per-cycle prestige content (Phase 10; not written yet)
- `hub.js`: the harbor's map (ground, plots, ruins, fog regions, props, the pier, the gate, the keepers' posts and walks, the waiting spots, the avatar); its checks are in the content test
- `meta.js`: the meta layer's numbers (what counts as a research run, the mastery ranks, first-sighting lines)
- `materials.js`, `sfx.js`, `palette.js`, and `credits.js`
- `tuning.js`: every feel value and curve
- `index.js`: imports and exports everything, and is the single entry point for the game and the tests

---

## 12. Saves, replays, and reports (`src/save/`)

### 12.1 Where saves live
| Build | Save | Settings |
|---|---|---|
| PC release | `%APPDATA%\Lamplight\profile.json`, through the preload bridge | `settings.json` in the same folder |
| Playtest | IndexedDB (localStorage as fallback) | IndexedDB |

- Settings are kept separate so display preferences don't travel inside an exported save.
- **Atomic writes** on the desktop: write to `profile.json.tmp`, flush, then rename (retried for about a second if Windows antivirus or a sync tool briefly locks the file). A crash mid-write can't corrupt the save. On every run end the previous save rotates into `profile.bak1.json` through `profile.bak5.json`.
- **Quarantine:** if the main save exists but can't be used (damaged, locked, or from a migration that failed), it is copied to `profile.unreadable-<time>.json` before anything else happens, so no later save can overwrite the newest progress. A save from a newer build is never overwritten either: this build can't read it, so the game doesn't start (a blank profile could never be saved). It says so in one plain sentence ("This save was made by a newer version of Lamplight... Your save is untouched."), from `app/profileboot.js`; the web build keeps checking for the newer version and reloads into it, and the desktop build says to open the newest Lamplight.
- **A failed save** keeps the progress in memory and shows a notice that stays up until a save works; the next change saves it. Nothing that leaves the page goes over progress that isn't on disk (`app/keeper.js`, through `app/flow.js`): an update's reload, Quit, reopening the window for a display mode, and reloading after an error all wait for the writes in flight and save again first. If that fails too, Quit and the error screen's reload ask ("Quit anyway" or "Stay"), and the others stay put. Closing the page any other way while a save still fails (a browser tab, the window's X, Alt+F4) asks first too. The desktop shell asks the game's own question (Stay by default) from its main process, which the page tells whenever a save starts or stops failing, so it asks even when only the pad has been used (a page can hold a close only after a mouse or keyboard press). In the browser the page holds the close (`beforeunload`) and the browser asks in its own words, which it does only after such a press: a browser tab closed having used only the pad closes without asking.
- **One write queue:** a run-end save and a hub save can't interleave and land out of order.
- Autosave after every run and every hub action.

### 12.2 Versioning and migrations
- The save document has a schema version `v`. `migrations[n]` converts version n to n+1, and loading runs every step needed.
- **A fixture save from every schema version is kept in `tests/fixtures/saves/` forever.** The gate loads each one, migrates it, and validates the result. An update can never wipe progress without failing the build first.
- Content IDs the game no longer knows go into an `orphans` bucket instead of being deleted, so progress is never thrown away (`meta/reconcile.js`, run on every load; the fixture `profile-v1.txt` carries a research id that no longer exists).
- **Version 2 (Phase 5)**: the Lamp Post's `upgrades` (id → rank); research keeps `done` and `active` (with `runsLeft`), and its slot count moved out of the save (it comes from the Library's level). The v1 → v2 migration adds the upgrades and drops the stored slots; `profile-v2.txt` is the v2 fixture.
- **Version 3 (Phase 7)**: gear gains `next` (the next item's number, past the highest held) and `rolls` (every re-roll made), and an item may carry its own `rolls` (its re-rolls, the seed of its next offer; anything but a whole number reads as 0); new parts: `consumables` (held counts, rune stones as 'rune:<id>'), `bestiaryClaimed`, `featsFound`, `challenges`, `setup` (the gate's last choices), and four lifetime numbers (boss kills, clears, lamps lit, bells rung). The v2 → v3 migration adds them empty and moves nothing. Reconcile sets aside gear of an unknown recipe whole, feats, challenges, and consumables it doesn't know, and claims for an unknown enemy, and brings them back when a build knows them; a worn item must be held and in its own slot; the gate's choices are preferences, so anything this build can't use is reset. `profile-v3.txt` is the v3 fixture.
- **No schema bump since v4.** 0.9.0's retirements (arcana, tonics, challenges and the bestiary, runes, mastery perks) go through `meta/retired.js` once per save (`profile.retired`, `RETIRED_VERSION` 2, and `profile.perksRetired` for the perks), moved unlock keys through `MOVED_UNLOCKS` (§12.5), and fields nothing writes any more stay in the schema so a save's shape holds (`setup.arcana`, `setup.rune`, `challenges`, `bestiaryClaimed`).
- **Version 4 (0.8.0, the Salt Marsh)**: Tides are opened per map: `tides` (map id to Tides opened) and `tide` keeps only `selected`, the last chosen; `well` (relic ids given to the Salt Well); `seen` (map id to what that map has shown: `'enemy:<id>'` or a thing's name); and `setup.map`, the gate's last map. The v3 → v4 migration moves `tide.unlocked` to `tides.drowned_docks` (the only map a v3 save could have cleared) and an older build's Tides set aside in `orphans.tide` to `orphans.tides.drowned_docks`, keeps `selected`, and seeds `seen.drowned_docks` with every enemy the bestiary has counted (so the first-sighting notices start from what the player has already met). Reconcile holds each map's Tides to this build's (the rest aside), sets aside a map it doesn't know (its Tides and sightings) and a relic it doesn't know (from `well`), and brings them back when a build knows them; `setup.map` resets to '' when it names no map here. `profile-v4.txt` is the v4 fixture.

### 12.3 Export and import
- The code is `LAMP1.` + base64url(deflate(JSON)) + `.` + a CRC32 checksum.
- Import checks the checksum, then the version, then migrates. It shows a summary (hub progress, playtime, characters) before overwriting, and backs up the old save first.
- Export covers the profile only, not settings.

### 12.4 Replays and bug reports
- Every run records seed + build + RunConfig + input frames. Input compresses well; a 20-minute run is expected to be tens of KB. The last 3 replays are kept in `%APPDATA%\Lamplight\replays\`.
- **Pause menu → "Save bug report"** (also on the error screen) writes a report file with the replay, the tuning he changed, settings, the last 300 events that matter, any captured errors, and a snapshot of the run, then shows where it went. On the desktop it goes to `%APPDATA%\Lamplight\reports\`; the web build downloads it (a browser's database can't be attached to a chat). Kevin attaches that one file. Claude plays it with `node tools/replay/replay.mjs <file>` to the exact moment and sees what he saw.
- Tuning changes and dev commands made mid-run are logged decisions like draft picks (§15), so a report from a tuned or cheated run still replays exactly.
- `window.onerror` and unhandled promise rejections are captured into an error log that goes into reports.
- **The error screen** ("Error": Save bug report, Reload) opens once, over whatever was showing, for a crash in the sim (which pauses it), a scene that couldn't start, or a run's end that couldn't be folded into the profile; it says whether a run was stopped. Reloading from it goes through the keeper like any way off the page (§12.1).

---

### 12.5 Rules the sweep added (0.9.0, X5)
- **Unlock keys that move.** When content's `unlock` key changes in `src/data`, add a row to `MOVED_UNLOCKS` in `meta/reconcile.js` (`{ was, now }`, at the end, never reordered). A save that had met `was` holds `now` from its first load on a build with the row: `profile.kept` lists the held keys and `profile.keptThrough` counts the rows already judged for that save, so each row is judged once and a harbor built after the move earns the content the new way. `meta/state.js contentOpen` (key met, or held) is what content reads (`resolveContent`, kits, Forms, props, map events, challenge runs, the codex's line); buildings, research, maps, and townsfolk lines read `isUnlocked`, so a held key opens content and nothing else. `tests/fixtures/saves/from-0.7.0-*.json` are profiles written by 0.7.0's own code, with what 0.7.0 let them play; `tests/x5-saves.test.js` asserts none of it is lost.
- **Reconcile is linear** in the save's size (Sets and Maps, no list scans inside loops, no argument spreads over a save's list). A save code is also refused when any list or table in it holds more than `MAX_SAVE_ENTRIES` (50,000; `save/codec.js`).
- **The checkpoint** (`app/resume.js`) is written as a run sets out (after what it used up is saved) and every minute. `createCheckpointer` packs only the frames since the last one (a chunk a minute; format v2: `{ config, decisions, chunks, frameBytes }`), so its cost doesn't grow with the run. It carries the gate's choice (`keptChoice`: character, map, mode, kit, Tide, curses, heirloom, challenge), which Run again uses after a resume. `createResumeOffer` is the title's Resume run: a tab that doesn't own the save never reads the slot, and an import drops the run and clears the slot.

## 13. Builds

### 13.1 Playtest build (web, Cloudflare)
- `vite build --mode web`. The package.json `build` script runs the test gate first, then Vite.
- The build writes `dist/version.json`. The page checks it with `cache: 'no-store'` on load, whenever the window regains focus or the tab becomes visible, every 5 minutes while the tab is in view, and whenever the game reaches the title or a quiet harbor (`app/version-check.js` `attachVersionCheck`, `checkAtSafePoints`). A newer build loads only at a safe point: never mid-run, never over the results.
- **If a new version appears during a run, the page does not reload mid-run.** It shows a small "update ready" marker and reloads at the next return to the hub or title screen. This departs from the brief's literal "reloads when it changes", because a reload mid-run would throw the run away (§20).
- **The harbor is a reload point only while it's idle:** no screen open (the results after a run, a building's panel, the gate, the menu) and no set piece running; the scene says when it becomes idle again, and a waiting update loads then. The results always get read. Before any reload, a save that failed is tried again, and the page stays if it still fails (§12.1). (Reopening the window for a display mode goes by the scene instead: at the title or anywhere in the harbor, since the settings themselves are a screen.)
- `public/_headers` makes `index.html` and `version.json` uncacheable, and makes hashed assets cache forever. Phase 1 confirms Workers static assets honor `_headers` after the first deploy. The fallback is a small Worker script (`main` plus `assets.run_worker_first` for those two paths in `wrangler.jsonc`) that sets the headers.

### 13.2 PC release (Electron)
- `vite build --mode desktop`: relative asset paths and no version check.
- `desktop/main.cjs`:
  - single-instance lock, taken before anything is set up: a copy without it registers nothing, opens no window, and reads no save (everything that starts the game is inside `if (HAS_LOCK)` in `main.cjs`)
  - `--smoke` always uses its own save folder (the one named, else a new temp folder), never the real one
  - the page is granted only clipboard writes and fullscreen (`shell.cjs allowsPermission`); a close question whose page reloaded or died is dropped, so the window's X always asks again
  - a fullscreen window by default (setting-driven), with a black background and no menu bar
  - `contextIsolation` on, `nodeIntegration` off, sandbox on
  - files served from a custom `app://` protocol instead of `file://`, which avoids module-loading and fetch quirks. The scheme is registered with `protocol.registerSchemesAsPrivileged` (standard, secure, fetch support) before the app is ready, then served with `protocol.handle`
  - F11 and Alt+Enter toggle fullscreen
  - the build number shows in the window title and on the title screen (brief §9.2)
  - display settings: **fullscreen** (default; Chromium's fullscreen is already borderless, there is no exclusive mode), **borderless window** (frameless, covering the display, not flagged fullscreen), and **windowed** (a whole multiple of 640×360, centered). Switching to or from borderless opens a new window, which reloads the page, so the settings screen offers it only at the title or hub. Vsync stays on (Chromium syncs `requestAnimationFrame` to the display)
  - every IPC call is checked to come from the game page itself; a page that fails to load or a renderer that dies shows a plain error page instead of leaving an invisible process
- `desktop/preload.cjs` exposes a narrow bridge through `contextBridge`: read and write the save and settings, write replays and reports, open the saves folder, toggle fullscreen, quit, and get the version. The preload is sandboxed and can't touch the file system, so every call goes over IPC to `main.cjs`, which does the actual file work.
- `tools/pack-desktop.mjs` runs the desktop Vite build, stages every `desktop/*.cjs` file plus the build, packages win32-x64 with asar and Windows metadata (name, version, and icon, all set without Wine: confirmed in Phase 1), zips it as `Lamplight-win-x64-v<version>.zip`, and checks the zip's contents. `--split-mb N` also cuts a split zip if a size limit ever blocks delivery.
- **Smoke test** (`tools/smoke/smoke.mjs`): the same app packaged for Linux, launched under Xvfb with a throwaway save folder. It plays itself for 30 seconds, saves through the real bridge (with a backup rotation), reloads the save, writes a replay and a report, and the main process checks those files exist on disk. It must pass before any .exe is sent. The Windows .exe itself can't be launched here, so Kevin's first launch is the Windows check.
- Delivery: about 150 MB zipped. Files sent in the chat are capped at 30 MB, so the zip goes as a split zip in 25 MB pieces (`.zip` plus `.z01`, `.z02`, ...), which Kevin opens with 7-Zip. The workspace tests the pieces with 7-Zip before sending (Phase 3).
- The .exe is unsigned, so SmartScreen warns on first launch ("More info", then "Run anyway"). Signing and Steam integration are later decisions and aren't built now.

---

## 14. Testing

### 14.1 The deploy gate (`tests/`, every Cloudflare build, under about 60 s total)
| Test | Checks |
|---|---|
| `boundary.test.js` | Import rules from §4; no forbidden globals or engine math (the `**` operator included) in `sim/`, `meta/`, `hub/`, `data/`, or `lib/` |
| `content.test.js` | Every shape; unique IDs; references resolve; hooks exist and get their params; nothing evolves; every weapon bare and with each of its lines, specials, and Form options runs 600 ticks cleanly and each piece changes what it does; ruling 16: nothing in the data fires from the dash and every dash item is plain; pierce within what a shot remembers; every tuning value finite; the harbor's tuning in its ranges; buildings (five levels, real costs, effects that only grow, a level's `needs`), research (tiers the Library opens, earlier requirements only, each adds something), the Lamp Post's upgrades (few, small, capped), every unlock key resolves, the MVP economy has no dead ends (map 1 pays for everything, and everything it drops has a use), Phase 7's content, every keeper line heard, a project's `runsOn` and a challenge's map real, and the harbor map (plots, doors, fog, the gate, the pier, and every walk fit together, §10.4) |
| `sim-core.test.js` | Pools and handles, the spatial hash against brute force, terrain collision and layouts, the flow field, the event buffer's drop rules |
| `ascend.test.js` | Forms and Embers (§5.16): three axes, two options each; leveling opens the first two at the 5th and 10th upgrade; the capstone opens by its deed unless the profile lacks it, and its partner passive makes it hit harder; an Ember opens an axis early or switches an option; the Stats screen's numbers; a run that takes, keeps, and spends Embers replays exactly; two Embers on one tick with one choice left |
| `forms.test.js` | Forms (§5.16): every line has an option on each axis, known mechanics (forms.js's or a character hook's), a real weapon, and names of its own; each does its own thing in a fight and nothing without it does; every full set a weapon can hold runs, changes the fight, and keeps the pools in bounds; the numbers they change; Skewer, Tether, Lunge, Pyre, Wisps, Vigil, Burning Glass, Fuel Line, and Great Toll up close |
| `weaponups.test.js` | How a weapon grows (§5.21): the sim's rarities are the data's; the odds are the tuning; Common and Uncommon are numbers and Rare and Epic its own lines; a number multiplies what the weapon does now; no card under the floor; every 3rd upgrade Rare or better, the 10th a Legendary, a line and a special once; a one-weapon draft never short, a Legendary never beside a number for the same weapon; `giveUpgrade`; the Tag bonus (`kinCount`) up to its cap |
| `guide.test.js` | The harbor's guide for a new player: its steps (the gate; the Lamp Post; back out through the gate, saying what's short, when a run brought home too little; then nothing), its marker (over the target, at the edge pointing to it, slid to the nearest clear end of a corner panel or the goal line and keeping its angle, moved off a panel that covers an on-screen target), the walk around the fog (`routeTo`, `routeExit`); walking off the quay (after `timing.walkOff`, once a push, not re-armed by a screen, on the gate's row and the one below and only there, only against the edge, not during a dash, open from a fresh profile) |
| `minimap.test.js` | The run's minimap: its place top right (off, Small, Large), no materials on the HUD, its marks (every drop kind, elites, bosses, you; no gems, plain enemies, dead elites, or what's outside the window; materials drawn under the rest; your slot kept), its whole-pixel window at both sizes and at the map's corners, uncovering exactly what the camera shows, fading while you're under it, its settings rows reaching the HUD live; and the real Pixi minimap and HUD in Node (the harbor rig's stand-ins): uncovering while Off, new ground sent at most every 150 ms, the clip, marks drawn, Whole map smoothed |
| `docks.test.js` | Phase 6 on the Drowned Docks: the layout's data checked in plain words (and a shortfall of slips or sheds an error), one door a shed, a bow narrower than the stern, the docks layout (the same every time, solid exactly where a kind is an obstacle, every kind present, every open tile reachable, the wreck closed but for its two-tile gap, no water run longer than a short slip), the run view's frame for each tile by kind and neighbors, the secret cache (opens once on reaching it, gives its relic or else one item, the reel, the notice, the state), the Wreck Lantern, the Bosun's Muster (captain, crew in a ring, the notice, a boss chest) and its timeline checks, the Maw's lure (drawn in, bitten; walking away escapes; never from off screen; a dash free of it; ended by its own Maw only), the Tidecaller's tide lanes (rows across you, each warned, staggered; the band between rows safe; a row hurting only after its own warning), boss data checks, the muster's crew all off screen, the Oil Flask landing on near and far targets, a Signal Mirror volley's beams on separate targets, lightning bolts only on a strike's own hits, the secret's meta (the codex key, its relic in the pools from then on, the results said once, kept through reconcile), and the dev warp |
| `player.test.js` | Movement, the dash (distance, one per press, cooldown, i-frames), walls, death and revival, regen, the XP curve, several levels in one tick; RunConfig, tuning, and InputFrame checks; the state hash's coverage |
| `combat.test.js` | Every enemy AI type, status effects, knockback, crowds, walls, teleport-ahead, contact; every emitter, motion, and on-hit effect; the starter weapons; gems, the magnet, pickups; boss attacks, phases, and chests |
| `stats.test.js` | The formula, caps, and tag scoping, against hand-computed tables |
| `drops.test.js` | Drop tables are valid; seeded expected values land within tolerance; a full pickup pool loses nothing the player earned |
| `draft.test.js` | The draft: 3 different cards from what can be taken; the early-weapon rule; reroll, banish, and skip (a skip pays what a dry level pays); passives to their max; the dry level's pay (food, Pearls, Coin, an Ember every 8th); the draft stream its own; relics never cards or from chests; chests (1 item from an elite, 1, 3, or 5 from a boss); a chest item and the bell's pick rolling weapon upgrades; Pick for me; slot rules; relic slots (a full pick swaps or passes, the one given up goes back to the Ferryman); relics with a downside; every relic and character hook named exists; the stun never adds up, a warned attack goes off on time under a stun or a freeze, and a warned charge runs its course; kills after deaths resolve still count |
| `meta.test.js` | Unlock keys, what building levels open, `resolveContent`, costs and their messages, restoring, upgrading, and research (each refusal with its sentence; the document passed in never changes), `applyRunResult` (materials, research ticking and finishing, the qualifying rule, the codex, bestiary milestones, the best time, junk results), the next three, a run's config (pools and upgrades as modifiers from 0:00, checked in a real world), completion, the fog, reconciling, every fixture save migrating, every writer (a run, a restore, an upgrade, a new project, both dev commands, and reconcile itself on every load, after the v1 migration too) leaving the rest of a seasoned profile alone, a hand-built profile missing a part still buying and unlocking (the part made new, never a crash), a project listed twice kept at the one further along (known or not, 'done' over running), only a boss this build has counting for research, research finished in a newer build coming back finished, and the harbor's dev commands (add materials, unlock everything: like a purchase, its changes shaped as a purchase's and a run's, marked in the save, a project this build doesn't know kept; a run that used the panel marks it too) |
| `marshharbor.test.js` | The Salt Marsh's harbor side (§10.6.1): which maps are open (every unlock key, `boss:` and `map:` keys), `runConfigFor`'s map and its refusal, a challenge on its own map, the map cards, the layers from the profile, Tides per map (a clear opens only that map's next, the Chapel's cap, `tide:<n>`, completion), the v3 → v4 migration and the v4 fixture, reconcile of v4's parts, research that counts runs on one map (and its words), the Well (the profile, the run config, the Ferryman's Well slot, the halt's view and a relic given), first sightings (notices once ever, the water's lines, the field guide, a real Marsh run's sightings into the profile), who arrives after a run, and the costs on the Marsh's materials. `hubscene.test.js` drives the gate's map cards, the Punter's boat after the results, the Well's screen, the Field guide, and the quiet line's first sightings |
| `workshop.test.js` | The Phase 7 buildings' purchases and rules (§10.6): gear made, worn, raised, and re-rolled (the same offer until one is taken); the Market's rates; run tools bought one at a time, slots by the Apothecary's level, food heals more at 2 and 4; Hunts and trophies (ruling 13); mastery's eleven ranks and their skill points; Harbor Orders' conditions and visibility; a run folded in; the run config's checks of every gate choice; the gate's six rows; reconcile and Next up for Phase 7's parts; every writer leaves the rest of the profile alone |
| `tasks.test.js` | The map's tasks in a run (§5.18, §5.20): a Wayside Lamp lights only while you stay in its ring and lights into its light with Pearls; one put off costs and pays more; Lantern Night banked, falling when the crowd closes in, its vault; lamps stay lit at a boss; the vault and the Reliquary's picks; a Fog Bell's small ring, crew, count, and chest; the Reliquary's gap, pity, and spread; Pearls; the Ferryman's calls, moorings, ring, and stock; a Tide's and curses' multipliers; the Heirloom from 0:00 (an old rune stone passed over); a kit; Spark Flint while you walk (never the dash), Barnacle Shell, Bloodied Hook, Drowned Bell, and the relics that were arcana; the signs, arrows, ledger, and first sightings; the Well's ring; a replay with all of it |
| `hub.test.js` | The harbor world: walking and the dash at the run's feel (the dash any way you last moved, the camera on you), walls, buildings, the fog, props, and the small lamps as walls, what's in reach, the keepers' walks, a ceremony (its camera gliding in and out, when the fog peels and the keeper and props appear), an arrival (the camera's glide, the newcomer stepping off from the bow's deck; one without a tile to wait at, refused plainly), skipping and queuing set pieces (a skip mid-peel ends the fog's fade; a skipped level's keeper and props land at once), nothing in reach during a ceremony even at a door, a keeper stepping out when the build is done, a prop reaching your edge pushing you clear at once, a dash held through a set piece not firing after it, last tick's positions kept for you and the people, each world's own timing read every tick (a tuned world's ceremony, build, and fog on its own clock, a ceremony retimed while it plays, an arrival's pans and walk, a keeper's pause, and the dash's cooldown; the live tuning holding every number to its range at both edges), the map's checks (no squeezing between two corners, a lamp or a prop off the map), and the same run of the harbor every time |
| `harbor.test.js` | The harbor's presentation: every tile, stage, prop, and townsperson on the atlas page (and every frame the hub view names), the path edges and fences, stages and lights by level, what a building shows and how its lights come up during a ceremony, the lamp's swell and the boat's shade, every light from the look (its colors as palette names or tints, the night's ambient and boost), an arrival's stand, halo, and bob, and the newcomer's hand-off at one height, the flicker's two waves, the light map at a point, a newcomer's shade and her boat's (lit alike where the fog is clear, in its shade in the fog) and when she's clear of it, the fog's clear mask and when it's drawn again, the fog at a point (smooth between tile middles), the camera's limits, the fog's density, and every sentence the panels, the next three, and the results say (a cleared map by its name); what a purchase plays out as |
| `hubview.test.js` | The harbor view itself, run on Pixi's scene graph with stand-ins for the GPU and the canvas (`tests/helpers/hubrig.js`), each part of the scene checked against the pure helpers it draws from: the layers' order, the light map and the fog each drawn into its layer's texture and cleared first every frame, the fog drawn whole and erased so each tile shows its density times the thickness, the fog redrawn every frame of a peel and once after, you and the people between ticks each hopping in their own time and a keeper fading in, each person, prop, and passenger in their own picture (people on their feet, props on their tiles, a building on its plot, the stages and the scaffold sorted by the building's front edge), the prompts placed from the camera, every light into the light map where harborLights puts it (flickering unless steady, the windows coming up through a ceremony, your lantern between ticks), a stage rising behind its mask (the town lamp's where the lamp stands) and the scaffold rising into place (its drawing on the plot, its outline a pixel past), a chimney smoking only once the last stage is up, an arrival from the boat out in the fog to her place on the square (cut at the gunwale, she and the boat lit as the light map would light them, over the boat with the hull over her feet and fading out as she walks clear, her waiting figure taking over, no light left where the boat's lantern was), every light on the glass its drawing has where the view stands each thing (every lit window's too), the look read live (the sea's swell, the wisps' drift, the camera's lift, a prop's pop, the puffs), every harbor number at the edges of its range (what must stay above 0 at a thousandth, a speed at a thousand) drawing a whole ceremony and arrival with nothing broken, a prop popping and a keeper fading in, and the sea and the wisps drifting, and the atlas left whole when the harbor goes |

a run's end is saved on its tick and the end screen's buttons go where they say (the run scene), a run is the app's config for it and Run again asks for the same character's again (never everything), the end screen ignores a press as it comes up, and a run that can't be folded in goes to the error screen and no end screen covers it; after Run again the results counting the runs and naming the map cleared; the error screen's words in a run and out of one; the layer's root carrying the view's unit and left edge; walking off the quay opens the gate and backing out with west still held doesn't reopen it; | `atlas.test.js` | The atlas builder without the packs: PNG reading and writing (palette images, damage, interlacing), mask drawing, tint (light, shade, holes, outline), layers, remap, the flash and elite variants, icon frames, the manifest's shape and Kevin's pack split, a whole build over stand-in packs (every entry gives its frames and variants; a bad entry, a spaced or short sheet, is named), the fingerprint's import walk, and the boss rigs (finite poses, the Maw's jaw drops, the lure stays on its stalk, the Tidecaller's bell swings harder when it rings, each attack's pose, the rig input read from a real boss, the rig's own light) |

It fails on a banned word or construction, an exclamation mark, an em dash, a lowercase tag, "Claude" outside the credits, a line narrating the player, "The" before a building, map, or character name (bosses keep theirs), "your" in a pick's description, a stat line without its sign, and a heading or button over four words; it checks its own checks on made-up lines |
| `interact-view.test.js` | The interactables' view, HUD, sound, rumble, and screens (0.8.2, §5.18): dashed rings (even dashes, gaps, each kind's color, never the warning color), the Ferryman rowing in, a bell's gathering and its wave, the edge arrows (on the edge toward the target, under the top line, off the HUD's boxes, spread apart, each pointing at its own, each kind its own mark), the map's row (its counts, the banked glow, on screen and clear of the minimap at every setting), Pearls' words, each kind's sound and the count's climb, rumble by kind (the pad in use, one shake at a time, off when switched off, Settings' defaults), the Settings rows, the notices word for word, the Fog Bell's pick (its view, a dozen cards, the pick), the relic pool line, the Ferryman's found relics left off, the ledger (rows, the pause line, the end), the gate's line of what a map holds, How to play |
| `view.test.js` | The HUD's layout at every minimap setting (nothing overlaps; see §6.2 item 13). The run view's memory fed by real worlds: which events make which effects, warnings ending on the tick their hit lands, the dying keeping their facing, bounded stores, the tuned damage-number limit, enemy looks (dazed included); animation on sim time (frames staggered and held, the hop, the daze wobble) and a source check that the run view calls those helpers with each enemy's phase; the floor's variants (no pattern, the right mix, one picker for the run and the title); what the HUD says, and that no two HUD pieces overlap at their longest |
| `uistack.test.js` | The menu stack's routing with a stand-in document: the input delay, released buttons on open and close, one Esc doing one thing, closing a screen that isn't on top, menu sounds |
| `runlogic.test.js` | Hit-stop, sounds per event, what each halt's screen shows, the pause and end summaries, the report event log, and a replay file and report that play back to the same state |
| `lib.test.js` | The RNG (golden sequences for every named stream, forks, state round trips, seed checks), `lib/fmath.js` against the engine's math and its bit-identical golden values, and the hashes against published test vectors |
| `input.test.js` | Glyphs from real controller ids, the deadzone, the DirectInput table for Sony pads, keyboard and pad movement, presses and holds (skip, the tuning panel's hold), menu repeat, devices coming and going, released buttons between screens, focus loss |
| `loop.test.js` | The fixed 60 Hz loop: steps per simulated second at any frame rate, alpha, stalls clamped, pause reasons, hit-stop, stop and start from inside a frame, a throwing step or render |
| `pauses.test.js` | When losing the controller or the window's focus pauses the game, and what resumes it |
| `app.test.js` | The screen layout at the common resolutions and display scalings, the scene manager (order, queued transitions, errors, safe points), the version check (reloads only at a safe point, never twice for one build, heals when stuck), the progress keeper (a failed save kept and shown, nothing leaves the page unsaved, checked after the flush, a settings write failing on the way out not holding the player), and the game's flow (`app/flow.js`: a run's end folded in and saved rotating the backups, then its replay, Run again's news added up and shown once, a run's config from the profile, the title's first button to the harbor, the window reopened only settled at the title or in the harbor, and every way off the page through the keeper: Quit and the error's reload ask, an update's reload and the display switch stay; closing the page another way asks while a save still fails, but not after a way off that asked); and a source check that `main.js` wires the scenes, the title, the error screen, settings, and updates through the flow and the keeper (each wire as a piece of code, its spacing and line breaks aside) |
| `desktop.test.js` | The Electron shell's pure helpers: app:// paths kept inside the game folder, the bridge only for our page, window sizes under display scaling, no restart loops, display modes, the title's build stamp, IPC payload checks, content types, the error page escaped, and the question when the window closes while a save still fails (the game's own words, Stay by default; asked by the main process from what the page last said, not twice after the game's Quit asked, and once for one close) |
| `workflow.test.js` | The build and deploy config: tests before the build, the quoted glob, every test file real, minimal runtime dependencies, wrangler serving `dist/`, caching headers, Node 22 said in package.json's engines (a GitHub web upload drops dotfiles, so nothing the build or gate needs is one), the desktop and tools packages kept apart, the Content-Security-Policy |
| `determinism.test.js` | Same seed and inputs produce the same state hash at 60 s, twice; a recorded run replays tick for tick, and so does a Harpooner run with enemies dazed on hundreds of ticks; a replay from another build is refused; the random streams are separate; and a fixed run on a frozen copy of the data (`tests/fixtures/sim-golden-data.json`) matches a stored hash, so only sim code or the math under it can move it (this catches math drift between engines; `tools/smoke` also runs it inside the packaged Electron) |
| `characters.test.js` | The six characters (§5.22): each with a signature in slot 0, a trade, a drawback, a starting dash item, and a tree of 13; nothing of theirs fires from the dash; the track (an upgrade from the clock, trade upgrades never closer than the gap at any kill rate, the past-the-wait bonus to its top); each trade's verb (the Lamplighter's flames and lanterns, the Harpooner's hook and the Grapnel's catch, the Punter's Momentum and its reach, the Bog Warden's raising and her dead's guard, the Drowned Man's crate, the Tide Priest's called beat and no healing); loops at past 600 kills a minute; replays with skills; her dead's own row in the results; every signature Form option's zones; numbers in text read from the data, and a typed digit fails |
| `skills.test.js` | The skill trees (ruling 15): 12 to 15 a character, two builds, three forks, needs, two order skills; points at their ranks, the last late; taking, forks, needs, orders, and no points; a reset gives every point back for materials; a save's trained perks paid back once |
| `sig-balance.test.js` | The signatures' benches (rulings 19 KH, KI): no whole option set over 2.5x its signature's median set (`fixtures/sig-sets.json`), option B everywhere still 12% or more of the character's damage (`fixtures/sig-bshare.json`), each fixture stamped with the options it was benched on; the trade pays at most two upgrades in any span |
| `forms-balance.test.js` | The drafted weapons' benches (rulings 17 W2, 18 WA): every option within 2x of its axis partner and none under x1.15 (a control option no loss on the tank ruler), every whole set within 2.5x its weapon's median (`fixtures/form-bench.json`) |
| `lines.test.js` | Every drafted weapon's Rare and Epic lines are behavior, not stats alone (ruling 19 RB) |
| `orders.test.js` | The Harbor Orders board: every condition read from the result (an unknown one never holds), each order opening one thing, judged from the first run and held until the Tavern keeps the board, old saves keeping what they earned; rarity names; Tide clears; the Lighthouse's ten stages |
| `c3.test.js`, `c3-weapons.test.js` | The rebuild's presentation (C3): each growth and character event's own sound and rumble, no two weapons firing alike, dash items' sounds, a look for every capstone, the HUD's meters and two boss bars, the damage shrink said in the glossary; weapon orders (three a weapon, each opening one real thing), the codex weapon page with its ticks, `legendaryEarly`, Both Ways |
| `glossary.test.js` | The glossary (W7, RULING-L1): every entry well formed and 40 words or fewer, the terms Kevin listed, every term a screen uses has an entry (and the check catches one that doesn't), the screen by group, the title, pause, and harbor buttons, and its button opening the focused element's term |
| `presentation.test.js` | Brightness, the ground's lift, enemy family tints, damage numbers kept off the player and thinned in a crowd, warning tiers drawn, the music's loops in their modes, each weapon's own fire sound, combat rumble |
| `events.test.js` | Every `EV.<name>` used is defined, no two share a code, each fits a byte |
| `formtool.test.js` | `tools/bots/forms.mjs`'s trial is sound |
| `firsthour.test.js` | The first hour (W7): the results' words, Ready purchases first, settings rows in plain sentences, tips over the things themselves, first sightings held until the enemy is on screen, the death recap, the one Boss chest screen |
| `runpass.test.js`, `gamerpass.test.js` | 0.7.3's rebuilt gamer pass, kept current: starters, XP past 40, the Docks' elites and overtime, the next chest, the Reliquary, `minFight`, Lantern Night's scaling; time-survived pay, the Market's row, Next up, the guide, mastery's ranks, merged results |
| `sweep-x1.test.js`, `sweep-x2.test.js`, `sweep-x3.test.js`, `x4.test.js`, `x6.test.js` | The sweep's batches (§10.9, §10.10, §5.23, §7, §8): one thing one name one description, numbers in the glossary read from the data; arcana, tonics, challenges, and the bestiary retired and a save's share paid back, the six-row gate, level gates; overtime's pay and drops; the Punter's punt, the overtime flood, the dawn, the threat, the Brine Vat; the Confirm and Back swap, held movement keys, the close reason, rumble while paused, the paste box |
| `marsh.test.js`, `marsh-fight.test.js`, `marsh-view.test.js` | The Salt Marsh (§5.17): its layout, the water and rule 8, the storm, the Well, thralls; its enemies and bosses; its ground, art, and what the run view draws |

The gate never asserts timings. Cloudflare's build machines vary, and a flaky gate blocks deploys. Claude measures the gate's total time in the workspace before every zip and trims tests if it passes 45 s. On Nov 20 it was 1,062 tests (the golden hash the only red) and took 5 min 12 s on a machine with other builders' runs going; whether it still fits the 60 s budget of §1 and §17 on a quiet machine is unchecked since it passed 1,000 tests (open in HANDOFF).

### 14.2 Workspace-only checks (run by Claude before zips)
| Tool | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `tools/soak` | A full run at full density with an invulnerable bot: both bosses killed, then 10 minutes of overtime, at the game's own pace (0.8.1: up to the 450 alive cap) or, with `--neutral`, the 0.8.0 pace up to the 3,000-enemy cap, which keeps the engine checked at 1,000+. Reports ms per tick by how many enemies were alive (against §17), garbage per tick, memory growth, and pool high-water marks, and fails on any budget miss, pool refusal, or a boss left alive. Run for both characters before every zip. The clock (0.9.0 release): wall time, on a quiet machine (1-minute load average under 1.0; otherwise it waits up to 5 minutes for the load to drop, and if it never does it judges by CPU time with 1 ms added to each per-tick budget and says so); both clocks are always reported. Not CPU time alone (0.9.0 W7/V5): the process's CPU time counts V8's background compiler threads, which recompile the big step functions on other cores for much of the run, charged to whatever tick they overlap (the release Marsh soak: p99 2.0 to 2.6 ms CPU against 0.84 to 1.0 ms wall on the same run). A player's machine compiles on other cores too, without holding up a frame |
| `tools/bots` | `pace.mjs` (0.8.1) measures the combat's pace on both maps with the pacing report's run configs: bots that can't die (enemies alive on average and at the peak, kills, XP, and materials a minute, the levels at 10:00 and 20:00, each ordinary enemy's hits and seconds from first hit to death by your level, and what the kill-counted things see: Bloodied Hook streaks, kills near you, Reliquaries) and bots that can (the clear rate, damage taken a minute alive); `--weapons` each base weapon alone for 9 minutes (its kills and damage a minute, its hits to kill each kind by minute); `--crowd` a Flare Gun standing still in the crowd (how soon a crowd of each size holds for 10 s: the Watchman); `--neutral` sets every dial as 0.8.0 had it, and `--tune path=value` turns one. `harpooner-trials.mjs` plays the Harpooner trials of §20 call 44 again (her Phase 3 kit, holding her ground with and without armor for it, twice the crowd armor, and the daze, on the same seeds) and prints the table in HANDOFF.md. `forms.mjs` measures each Form against its weapon without one (a bot that can't die, 12 minutes, one Ember at 1:00 for each Form on trial; the weapon's own damage and its own kills, read from the kill events' killer slot), and all three of a line's Forms together, which is how 0.7.2 toned them down. `bots.mjs`: balance sweeps: N runs per character and per **build archetype** (orbit tank, projectile storm, burning-ground kiter, summoner, chain lightning, speed glass cannon), spread over worker threads. Reports survival time, how many runs cleared and killed the first boss, deaths by minute, the level at 10:00 and at the end, and kills. v1 (Phase 2) plays one careful bot (`sim/bot.js`: it scores 16 directions by danger and wants, circles the crowd at weapon range, fights bosses up close between their attacks, and dashes out of trouble) with draft preferences per archetype; DPS by minute and per-archetype play styles come with the balance phase. This is how the brief's "6 archetypes viable at the top difficulty" gets checked |
| `tools/pacing` | The progression simulator from §10.3 (`--skilled`: bots that can't die, a practiced player's pace, kept in `yields-skilled.json`): bots measure what a run from a fresh profile brings home (`measure.mjs`; kept in the repo in `tools/pacing/yields.json` with the fingerprint of the code that measured them, `yields.mjs`), then 40 simulated players each play until the harbor has nothing left, through the real meta layer (`model.mjs`), and the report (`report.mjs`, which the gate runs on the committed yields) says what it stands on, then gives each milestone's median hours and range against its target, the runs between new things, the MVP hours on the shortest third of the runs, and the fresh profile's share of the run content |
| `tools/bots`, the rebuild's | `runaway.mjs` (ruling 19 RA, 21 WB): careful runs to 20:00 on fixed seeds, every character, both maps, option A, B, or both, `--tide 0|5`, `--jobs N`; prints each run's top weapon over the next and fails a weapon and set over x4 in more than one run. `builds.mjs`: the kite bot following the arrows from a harbor stage's profile, six archetypes; kills by minute, levels, Forms, rarities. `threat.mjs`: the threat curve as a `ref` line. Not in the repo, in `/mnt/user-data/outputs/work/cw/`: C1's `runc1.mjs` (one character's run, measured; `--pick careful|random|plain`, `--tide`, `--seeds`, `TREE=` the tree to read, `PATCH=` a data patch), `resume.sh`, `pair.mjs`, `top.mjs`, `held.mjs`, `bench.mjs`, `formfixture.mjs`; C2's `tools/sigsets.mjs` and `sigfixture.mjs`. HANDOFF.md has the commands and the measurement rules (Ruling 22) |
| `tools/shots` | Headless Chromium screenshots of scenes at set moments, so Claude can check visuals by looking (`--query "shot=levelup&busy=1"`: title, levelup, chest, pause, settings, tuning, end, or run; busy adds a crowd; `jump=596` starts late, to meet a boss; `char=harpooner`; the harbor: hub, building, gate, results, ceremony, arrival, with `levels=library:3,...`). `shot=loop` plays the whole loop through the real screens and saves (restore the Lamp Post, its ceremony, set out from the gate with the profile's own pools, leave, back to the harbor, the results) and fails if any step doesn't happen. The renderer is software, so these say nothing about frame rate. `hint-check.mjs` (X6) opens 23 screens at 100, 125, and 150% text at 1280x720 and fails when a title or hint row leaves the view or a panel scrolls; `gate-check.mjs` and `skills-check.mjs` (C3) do the same for the gate's two steps and every skill tree (each needs a built copy: `npx vite build` first, or `BUILD=`). `harbor-probe.mjs` measures, on that renderer with the page's clock stopped between frames, the fog's share of the picture at known densities and a newcomer's brightest pixel tick by tick across her step off the boat |
| `tools/atlas` | Builds the atlas from the packs (`build.mjs`; `--check` says whether the committed one is current), writes a 4x contact sheet of every frame to look at, `rig-preview.mjs` draws each boss rig in its poses, and `hub-preview.mjs` draws the harbor at any building levels, by day or by night |
| `tools/replay` | Plays a report or replay file Kevin attached and prints the run's state at the end or at `--until m:ss`. A replay starts from the RunConfig the run started with, and records each tick's input before stepping, so a crash reproduces at its tick |
| `tools/smoke` | The desktop smoke test from §13.2 |
| `tools/golden` | `freeze.mjs` rewrites the golden run's frozen data (`tests/fixtures/sim-golden-data.json`); then set `GOLDEN.hash` in `tests/helpers/golden.js` to the hash the determinism test reports. Done once a release, after the last sim change |
| `tools/marsh` | `rule8.mjs`: the Marsh's rule 8 swept whole (§5.17): every standable tile at 5 water levels (its chain home 4-connected and its hold complete, every 97th also put on the ground and walked), and six 6-minute overtime walks with pillars raised around you every 2 s, a walk home checked after every change of water. About 30 s; exit 1 on a failure. `tools/soak --map salt_marsh` soaks the Marsh (its report adds the searches and ground rebuilds) |
