## 19. Session protocol

**Starting a phase:** a new chat on the setting from §18. Attach the latest `lamplight` zip and say "Run phase N." The session then:
1. Reads `BRIEF.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `HANDOFF.md`, and `DECISIONS.md` completely.
2. Confirms the setting fits the phase. If the work turns out to need a higher setting (for example, a Sonnet session hits a sim-core bug), it stops and says so rather than pushing through.
3. Posts a task list.

**Before every zip, at the end of a phase or not** (tuning rounds and bug fixes included):
- Run the gate, the production build, `npx wrangler deploy --dry-run`, and `tools/soak`.
- If a file was deleted or renamed, list the exact paths Kevin must delete in GitHub, because a zip can only add and replace. Avoid deletions and renames where possible, and record pending deletions in `HANDOFF.md` until Kevin confirms them.

**Ending a phase:**
1. Runs the checks above. For milestone phases (1, 3, 6, and later on request) it also packages the .exe and runs the smoke test.
2. **Drift check:** compares the work against the phase's row in §18 and against the brief's hard rules (§2 of the brief), and says plainly if anything drifted.
3. Updates `HANDOFF.md` (state, what's done, what's next, questions for Kevin) and appends new builder's calls to `DECISIONS.md`, each with date and phase, so Kevin can veto them.
4. Bumps the version (`0.<phase>.<n>`).
5. Delivers **one** repo zip, the newest only. Until Kevin's first push, every zip is the full repo, because the baseline hasn't been reset yet. After he says "delta the repo," zips contain only files changed since then. Zips stay at 100 files or fewer and 25 MB per file; a larger set is split into numbered parts with the push order stated. When the test script changes, the zip carries every test file it names.
6. The .exe zip, when there is one, is a separate delivery.

**Split uploads:** each GitHub web upload is one commit, and each commit triggers a Cloudflare build. When a delivery must be split, a build triggered by an early part can fail. A failed build doesn't deploy, so the last good version keeps serving (Phase 1 confirms this in Cloudflare's documentation). Kevin's first push will likely be a split, because the repo will pass 100 files around Phase 3. That's harmless: there is no previous deployment to lose, and the build after the last part is the one that counts.

The workspace may be wiped between sessions, so the zip is the only state that carries over. Nothing important lives only in the workspace, except raw art packs, which Kevin re-attaches when needed.

---

## 20. Builder's calls for Kevin to confirm or overturn

Each is also logged in `DECISIONS.md`. Silence means accepted. A call describes the game when it was made: many below name systems that are gone (runes, evolutions, arcana, consumables as such, Kindled, mastery perks, Form progress, the signature in the draft), and a later call or ruling says so. From ruling 14 (Nov 16) on, the rulings and calls are logged in DECISIONS.md only, with the builders' own calls in their logs under `/mnt/user-data/outputs/work/cw/` and `work/lang/`.

1. A web playtest build exists alongside the .exe, purely for fast iteration.
2. Plain JavaScript, not TypeScript.
3. DOM menus over a Pixi world and HUD.
4. The world renders at 640×360 and scales by whole numbers. Lighting is soft; sprites are crisp.
5. Dash is in the feel prototype, on by default, with a tunable cooldown and distance. It gives brief invulnerability, and it's never an attack.
6. The player keeps all materials on death.
7. Research advances only on runs that reach 5:00 or kill a boss.
8. The MVP buildings are the Lamp Post, the Library, and the Tavern.
9. Maps are bounded at about 256×256 tiles (4,096 px square, about 6 screens across) with a fog wall at the edge.
10. Relics come from elite and boss chests plus a rare draft card. (Overturned by Kevin in Phase 7: Reliquaries, the vault, the Ferryman, and secrets instead, §10.8 and call 82.)
11. A new version never reloads the playtest build mid-run. It waits for the hub or title screen. (This departs from the brief's literal wording.)
12. Enemies don't y-sort among themselves.
13. Skip on the level-up screen is a hold, so it can't happen by accident.
14. The Library starts with one research slot, and later levels add more.
15. "Lamplight" is the working theme. Swapping it is cheapest before Phase 6, because content names come from the theme.
16. Phases 4 (art) and 5 (meta and hub) run before the feel gate, while Kevin is away. Content still waits for the gate (§18).
17. Prestige also keeps character unlocks, codex discoveries, feats, lifetime stats, and settings, on top of the brief's list (§10.7).
18. Materials are assigned to maps per §10.5, so everything the MVP needs drops on map 1.
19. The engine's "Curse" stat is named `density`, so it can't be confused with the Chapel's Curses.
20. Cross-engine determinism uses the sim's own math functions (`lib/fmath.js`) instead of the engine's trig.
21. The tuning panel opens on a 1-second hold of Create (View on Xbox), not the Create + Options chord, so it can't also trigger Pause (Phase 1).
22. Display mode changes to or from borderless reopen the window (and reload the page), so they're offered only at the title or hub (Phase 1).
23. Weapons that fire the way you face bend their shots onto the nearest enemy within 40° of that direction (§5.8). The player still never aims (Phase 2).
24. Contact damage comes from the hardest-hitting enemy touching you, then 0.5 s of invulnerability; a crowd jostles you at most 9 px a second, and bosses and warned enemies don't budge (Phase 2).
25. Enemies gain 12% of base health and 3% of contact damage per minute, in a straight line; overtime compounds on top; bosses keep their listed health (Phase 2).
26. The last boss's chest opens on the spot, before the stay-or-leave choice (§5.11, Phase 2).
27. Nothing earned is lost to a full pool: big gems past 1,500, materials straight into the haul (§5.3, Phase 2).
28. Swarm walls vanish without counting as kills once they're far off screen (Phase 2).
29. Tuning changes and dev commands are logged decisions, so a run tuned mid-way still replays exactly (Phase 3).
30. The ground is a live layer of the tiles in view, not pre-baked chunks (§6.2, Phase 3).
31. Fog v1 is drifting noise with the lights erased out of it, with no custom shader (§6.2, Phase 3).
32. The menus use Jersey 10 (a free pixel font, SIL Open Font License); the HUD and damage numbers use a code-drawn 5×7 font (Phase 3).
33. Until the hub exists (Phase 5), a run's materials and totals go straight into the profile, so nothing earned in playtests is lost (Phase 3; since Phase 5, `applyRunResult` does this, and the results show in the harbor).
34. A minimal title screen (the lampyard behind it), a character choice, and Credits, so the feel gate can start runs; Phase 5 builds the real title and results (Phase 3; since Phase 5 the title's Start or Continue goes to the harbor, and you choose who sets out at the gate).
35. Warnings are drawn above the light and fog, so a hazard is never lost in the dark (§6.2, Phase 3).
36. The web build's Save report downloads the file (Phase 3).
37. The atlas ships in `src/assets/` (Vite fingerprints the PNG) instead of `public/atlas/`, so a page can never load an atlas from another build (§16.3, Phase 4).
38. The characters come from Tiny Dungeon: the Lamplighter is its tile 99 (recolored to an amber dress and auburn hair, with a lantern drawn in her hand), the Harpooner tile 100 (white hair, a red coat, a harpoon drawn upright in her hand) (Phase 4).
39. The enemies come from the 1-Bit Pack: the fog wisp (419), drowned sailor (471), bloater (367), bilge rat (423), lantern eater (271, a moth), wreck gunner (74), and drowned bosun (466). The gull and the mud crab are drawn in code, animated, because the pack has no bird in flight and no crab that reads as one (Phase 4).
40. Elites stay twice the size, pixels still square, with a dark and a gold line around them. It is the one place the world mixes pixel sizes: a bigger silhouette is what says "this one carries a chest" (Phase 4).
41. The bosses are rigs drawn in code: the Harbor Maw an anglerfish whose lure glows like a lamp (it lures lamplighters), the Tidecaller a hooded figure ringing a green bronze bell on a crook, with tentacles under its robe (Phase 4).
42. Icons are 18 x 18: a 16 px picture (mostly from the 1-Bit Pack) on a dark square, framed in the item's tag color, and tinted in that tag's colors (Phase 4).
43. The palette gains a skin ramp and a wood ramp (for the characters, props, and later the hub) (Phase 4).
44. The Harpooner: what she hits is dazed for 0.4 s (it can't walk or hurt her; bosses are immune), in place of Kevin's suggested bonus for standing still, which was tested and dropped. In the bot trials (`tools/bots/harpooner-trials.mjs`: 12 runs of up to 24 minutes per version, same seeds), no standing-still version beat plain kiting (14.5 minutes alive on average): holding her ground in the crowd she reels in gave 10.2 to 12.3 minutes with armor, healing, or extra damage for standing still, and standing still only when nothing was near gave 11.0 to 13.2. The game also gives too many reasons to move. The daze took her to 21.3 minutes and 3 deaths in 12 (from 9). In the standard 24-minute sweep she clears 8 of 12 and dies in 1, against the Lamplighter's 7 and 3 (§5.12, Phase 4).
45. The atlas's source fingerprint ignores comments and spacing that can't change the code, so editing a comment or running a formatter never asks for a rebuild that needs the art packs (Phase 4).
46. The player is drawn above the light, the fog, and the warnings, so she is never hidden by them; she no longer sorts behind elites and bosses (§6.2, Phase 4).
47. An enemy in the middle of a warned attack (a crab winding up or dashing, a gunner aiming, a boss warning or charging) is neither dazed nor frozen: holding it would stop its attack's clock, and it would strike after its warning had ended. Its attack goes off when the warning said, and a boss's charge, once warned, runs its course (§5.12, §5.13, §16.4, Phase 4).
48. A dazed enemy reels (a quick sway) instead of dimming, because a dimmed enemy would break the contrast rule for enemies (§6.4, §16.4, Phase 4).
49. The Lamp Post's upgrades (the capped stat tree): ten upgrades over five tiers, one tier a level; each rank costs its first rank times the rank; at most 25% from any one of them at its last rank (a percent, a "more", or a flat bonus against its base), except Second Wind, the last tier's one revival, by design. They are the one place the harbor adds bigger numbers (§10.4, Phase 5).
50. The Library's research (tier 1 to 3, one to three projects at a time with its levels): seven projects that put things into your runs (the Lantern Orb, the Tide Bell and Undertow, runes, the Ballast, the Almanac and the Lucky Knot, the Backfire rune, Volatile Gems). A project finishes after its number of runs that last 5:00 or kill a boss (§10.2, §10.6, Phase 5).
51. The Tavern brings the Harpooner at level 1 (she arrives by boat) and the four relics at levels 2 to 5 (§10.4, Phase 5).
52. You walk the harbor as the Lamplighter; the other characters you have wait on the square, and you choose who sets out at the gate at the quay's west end (or from the harbor's menu, or "Set out again" on the results) (§10.4, Phase 5).
53. The harbor's keepers are Old Wick (the Lamp Post), Silas (the Library; the pack's townsman in a long ink-violet coat, since its robed scholar read as a wizard; the librarian is a man where the first draft had a woman, because the pack's two women are the playable characters), and Bram (the Tavern). Each stands by the door from level 4 and walks the streets from level 5 (§10.4, Phase 5).
54. The fog is a wall: you can't walk into a region that hasn't cleared, and a building comes out of it all at once (never half fogged) (§10.4, Phase 5). Kevin had it taken out in 0.6.0, then, seeing it was how the town shows its progress, had it put back (0.6.1). The night stays the lighter dusk he asked for, so what's cleared is easy to see.
55. Props appear as the town comes back (a well, a tub, a cart on the square; a cart by the Library; barrels by the Tavern), and they are solid (§10.4, Phase 5).
56. A ruin is drawn as the building looked down into with its roof gone (the pack has no ruins), and level 1 puts a scaffold over it before level 2 puts the roof back (§10.4, §16.5, Phase 5).
57. The boat bringing someone in is drawn over the fog, its lantern showing through, so the camera following it never shows a screen of fog (§10.4, Phase 5; back with the fog in 0.6.1).
58. The run's end screen offers "Back to the harbor" (the results show there) and "Run again" (straight out, the results already saved) (§10.2, Phase 5).
59. The first costs, from the pacing simulator with the bots' yields (a fresh profile's run, measured through the 20:00 boss: about 22 minutes, 141 Coin, 81 Oil, 44 Drowned Pages, 36 Timber, 23 Rope, 12 Rum, 4 Iron, 2 Map Scraps): the first building level at the end of run 1, the Harpooner at run 2, the first research at run 2, the MVP harbor fully restored in about 7 hours (5.6 to 8.6). The bots last longer than a new player will, so real play will be slower: on the shortest third of the bots' runs it is about 18 hours. The costs are one data file, and Kevin's own first runs set them (§10.3, Phase 5).
60. A fresh profile's pools hold 42% of the run content (10 of 24 weapons, passives, relics, and runes), above the brief's 30%. It falls as the content passes add more behind research and buildings; until then, a first run with fewer choices than this felt too thin to be fair to the combat (§10.6, Phase 5).
61. Every word the player reads follows `docs/WRITING.md`: labels and stat lines as the genre writes them (`+1 Pierce`, `-0.15s Cooldown`, `Survived 7:11`), names in the data without "The" except the bosses', flavor only on materials, enemies, and the keepers' speech, and no screen that names Claude (Kevin, 0.5.0's screenshots: "it sounds completely AI"; the language pass, 0.5.1). Since 0.7.1 a system is also explained in a plain sentence where a player first meets it, a keeper comes with their role (`Ned, the dockhand`), a building takes "the" mid-sentence, and a refusal says what to do (`Needs the Library at Lv 2`) (Kevin, 0.7.0's Docks panel: "who the heck is Ned?"; WRITING §2.5).

62. The Drowned Docks is built by its own layout kind (`docks`): slips, footbridges, sheds, crates, and a wreck from one seed, every open tile reachable. The water is solid like the crates; swarmers still fly over it. The slips are basins with open wharf all round, not channels across the map: with channels every 32 tiles the bots' runs fell from about 22 minutes to 12 (pinned against the water); with basins they average about 19 (§5.7, Phase 6).
63. The elite event is the **Bosun's Muster** at 15:00: the Drowned Bosun as an elite captain with 24 Drowned Sailors in a ring around him, and the captain pays a boss chest (so a third evolution can come mid-run). It replaces the 14:00 sailor ring; the Lantern Eater's elite moves to 17:00 (§5.7, Phase 6).
64. The MVP secret is the Wreck Cache: a cache aboard a wreck in the map's far northeast, about 35 seconds' walk from the start in a straight line (far longer through a crowd), with no marker. It gives the Wreck Lantern (every 5th kill leaves burning oil), and finding it once puts the relic in the pools for good (§5.7, Phase 6).
65. The new weapons (Signal Mirror, Oil Flask, Lightning Rod, Corpse Candles) come from research, two at tier 1, one each at tiers 2 and 3, and have no evolutions yet: the MVP's 4 evolutions are the starting four's. Their evolutions come with the content passes (Phase 6).
66. The bosses' own attacks, the lure and the tide lanes, sit in each boss's rotation beside the shared ones rather than replacing them (§5.13, Phase 6).
67. A fresh profile's share of the run content falls to 10 of 29 (34%), still above the brief's 30% (call 60; Phase 6).
68. The costs of every building level after the first, and of every upgrade rank, are 1.5 times Phase 5's (1.15 in 0.6.0; raised in 0.6.1 because ascensions add to what a strong run kills and brings home) (rare materials rounded up), which puts the bots' MVP harbor at 8.3 hours (6.6 to 9.9), inside the brief's 6 to 10. The bots' yields swing a lot between measurements (one measurement on Phase 5's costs gave 5.2 hours, the next 9 or so), so this is a rough fit. Kevin's own runs still set them (call 59; §10.3, Phase 6).

69. Embers and ascensions (§5.16) are Phase 7 systems pulled forward at Kevin's ask (0.6.1): three ascensions a weapon line, every drafted weapon has a line, and evolutions share their base's. The Ember drops are 1 a boss, 1 a muster captain, and 15% an elite, so a run that reaches 20:00 sees about 3 to 5.
70. The minimap uncovers the ground as you see it rather than showing the whole map from the start, so the wreck and its cache stay a secret until you've been near (§6.2).
71. Stats lives in the pause menu: your character's numbers, what reaches every weapon, and each weapon's own with its DPS and Total Damage (§8; 0.6.2 changed "dealt this run" to these, call 78).
72. The harbor's guide covers the first steps only (the gate; then the Lamp Post, or back out through the gate when a run brought home too little for it); after that the Next up panel already says what's within reach (§10.4).
73. The minimap's "small icons" are small shapes, a few pixels each, one per kind of thing (a hollow red diamond for an elite, a filled one for a boss, a hollow amber box for a chest, and so on; §6.2 item 13), rather than the drops' own sprites: at 1 to 2 px a tile a 16 px sprite would cover a sixth of the map. Kevin can ask for the sprites, drawn small.

74. Forms replace 0.6.1's ascensions (Kevin, 0.6.2): three a line, one for each part of how a weapon works (how it fires, how it flies, what it leaves), so all three stack on one weapon; the Ember screen picks a weapon, then its Form; the evolution keeps them (§5.16). Their numbers are first guesses for playtesting: Kevin hasn't played them yet.
75. Burning Glass sets fire (what it leaves) and counts the beam finding the same enemy within 2.5 s (not "held in the beam"): the Signal Mirror's beam lasts a quarter second every 1.5 to 1.8 s, so a hold window shorter than its cooldown would never build. Great Toll's kills can take at most half the wait off the next ring: uncapped, a 1,000-enemy crowd had it ringing every tick.
76. Three Forms were renamed so no name is shared: Breakers (not Undertow, a passive), Full Circle (not Lighthouse, beside Lighthouse Lens), Vigil (not Wake, beside the Reaper's Wake relic). The gate checks it.
77. Planted Rod's strikes only fall within its ring (150 px): with nothing near the rod it waits, and walking 260 px off replants it where you stand. That is the Form's trade (strikes you place, not strikes that find enemies).
78. Stats shows each weapon's DPS as its Total Damage over the seconds since it was taken (kept through level-ups and the evolution), a whole number past 10 and one decimal under it (§8).

79. The six Phase 7 buildings restore with map 1's materials (Timber, Rope, Iron, Oil, Drowned Pages, Rum, Map Scraps, Coin) rather than the brief's Salt Glass, Candles, Bone, and Marsh Herbs, which have no map yet: a cost nothing drops would be a dead end. Their costs move as those maps arrive (§10.5).
80. The Docks run the Ferryman until there are maps to sail to; each map's boat joins the Docks' levels when that map is built (§10.5, §10.8).
81. Gear's slots are the brief's four (Lantern, Coat, Boots, Charm) plus a Ring and a Pendant. Every recipe's own affix changes the build (a tag's projectiles or pierce, a hook, a rule), never a flat stat; stats come only as the rolled extras; a rule affix only on a Charm, Ring, or Pendant (§10.6).
82. Relics as Kevin set them (three tiers, carry everything, never from level-ups or chests, actives on a dash, a hit taken, a kill streak), from a Reliquary, Lantern Night's vault, and the Ferryman. The Reliquary's numbers (a 0.06% chance a kill, at least 150 s apart, certain after 1,800 kills) and the tier weights by minute are first guesses for playtesting. Two plain Trinkets (Gull Feather, Brass Button) start in the pool so a first run's Reliquary has something to offer (the fresh share stays at 34%, 12 of 35, call 60).
83. Sea Glass left at a run's end is lost unless the Market buys it (half a Coin each from its level 2, one from level 4), so it is spent in the run, which is what it's for.
84. The next-3 list counts only materials in hand, not what a Market trade could make: at these rates a trade is a last resort, and a "with the Market" mark would point at it first (§10.6 had planned the mark).
85. Feats are judged only once the Tavern keeps the list (its level 1); before that, nobody's counting. Mastery XP builds from the first run, though perks are trained from the Tavern's level 2.
86. Tides open one at a time by clearing the map at the highest Tide opened (Tide 1 from a first clear), and the Chapel's level caps which can be chosen. A Tide's health multiplier reaches the bosses too.
87. A challenge run keeps the harbor's upgrades, worn gear, and trained perks: its restrictions are the pools it empties, its Tide, and its curses. Each is won once.
88. The Cartographer's hints are the map's secrets on the minimap (level 5) until there is a map select to put them on; its level 1 shows the tasks on the minimap from 0:00 (without it a task shows once you've been near).
89. The harbor's pace is set against a practiced player, one who clears the map (Kevin cleared his first run): the MVP harbor's 6 to 10 hours are read on the cleared runs (8.4 h in 0.7.2, the whole harbor 12.0 h), and the bots that die are a newer player's pace (17.0 h; §10.3, 0.7.2). The early levels of the Library, the Tavern, and the Docks, and the Lamp Post's second, cost two to three times what they did, and every Lamp Post upgrade twice. `run.materialMul` is 1.2, which brought the cleared runs back inside the target after the Forms were toned down. A cleared first run usually buys the Lamp Post's first two levels (in one of five measured, a third) and the Library's first (in two of five, a second), not four buildings.
90. Any chest evolves a ready weapon, an elite's included (brief §5.3 says a boss chest, "as in VS"; Vampire Survivors' elite chests evolve too, and Kevin couldn't tell why nothing evolved). A weapon that's ready says so, and again every minute while it waits ("Flare Gun is ready to evolve: open a chest"); its HUD icon carries a gold corner mark and the pause menu says "Ready to evolve: open a chest" until it evolves. A hidden evolution shows none of this.
91. One Form is worth 1.1 to 2.1 times its weapon's own kills, and a whole line of three 1.4 to 4.3 (`tools/bots/forms.mjs`, the table in §5.16); before 0.7.2 one Form could triple them. Finer numbers wait for Phase 9 (Kevin: "the last phase is balance").

**0.7.3, the gamer pass** (rebuilt Nov 2026 from the notes of the lost version; these numbers replace the lost version's calls 92 to 115, which are gone with it)

*The run (B1)*
92. The starters are the Flare Gun, Harpoon Chain, Lantern Orb, Tide Bell, and Signal Mirror, and the Ballast is a starter passive. The Tallow Candle sits behind Orbwright's Notes, the Lodestone behind Tide Charts, and Sea Legs behind Ship's Ballast, which moved to tier 1 (10 Drowned Pages, 6 Timber, 1 run) so all three passives wait on tier-1 research. A fresh profile's pools hold 13 of 35 pieces of run content after 0.7.3 (37%), and 13 of 57 (23%) once the Marsh's 22 locked pieces are in: under the brief's 30% for the first time (calls 60, 82).
93. Signal Codes is now the Harbor Clock and opens the Tide Clock relic, so the Tavern's level 2 opens only mastery training. The Storm Glass research is now Weatherglass, because the Marsh's weapon takes the name. The ids stay, so saves carry over.
94. The Docks' elites come at 2:30 (an elite Drowned Sailor, the easy one), 7:30 (the Bosun), 12:00 (the Lantern Eater), and 18:30 (the Wreck Gunner). The 5:00 and 17:00 elites are gone and the 2:30 gull wall moved to 3:15, so no gap between chests is longer than 5 minutes.
95. The ready-to-evolve notice names the next chest: one already on the ground, then a living elite or boss, then the next on the timeline, then overtime's. It's said once per weapon and again only when an elite, a boss, or a muster appears, never for the second elite of a Tide 2 pair. This replaces 0.7.2's every-minute repeat (call 90).
96. Levels past 40 cost 1.06 times more each, compounding. The bots reached about level 50 at 10:00 and 81 at 20:00, where they had reached as high as 203.
97. A level-up with nothing left to level pays without stopping the run: 30 HP of food below half health, else 3 Sea Glass while the Ferryman is in the run, else 10 Coin. Every 8th such level also gives an Ember, kept for the pause menu, while a weapon can still take a Form. Repeats collapse into one line on the quiet line ("Levels 54 to 56: +30 Coin"), with no chime and a small glint in place of the level-up burst.
98. A boss's health is set from your level when it arrives. The listed health is for level 48 (the Harbor Maw), 80 (the Tidecaller), 50 (the Old Grey), and 82 (the Salt Widow), plus or minus 5% a level, held between 0.6 and 3 times.
99. `minFight` is a hard floor: a boss can lose health only in step with the time since it arrived, so the Maw's fight lasts at least 30 s and the Tidecaller's 45 s. A hit the cap eats shows no number; instead a grey ring (at most one every 120 ms) and a pale ward round the boss for 0.26 s. Once the minimum has passed nothing is held back.
100. Lantern Night's burn grows by 12% for each minute of the run before the last lamp is lit, the rate enemy health grows.
101. Overtime on the Docks repeats an elite every 90 s (the Bosun, the Lantern Eater, and the Gunner in turn), a ring of Bilge Rats every 120 s, and a Bosun's Muster of 30 every 300 s, announced 5 s ahead ("A muster in 5s"). Enemy health grows 1.08 times a minute, and the materials multiplier stops at 3.
102. Each Tide from 2 adds a rule and keeps the ones below it: 2 elites in pairs, only the first carrying a chest; 3 healing x0.75; 4 walls and rings bring 25% more enemies; 5 bosses' waits between attacks x0.85; 6 musters bring 50% more crew; 7 elites 20% faster; 8 Reliquaries half as often; 9 elites in threes; 10 Lantern Night half as long. The Chapel and the gate show each rule.
103. The Ferryman's Ember costs 70 Sea Glass plus 30 for each Form the weapon already holds. It's bought for one weapon (the cheapest by default), and only that weapon can take its Form. The trade screen shows the cheapest weapon's price; picking another weapon there isn't on screen yet.
104. Weapon numbers: the Lamplighter's light deals 7 and gains 4% a level; the Oil Flask's pool deals 20 every 0.3 s; the Corpse Candles deal 13 (level steps 5, 7, and 5) and fly at 126.

*The meta and the screens (B2)*
105. A run pays for the time it lasted: 4 Coin and 2 Oil for each whole minute, up to 20. It has its own line on the results and in the totals.
106. The Market sells at most 3 rare materials between two runs, with the count on the panel. Its trade row has an Amount (1, 5, 10, or 25 a press), and left and right choose what you pay with, starting on the common material you hold most of.
107. The bestiary counts from the first run. Each enemy has its own ladder (they were estimates, since the pacing yields don't record kills by enemy, and 0.8.1 cut them to 0.3 of that: call 159). Rewards are still claimed at the Cartographer's level 2. The third claim opens that enemy's trophy at the Apothecary, a one-run boost (one per ordinary enemy, drawn with the enemy's own sprite), and only the fourth gives +3% Damage, capped at 3%.
108. Mastery has 10 ranks and 5 perks per character, at ranks 1, 3, 5, 7, and 9, three of which change the character's mechanic. Every perk that repeated a Lamp Post upgrade or another perk was replaced, and the old ids stay at the same ranks, so a perk trained before 0.7.3 becomes its replacement. Mechanic perks reach the run as `RunConfig.characterParams`; the Punter's dash bonus is a parameter of his hook so Push Off can be one.
109. Next up lists the closest first, no consumables, and at most one upgrade rank. It keeps one slot for the nearest building level out of the fog and one for the nearest thing that opens content, with a line saying what it opens. After a run that opens something, the guide says "New at the Smithy" and points at that door until you go in.
110. A system you can't use yet isn't reported: no Sea Glass in runs before the Docks are restored, no mastery line before the Tavern's level 2, and the Tide line says which building or fog stands in the way.
111. The Bell Pendant's own affix is a bell toll every so many kills (40 in 0.7.3, 11 since 0.8.1), not tied to the Echo tag, because that would need sim code. Pendants already made keep their old +15% Area.
112. A death in overtime after a clear counts as a clear ("Map cleared"). Unplugging the controller in use opens the pause menu, and plugging it back doesn't resume: that takes a press of Resume.
113. None of the repeated keeper phrases from the lost version were in the restored code, so nothing was rewritten; 9 new keeper lines were added, each different, and a waiting line for every character, the Punter and the Bog Warden included.

*The art (B3)*
114. Each building has its own roofline (the Chapel's bell-cote, the Smithy's forge stack, the Docks' flagstaff, the Market's fish weathervane, the Apothecary's chimney pots, the Cartographer's lookout, the Library's cupola, the Tavern's stone chimney) in one tile of room above its plot, which comes out of the fog with the building. Props come by level, the gate's by milestone.
115. The town's life (gulls on the sea wall, moored boats, a fisherman and his dog, a sailor, a cat, a woman shopping) is decoration only: never solid, and nobody talks to it. The east gulls perch at [37,27] and the cat sits at [39,12], because their first spots were still in the fog at their milestones.
116. Level 5 is an open door: the leaf edge-on against its jamb, warm light low across the floor and spilling onto the street, and the Smithy's forge hotter than at levels 3 and 4. The door pools, the open door's light, and the forge are set from the door lantern's numbers rather than new tuning entries.
117. The waving townswoman at the gate, from the tenth run, is the pack's woman recolored with a waving arm drawn in code.
118. The run HUD is in sentence case, on a 5x9 font with descenders ("Lv 1", "3 kills", "Overtime: minute 2"). The boss's name moved up 1 px and the minimap down 2 px to fit it.
119. Notices that ask nothing of you (a level gift, a first sighting, the Ferryman's new stock, a task done) go on a fog-grey quiet line low on the screen (y 296); the middle line stays bright for what does ask. While a menu covers the run, both lines hide and their clocks pause.
120. Each enemy type has its own accent ramp: no two on a map share one, every pair is at least 0.02 apart in OKLab, and all are cool and dimmer than every character. The art test checks it on every map.

**0.8.0, the Salt Marsh (Phases 8b and 8c)**

*The Marsh's sim (B4, then the fixes after judges M1 and M2)*
121. The water starts low, so no water event fires on the first tick. In a short run the high-water windows and the Eye follow the halved clock (the Eye lasts 22.5 s and ends before the Salt Widow), but the 10 s creep and the 15 s warning stay full length.
122. Glass pillars count as open ground in rule 8's search, never rise on your held way home, and one that your way home comes to cross (the water rose, or you moved) falls at once (the lead's ruling after judge M1 found a pillar that could close the way home for about 4 s). On a map without water a pillar changes the ground at once; on the Marsh at the next rebuild, a quarter second at most, except one falling off your way home, which updates that tick.
123. Whatever the water catches moves to dry ground: a chest, a Reliquary, the vault, an open Salt Well, and thralls. An eel left on dry ground sinks into the mud, with no kill and no loot.
124. The Creek Eel's lunge carries up to 24 px past the water's edge (the lead's ruling after judge M1: an eel that stopped at the edge never touched a player walking the bank). Its warning line is exactly as long as the lunge, the lunge ends where it reaches you, and it slides back; you never push an eel, and a frozen one on the bank still slides back. It spawns only in water on the spawn ring, and if there's none that spawn is skipped. Its text reads "lunges along a line as you pass, a short way onto the bank."
125. An eel warning or lunging and a toad in its hop count as mid-attack, so no freeze, daze, or salt holds them, and salt spares every enemy mid-attack (chargers and gunners too) and anything in the air (§5.12, call 47).
126. Leeches only latch, never bite: at most 3, and a fourth waits beside you and takes the first place a latch frees (the lead's ruling after judge M1). None latches while the Bog Torc has you sunk; while sunk you aren't shoved either, and the Salt Well can't open.
127. A puddle freezes an enemy only when it isn't already frozen, so overlapping puddles don't keep refreezing it, and the 2 s thaw still blocks a new freeze.
128. Thralls bite when within one pixel of touching, because exact contact never quite closes. The pool holds 8; the Bog Warden's own limit is 6.
129. The Quencher counts each burning enemy once, however often Sea or Frost hits it. The Watchman's crowd is enemies within 56 px, checked every 0.1 s, and the result keeps the longest stretch.
130. A boss's weak window doubles damage before the `minFight` cap, so it never shortens a fight below its floor (call 99; the Old Grey 32 s, the Salt Widow 50 s).
131. The Mortar Form also refuses homing weapons, so the Familiar rune and the Mortar can't cancel each other.
132. The Dawn and High Tide feats say `map: 'drowned_docks'`, so a Marsh run can't earn them, and the four challenges name their map.
133. The Tide tag is Sea everywhere (Kevin's decision 8). The gear affixes `tide_might` and `tide_volley` keep their ids so saves still load; only their names say Sea.
134. Giving a relic to the Well undoes what it set up in the world (the Sea Urchin, the Brine Vat, the Try-Pot, the Bog Torc) and bans it for the rest of the run; giving up the Brine Vat ends every salt and stacked slow on the field. Keeping your relics lets the Well ask again only after you walk away and come back. Without the `well` layer no well opens, and the overtime Salt Watch still comes, opening nothing.
135. The Try-Pot: after a skip, the next level-up offers only what you own, and the one you pick gains 2 levels.
136. The Marsh has no gull walls: gulls come as flocks of at most 8 in a tight clump from one side, at 6:30, 11:30, and 16:30 (the design's rule 2).
137. Every storm ring goes through the crowd pick among enemies on salt, and every ring counts toward the Salt Glass. With fewer crowds on salt than rings, the burst is that much smaller rather than scattering the extras.
138. Salt crumbles whatever the crust, so a Salted Dead under the Brine Vat dies as the relic's text says.
139. A Salt Pan shard's kill never shatters again; only the spouts' own frozen kills shatter.

*The Marsh in the harbor (B5a, then the fixes after judge M3)*
140. The Salt Marsh opens with the Docks at level 1, the Harbor Maw killed on any run, and the Tavern at level 1, so skill and the Tavern open it, not a pile of Coin. "Unlock everything" restores buildings and research only, so the Marsh still needs the Maw killed.
141. A map card names only the materials that drop on that map alone (Coin, Oil, and Map Scraps drop everywhere), the research that counts there (projects that count everywhere included), what it "gets you closer to", and, once the Chapel is restored, the Tides open there, held to what the Chapel allows ("The Chapel allows up to Tide 2. Restore it to Lv 2 for more").
142. The gate opens on the map chosen last, and a challenge run leaves that choice alone; it offers only challenges whose map is open. A remembered map that's closed again is kept as the preference, and the gate sets out on the Docks (a fixer's call: reconcile could reset it instead).
143. Research that counts on one map says so: "Then 2 runs on the Salt Marsh" in Next up for one not started, "2 more runs on the Salt Marsh" on the Library row and the results. While its map is closed a project can't start, and a cost in a material only a closed map drops says where it comes from and what opens it.
144. A Well relic goes into a run only while the run's pools still hold it, so a challenge that empties the relics has no Well slot. The Ferryman's Well slot sits beside his own relics, offers one Well relic per stock in turn at its tier's price, and skips any already on offer or carried.
145. A first sighting goes on the quiet line as two lines, the name and then what it is, and only for what no earlier run has shown on any map. The v3 to v4 save migration fills the Docks' field guide from the bestiary, so a player already on the Docks isn't told about enemies they know. The Field guide is in the pause menu on every map.
146. The high-water warning and the Well opening go on the middle line, because they ask something of you; high water, low water, the Eye, and overtime's rising flood go on the quiet line.
147. The Well screen's focus starts on "Keep them", and Back keeps too, since ignoring the well costs nothing.
148. Whoever a run brings arrives by boat when you press Continue on the results; choosing Start run there puts them straight on the square. The Punter arrives after the first crossing (5:00 on the Marsh).
149. The Harpooner arrives at the Lamp Post's level 2, sooner than the Tavern's level 1 (the lead's call; the notes asked for her sooner).
150. The Marsh's materials enter costs from a building's level 2 or 3, never its level 1: the Apothecary's Oil becomes Marsh Herbs from level 2, the Docks take a pinch of Herbs at levels 3 to 5, the Smithy Salt Glass from level 3. Gear tiers 4 and 5 add 2 and 4 Salt Glass, a re-roll costs 1 Iron and 1 Salt Glass a tier, a rune stone 1 Salt Glass, and the Gull Bone and Tar Salve take Herbs beside a Docks material.
151. Salt Glass isn't traded at the Market. The build contract said it trades as a rare; the fixer left the Market's lists alone and made the comment say the Marsh's materials aren't traded (HANDOFF's questions).

*The Marsh's art (B5b)*
152. The salt flats are the palest ground but stay at night-3 to night-4, so every walkable tile is under 1.5:1 and every enemy at least 2:1 on salt. That keeps them dim at night (HANDOFF's questions).
153. Midges are teal specks, because blue-black ones were lost under the fog. A thrall gets a cool gem-4 tint (at least 1.86:1 on salt for every enemy; a fog-2 tint dropped them to 1.0), tar a dull sheen never darker than rot, and salt the enemy's white silhouette tinted fog-3, held still.
154. Things in the air rise at most 12 px for a toad and 36 px for a boss, with their shadow on the landing spot.
155. A strike's light is local, a third the size with Reduce flashing, and no strike tints the whole screen.
156. The Salt Widow's eyes are her light; the Old Grey has none. On the Marsh the secret is drawn as a bog body. The Quench Bucket has no handle (drawn closed it read as a hat), so it shows water splashing.

**0.8.1, the combat's pace** (Kevin, Nov 7: fewer, slower, stronger enemies; §5.6, §5.7)
157. The seven pace dials sit together under Pace on the tuning panel: enemy count 0.35x, at most 450 alive, speed 0.8x, health 2.5x, touch damage 1.3x, XP 3.5x, drops 3.5x. `pace.maxAlive` replaces `director.maxEnemies`. The alive cap stops only the band's spawning; a map's rings, walls, flocks, and musters still come in full. Each band's floor (8 enemies in the first minute, rising to 120 by 18:00) holds whatever the dials say, except while a boss is alive.
158. What the dials reach: boss crews and a Bloater's rats count as ordinary enemies; elites get the touch dial and bosses don't; bosses' warned attacks keep their speeds while their walking slows; shots and hazard pools keep their speed and damage; heals and the magnet ride the drops dial with the materials. Health, XP, and drops reach new spawns only; count and speed apply at once.
159. Everything that counts kills was set again in the data for these dials, about 0.28 of the kills, rather than read from them live, because the descriptions show the numbers: the bestiary ladders to 0.3 (the Creek Eel 5, 50, 500, 5,000), Ten Thousand to Three Thousand (same id, so a feat already earned stays earned), the Quencher 50 to 15, the Watchman's crowd 40 to 20, the Fog Bell 10 plus 2 a minute, the Reliquary's chance 0.0021 with pity at 500, Bloodied Hook 15, Watchman's Rattle 15 (re-arming under 8), the Drowned Bell every 8th kill, the Wreck Lantern every 2nd, the Bell Pendant's toll every 11th, Eclipse at 16 kills, and Great Toll 0.17 s a kill. Turning the count dial back up makes all of these that much easier. The Wreck Lantern's oil stays out of the last quarter of the zone pool.
160. The map hazards weren't scaled: the storm's 80, the Eye's 120, and Lantern Night's burn are unchanged against 2.5 times the health, so the storm no longer one-shots a Salted Dead from mid-run.
161. The Lantern Orb's knockback is 0.15 (was 0.5): at the new health it pushed tougher sailors out of the orbit between hits, and alone it killed 1 sailor between 6:00 and 9:00, against 40 before. At 0.15 the orb alone is back to 67 kills a minute (the lead's change). The other area and pierce weapons, which lose the most to fewer, tougher enemies (the Glass Caltrops most), wait for Phase 9.

*The pacing batch (B6, measured on 0.8.1; §10.3)*
162. The yields are measured at four harbor stages, each a declared share of the harbor (none, a quarter, a half, three quarters) that never reads a cost, so a cost refit leaves the yields fresh. Each simulated run uses the stage nearest its player's own progress.
163. The Marsh is measured with its storm and Well on, because a profile has them after its first Marsh run; its first one or two runs are slightly overstated.
164. A simulated player runs where a project under way points, else on the map whose own materials the next thing they want is shortest of, else on the newest map open.
165. A run's haul is scaled by the player's greed over the greed of the stage it was measured at.
166. The economy grew about four times in common materials and about twice in rares, so the costs were scaled to match rather than cutting what runs bring home: building levels 2 to 5 (and the Docks' and the Tavern's level 1, so run 1 can't open the Marsh), every upgrade rank, and research tiers 2 to 4 at four times their commons and twice their rares; the Marsh's Herbs 14 times and Salt Glass 10 times their first placing. Lowering `run.materialMul` or the time pay would do the same job with smaller numbers. The Apothecary's level 2 is 400 Coin, not 440, to keep a pinned test true.
167. Gear, consumables, and mastery perks kept their prices in common materials (only their Marsh costs moved), so they are about four times cheaper against a run's income than before. Raising them would break pinned tests, so it was left for Kevin (HANDOFF's questions).
168. Re-rolls keep 1 Iron and 1 Salt Glass a tier, which a test pins, so they are cheap against the new Smithy.
169. Every measured run cleared the map, the ordinary bots' included, so nothing in the tool measures a newer player any more; that would need bots that play worse. The report says so instead of calling the ordinary bots a newer player.

**0.9.0, builds (batch W1; the lead's rulings 1, 3, and 4; numbered from 170 for the lead to renumber against the other batches)**
170. The capstone is each line's payoff Form (the brief's third kind, "what a hit leaves behind"), not the third Form taken: it needs the partner passive at Lv 5, not the weapon at its max level, and no chest.
171. Each old evolution's change rides on its line's capstone as a boost (the Flare Barrage's explosions and extra flare on Hanging Flare, the Lens's burn on Wards, the Kraken Line's pull and chain on Tether, the Storm Bell's freeze on Riptide, the Salt Pan's shatter on Salt Crust, Storm Glass's spikes on Glass Spikes, the Conger Pot's daze on Conger, the Sea Anchor's armor damage on Deep Anchor), with its sprite and icon as the capstone's look. The six weapons that never evolved get +25% to +40% Damage (and Area or a Projectile) on theirs, and their look stays their own until W6 draws one.
172. New capstone partners where a weapon had none: the Signal Mirror Bittern's Call, the Oil Flask Wick Oil, the Lightning Rod the Lodestone, the Corpse Candles the Tallow Candle, the Ice Picks Waders, the Glass Caltrops Sea Legs. A passive may open two capstones (Wick Oil: the Flare Gun's and the Oil Flask's).
173. Four tier-1 projects that only held a passive now open a capstone instead (Orbwright's Notes: Wards; Tide Charts: Riptide; Ship's Ballast: Tether; Oil Stores: Fuel Line), so the passive and the Oil Flask could join a fresh pool (the brief's "research reveals evolution recipes"). The fresh profile's share of run content rises to 20 of 57 (35%), over the brief's 30%: the lead's "pool about twice the slots" won.
174. Form progress takes 5, 7, then 9 picks of a maxed weapon's Form card for its 1st, 2nd, and 3rd Form, and then the Ember is bound to that weapon. A weapon whose only Form left is a capstone it can't take yet offers no Form cards (they'd fill toward nothing). (W1b: replaced by Form progress from level 1, call 255.)
175. Slots: weapons +1 at the Lamp Post's Lv 3 and 5, passives +1 at the Smithy's Lv 2 and 4, relics +1 at the Docks' Lv 3, 4, and 5. The Smithy is in the fog until the Lamp Post's Lv 4, so passive slots come later than weapon slots.
176. A full relic pick can pass (keep what you carry) as well as swap. The relic given up isn't banned: it can come again from a Reliquary, and the Ferryman stocks it at its tier's price. Without the Docks there's no Ferryman, so it only goes back to the pool.
177. Four Relics carry a price: the Seventh Bell -10% XP, Volatile Gems -25% Pickup Range, the Drowned Bell -10% Move Speed, the Brine Vat -15 Max HP.
178. A skip pays what a dry level pays (Food when hurt, else Pearls with the Ferryman, else Coin) and counts toward the dry levels' Ember; banishes start at 2 (were 1).
179. Balance (V2's traps): the Harpoon Chain 28 Damage (was 16), 1.3 s Cooldown (1.6), Pierce 4 (3), bigger damage steps; the Lantern Orb 14 Damage (9), its orbs always out (3.2 s of a 3.2 s cooldown), bigger damage steps; the Glass Caltrops 17 Damage (10), Pierce 3 (2), a little wider, bigger damage steps. Alone they reach 2.2 to 3.1 times their 0.8.2 damage (§5.19). The Anchor Chain (4,927) and the Eel Pot (5,334) are now the lowest alone; neither is a starter.
180. The sim never names a weapon's look (the pacing fingerprint), so a capstone's sprite and icon are read by the view through `weaponLook` in `data/ascensions.js`.

**0.9.0, combat and enemies (batch W3; V1's and V2's lists, the lead's rulings 5 and 7; numbered from 200 for the lead to renumber)**
200. Early pressure comes from three things together: each early band's own `pace` (2.2, 1.85, 1.5, and 1.2 times the density dial from 0:00 to 7:00), higher floors (15, 26, 36, 40), and the fast kind on each map keeping its speed (the Bilge Rat, now from 0:00 on the Docks, and the Midge Cloud). Floors and density alone didn't touch the kite bots: they outwalk everything at 80 px/s.
201. The rat's pounce and the midge's dart lead their target: the warned line points where you'll be if you keep going, and the leap bites what it comes within 8 px (rat) or 7 px (midge) of. Aimed at where you stood, every pounce missed a moving player by a few pixels. Both are warned at least 0.8 s, and both pounce once each.
202. The midges, the Marsh's commonest enemy, get two traits (the dart and the sting) where the rats get one: without the dart, nothing on the Marsh reached a player before 5:00.
203. A warning's tier is set once, when it starts: FULL needs the line to cross, or the ring to hold, where you stand at that moment, and a place among 4. The view draws the rest as a short, dim tell. A tier never changes an attack.
204. What hurt you is found without touching the weapons code: a shot or pool remembers its maker by its pool slot, and a hit from the weapons code is matched to the shot or zone standing exactly there.
205. Hit-stop is a request from the sim (`EV.HIT_STOP`); the app is asked to read only that. A hit a shield takes whole still asks (it's a hit).
206. A gate holds a boss a hair above its notch (1 HP above, so it stays in its phase) and at 1 HP before its death; a boss held at a gate throws its signature next; a gate opens anyway after 20 s (a safety for a signature that can't start, such as a lure off screen).
207. The signatures: the Harbor Maw's lure, then its charge; the Tidecaller's tide line, its call, then its charge; the Old Grey's leap in both phases; the Salt Widow's lightning march, then her salt shards. A signature counts when it lands (its execute), not when it warns.
208. Boss health refit for fights without the time cap: the Old Grey 6,500 at level 46 (was 4,000 at 50) and the Salt Widow 30,000 at level 72 (was 12,000 at 82); their old levels sat above where Marsh players are, so both were held at the 0.6 clamp. The Harbor Maw and the Tidecaller are unchanged.
209. The five affixes are V1's (Warded, Herald, Molten, Tethered, Brine). Herald's six and Tethered's two links are the elite's own kind, spawned with it, so a link always exists; a dead link is let go on the elite's next turn.
210. A ranged elite stands its ground instead of backing away: the 18:30 Wreck Gunner elite lived 39 to 215 s backing off.
211. Champions have twice an elite's health and no affixes. The 5:00 champions pay an elite's chest (no certain Ember), so the 5:00 fight doesn't speed the build more than an elite would; the 15:00 ones keep the muster's boss chest and Ember. The Marsh's 5:00 elite midge is replaced by the Reed Cutter's muster.
212. The salt wall is a row of brine pools you can't stand in for 6 s, not solid ground: solid walls would fight rule 8 (the flood never closes round you) and the pillars' limits.
213. Threat that follows power reads damage dealt over the last 30 s against a reference curve from the 0.8.2 kite bots at Tide 0 (one curve for both maps), raised to 0.4 and held between 1 and 1.4, from 4:00. It reaches touches, a pounce's bite, and new elites' health; not a toad's landing, shots, pools, or bosses. Before 4:00 it would have taken a harbor's upgrades back at the start: a three-quarter harbor's build at Tide 10 died in 2 to 6 minutes with it.
214. The Tides add horde danger from 0:00 by raising every band's floor by half their density step, and a Tide's health reaches bosses only as its square root (`director.bossTideExp`; call 86 gave them all of it: with the time cap gone, the Old Grey at Tide 7 had 28,800 HP and held the bot at 10:00) (a Tide's density used to reach only the bands whose targets were above their floors, which the early ones never were), and from Tide 2 every elite has two affixes.
215. The XP steps at 20 and 40 are spread as triangles over the 8 levels each side, at the same total XP: no level costs less than the one before, and the cumulative XP matches the old curve by about level 30 and 45.
216. The test field (tests/helpers/simworld.js) gives plain elites and no threat (`NEUTRAL_COMBAT`) so mechanics tests read their own numbers; `pastGates(b)` opens a boss's gates for a test that needs one hit to kill it.

**0.9.0, the run's arc and the maps (batch W4; V2's and V3's lists, the lead's ruling 6; numbered from 230 for the lead to renumber)**
230. The acts begin at events, not at fixed minutes: act 2 when the first boss arrives, act 3 when you stay for overtime. Each act ends on a boss (or the Long Night).
231. Lit lamps stay lit for the rest of the run, and each act brings new lamps at new sites; Lantern Night banks again once every lamp out is lit, so each act can bank one.
232. An act's lamps are all out from its start (the walk is the task); its bells wake one at a time (the Docks and the Marsh at 0, 2:40, and 5:30 into act 1), each placed away from you when it wakes, so every act has a fight to choose every few minutes. A bell that has woken waits for you; nothing counts down.
233. Sites: 16 lamp and 11 bell on the Docks, 14 and 10 on the Marsh, over the whole map. The Docks bring 5, 5, and 4 lamps an act, the Marsh 4 each; 3 bells an act on both. The Marsh's lamp sites are on the peat islands where there are any (the peat never floods), and act 3's there keep to ground overtime's flood never takes.
234. The draw is spread rather than random: away from you, apart from the act's other lamps, one place at a time, relaxed in that order when sites run short.
235. The acts draw from their own stream, a fork of the root, so the spawns, loot, and draft streams don't move.
236. Twists: none in act 1 (it teaches the plain tasks), one in act 2, two in act 3, from the held lamp and the elite crew. The held lamp is a defend-the-point (2.5 times as long, a crowd of 8 every 5 s while you're in its ring), never a timer: leaving pauses it, as any lamp. It pays a chest and twice the Pearls.
237. The deck draws 2, 3, and 2 events an act, in equal slices of the act's span; an entry comes once a run while the act has others, then repeats. Four entries a map start open; the other four come from map orders.
238. A cache's guard rises 460 px out, just past the view's edge (320 px half-width), so it rises unseen and you see it walk in.
239. The Docks' districts are built from the ground kinds there are (crates as container stacks and stalls, sheds as halls and the ropewalk, water and planks as the harbor mouth and the breakwater, the wreck's tiles as a ship's frame), so no new tiles and no atlas rebuild. Slips, sheds, and crates were cut from 34, 44, and 240 to 24, 32, and 200 to make room.
240. Each map's own task re-arms each act rather than once a run: a tool you choose when to use, three times a run with overtime. The crane drops 3 loads; the cairn works 20 s. Their damage grows as enemy health does, so they clear ordinary enemies all run and only dent elites.
241. A crane's load and a cairn's strike never hurt you, so their warnings are dim tells, not full ones (the lead's note: MAJOR or FULL only for what can hit you). The peat fire can hurt you (10), so its rings are MAJOR.
242. The crane and the cairn are drawn in code from rect lists (render/landmarks.js), after the written specs of a portal crane and a field-stone cairn (the image search showed photos the builder couldn't inspect), against the atlas's lamppost as the style anchor, in three logged passes.
243. Small finds are secrets with a gift: they go through the secrets' plumbing (the codex, completion, the Cartographer's marks and hints) and count toward the result's secretsFound. Pearls become Coin without the Ferryman.
244. The character secret is a find at the breakwater's point, out from act 2 ("after the first boss, something has washed up"): the farthest walkable point of the Docks, reached by a 90-tile walk out along the arm.
245. The Salt Well asks once a run on its own; after that, standing still in its ring for 0.6s asks again (a deliberate act, so nothing stops the run uninvited; it was a dash into the ring until ruling 16).
246. Overtime pairs two elites a minute in turn (was one every 90 s), brings the first boss back at 5:00, and ends in the Long Night at 10:00: both bosses, the second 15 s behind (so two first phases don't open together), each at 80% health.
247. Dawn ends the run itself, 8 s after the last boss of the Long Night falls: the horde crumbles without kills or loot, the spoils on the ground come to you, and the dawn chest opens on the spot (a boss chest). Losing is dying in the Long Night (the map stays cleared, as any overtime death).
248. The final boss is marked on its timeline event, and the director's last-boss index follows inserted events, because the deck's events are inserted into the timeline.
249. The follow bot walks a breadth-first path field to its errand and goes to chests on the ground; the plain kite bot is unchanged, so the balance sweeps and pacing yields play as before.
250. Map orders open deck entries (W5's 'event' content kind): reach act 2 with lamps lit, ring 6 bells, find 3 finds, win the Long Night, per map.
251. The bell's first-meeting tip says "Stand in the small ring to start it. Kill its crew to ring it" (V3: standing there starts it; killing the crew rings it).

**0.9.0, rolled weapon upgrades (batch W1b; Kevin's ruling 10; numbered from 252 for the lead to renumber)**
252. Upgrade types are data (`data/weaponups.js`); their numbers, the odds, the rarity multipliers, the caps, and how often each type comes up are tuning (`tuning.weaponUps`), so the tuning panel steps them. `data/rarity.js` stays view-only: the sim keeps the five ids itself and a test holds the two the same.
253. Type weights within a rarity (damage 4, cooldown 2, area 1.5, duration 1, speed 0.6, knockback 0.4; projectile 0.5, two projectiles 0.35; the rest 1), because the first measurement, with every type equal, rolled damage one level in six and fell 13% to 26% behind 0.8.2's kills.
254. A type whose lowest rarity is the roll weighs 2 times the rest (`gateWeight`), so a Rare roll is mostly a projectile, pierce, or crit, and a Legendary roll mostly the special or a bundle. A bundle is two Common types at the Rare value each.
255. Form progress from level 1 at 9, 8, and 8 steps (the ruling said about 5): at 5 a run takes all three Forms on most weapons by 15:00; 9, 8, 8 keeps a run's Form count near where W1's 5, 7, and 9 after max level put it (6.2 a fresh run, 12.0 at half the harbor, against 7.1 and 13.0) and the first Forms near their minute (5.0 by 15:00, against 4.4).
256. A paused weapon's progress waits where it is (all Forms taken, or only a capstone whose partner passive it lacks) rather than filling toward nothing.
257. A draft with fewer candidates than cards shows a weapon twice with two different upgrades (a short draft read as broken; the pool never runs dry).
258. The bots weigh a weapon upgrade over the build's best tag weight, so a Common on their best weapon scores what a level-up did before against a new weapon or passive. Scored raw (rarity x tag weight alone), they stopped filling slots and poured every level into one weapon (the Flare Gun reached level 23 by 15:00 at 27 times its base output).
259. The Fog Bell's weapon levels roll when picked, not when it opens, so its options never spend random numbers just to be shown.
260. The test helper's `give()` builds a level from a fixed ladder of upgrades (projectile, damage, cooldown, damage, projectile, pierce, damage, area), about what the old tables gave, with no roll, so tests that need a well-levelled weapon stay exact.
261. The weapon content test compares a rune's damage and kills with the bare weapon's: with rolled numbers, an equal hit count matched a rune's damage by chance once.

**0.9.0, characters are trades (batch W2; the lead's ruling 2; numbered from 262 for the lead to renumber, after W1b's 252 to 261)**
262. A signature weapon takes one of the run's weapon slots (slot 0), and the start weapon is slot 1. The Lamplighter's lamp, before this outside the slots and never levelled, now takes a slot and levels like any weapon.
263. A signature weapon has weight 100, so the draft offers its upgrade cards; nothing offers it as a new weapon (`unlock: 'innate'`), and the dev panel won't give one.
264. Signature bases are set so 0:00 to 3:00 matches the Lamplighter's damage: Grapnel 20, Punt Pole 16, Bog Hands 15, Grave Pennies 8, Lamplight 7, King Tide 140 (once a minute, its first at 0:20).
265. Most signature Forms are built from the character's own mechanic: their `form` keys pass through sim/forms.js and the character's hook does the work. Bog Hands' capstone is Bog Kin (its key stays `bog_host`).
266. Lantern Night stays everyone's. The Lamplighter banks two at once, hers falls at 10 enemies within 160 px (15 for anyone else), and her light reaches 30% farther during it.
267. The Harpooner's hook yanks an elite to 90 px from her, dazed 2 s: inside her weapons' reach, out of its own. At 40 px it landed on her, and the mortal bot died at 6:00 on the Docks.
268. The Punter's water is ground only to him (his own collision grid), on both maps, at 75% speed. Shots, fliers, and swimmers still reach him there; only walkers stop at the edge.
269. A punt cache is drawn from the water 160 to 480 px from the Punter and waits, trying each second, until there is some. A draw over the whole map almost never landed near him.
270. The Bog Warden's risen dead (bog bodies and wreck crews) stand outside her thrall limit, and the ally pool holds 16.
271. A secret another character owns (the Drowned Causeway, the Warden's) is simply not there for anyone else: no hint, no mark.
272. The Drowned Man's short slot is taken on the first tick, because the world sets the slot count after the character's setup. Each boss gives one back, up to 8.
273. "Can't heal" means every heal: food, regen, chests' and drafts' heals, the Ferryman's food, the Apothecary's goods, and lifesteal. A revival (Bog Torc, a revive) isn't a heal and still works, and Max HP she gains still adds to her health, as it does for everyone.
274. The Tide Priest's order asks 15:00 with 100 damage or less (was 40). The mortal bot took 23 to 348 by 15:00, median about 130; it met 40 in 2 runs of 40 and 100 in 9.
275. The Tide Priest wears the pack's hooded robe, sea-bleached pale with a sea-blue stole, and holds a crook with a bell high in her left hand. A sea-blue robe or cowl fails the warm rule, which the lead kept; the sea cowl is offered to Kevin as an option.
276. Punt Pole and King Tide lose their Duration upgrades (and the pole its Hit delay), and the pole takes the Signal Mirror's caps: each is made to hit an enemy once a shot (W1b's guard).
277. A trade's state is numbers in the character hook's state, with site and bell flags as bits in two numbers, so the world hash walks it and replays match. Its event is `EV.TRADE` (80).
278. The Punter's capstone order is a Salt Marsh clear at Tide 2, not any Marsh clear as the design had it, so a capstone order stays a goal past a character's first clears.
279. The Tide Priest's sustain is the Tide Ward (the lead's design): each King Tide that breaks leaves her a shield of 2 for each enemy it hit, at most 40, in place of the last, until the next breaks. The HUD's thin shield bar over her health shows it, with no text. Measured against 170 Max HP and no ward, the ward did better (HP alone didn't move her clear rate).
280. The Lamplighter's lamp grows half as much with her level as before 0.9.0 (+1.25% area and +2% damage a level), because it now also levels as a weapon.
281. Costs were softened where the bot measured a trap: the Harpooner moves 5% slower (was 10%), and the Bog Warden's weapons deal 5% less damage (was 15%). Grave Pennies hit for 7 (was 8).
282. Signature bases were not raised for the Harpooner and the Warden: at 0:00 to 3:00 they already match the Lamplighter's damage on the current code. Their shortfall comes later, and the costs are where it was.

**0.9.0, rolled weapon upgrades, continued (batch W1b)**
279. A weapon's data can hold its own growth back: `upCaps` (a cap of tuning.weaponUps.caps it lowers for itself) and `upMul` (its upgrades' share values x this). The measured runs found weapons whose every hit lands on a crowd outgrow the rest by 4 to 16 times once levels never stop: the Signal Mirror (beams stop at 3, length at 2 times, upgrades half), the Lantern Orb (orbs stop at 4; its Eclipse Form is as strong as their count; upgrades half), and the Corpse Candles (flames stop at 5; upgrades half). Each count cap is the old top level's.
280. A weapon that hits each enemy once a shot (its shot lasts less than its hit delay) has no upgrades that stretch the shot past the delay: the Mirror and the Tide Bell lose Duration and Hit delay, and content.test holds every weapon to it.
281. The Harpoon Chain has no Knockback or Speed upgrades (knockback throws enemies out of its line). It is still the weakest weapon played alone by the kite bot, which faces away from the crowd it flees; in full runs it holds up.
282. The balance target was 0.8.2+W1's kills a minute within 10% at 5:00, 10:00, and 15:00. A straight line can't meet the old curve at both ends (the old tables front-loaded seven big levels, then stopped): 5:00 lands a little under, 15:00 over, and the means are medians' business when a few runs run away; numbers in work/veteran/reports/W1b.md.
283. Survival joined the balance target (W2's clear rate: the mortal follow bot, a fresh profile's pools, the Docks at Tide 0, 24 seeds a character). W1b's first calibration matched kills a minute by the median and still dropped the Lamplighter's clears from 23 to 10 of 24, because the early minutes were weak. The fix is the shape, not one big Common: early levels boosted, core types first, the first projectile at Uncommon (§5.21). The numbers are in work/veteran/reports/W1b.md.

---

## 21. Where every part of the brief lands
